# Architecture — Notebook v0.4

## 1. Decision: file format

**Choice: option A — custom `.notebook` extension with versioned JSON content**, opened by a `FileView` registered with `registerExtensions(['notebook'])`.

Reasons, in order of weight for M1 (data reliability first):

1. **No one else writes to the file.** A `.md` (options B and C) is touched by Obsidian and by other plugins: Linter, Templater, automatic link updates on rename, frontmatter changes, a Markdown editor opened by mistake. Any of these can corrupt a large data block. With our own extension the file is written only by Notebook.
2. **No workspace patching.** B requires intercepting `WorkspaceLeaf.setViewState` (as Excalidraw does with monkey-around): fragile code across Obsidian versions and with the "deferred" tabs of 1.7.2+. `registerExtensions` is a public API.
3. **No indexing cost.** A `.md` with thousands of points is indexed by search and the metadata cache on every save; on Android that is a real cost.
4. **The advantages of B/C (links, tags, backlinks, Graph) belong to M4.** The format is designed to get there without rewriting the data (see §5).

Accepted and documented costs:
- Native search, tags and backlinks do not see notebooks (until M4).
- **Obsidian Sync** syncs unknown extensions only with the "Sync all other types" option enabled (stated in [install.md](install.md)). Syncthing and USB copy have no limits.

## 2. JSON schema v24

The machine-readable JSON Schema of this format is [schema/notebook-v24.schema.json](schema/notebook-v24.schema.json); [format.md](format.md) is the guide for third-party tools. A test (`tests/format-schema.test.ts`) validates what the serializer writes against it, so a format change must update the schema.

v24 adds the optional `opacity` of a stroke, an image and an area (*Opacity, format v24* below). v23 adds the element type `area` (*Area highlighter, format v23* below). v22 adds the optional stroke fields `fill`, `fillTint` and `dash` of a shape (*Filled shapes and dashed lines, format v22* below). v21 adds the optional notebook-level `views` list (*Saved views, format v21* below). v20 adds the tools `fountain`, `brush` and `marker` and the optional stroke field `nib` (*New pens, format v20* below). v19 adds the optional page fields `startAt` and `endAt` (*Page start and end time, format v19* below). v18 adds the optional element and stroke field `group` and the optional element fields `locked` and `lockAt` (*Groups and locked elements, format v18* below). v17 adds the element type `checkbox` (*Handwritten checklists, format v17* below). v16 adds the optional page field `comments` (*Margin comments, format v16* below). v15 adds the element type `sticky` (*Sticky notes, format v15* below). v14 adds the optional page field `locked` (*Locked pages, format v14* below). v13 adds the optional page field `states` (*Drawings with states, format v13* below). v12 adds the optional document table `slides` and the optional stroke field `slide` (*Notes ↔ slide link, format v12* below). v11 adds two optional metadata fields, `meta.description` and `meta.coverArt` (§9, *Creation dialog and folder metadata*); the example below shows the fields up to v10.

```json
{
  "format": "obsidian-notebook",
  "version": 10,
  "id": "6f1c…",
  "createdAt": "2026-10-29T14:30:00.000Z",
  "modifiedAt": "2026-10-29T14:42:10.000Z",
  "pages": [
    {
      "id": "a91e…",
      "width": 595.28,
      "height": 841.89,
      "paper": { "kind": "grid", "spacing": 14.17 },
      "strokes": [
        { "id": "c03d…", "tool": "pen", "color": "#1e293b", "width": 2, "render": 1,
          "points": [[72.5, 90.12, 0.512], [73.1, 90.4, 0.534]] },
        { "id": "d71a…", "tool": "highlighter", "color": "#facc15", "width": 14, "render": 2,
          "points": [[72.5, 120.0, 0.5], [180.2, 120.3, 0.5]] }
      ]
    }
  ]
}
```

| Field | Rule |
|---|---|
| `format` | always `"obsidian-notebook"`: distinguishes the file from any random JSON |
| `version` | integer. A version higher than the supported one → the file opens **read-only** with a warning and is never rewritten |
| units | **typographic points (pt, 1/72 inch)**, origin at the top left of the page. A4 = 595.28 × 841.89 pt. Chosen because it is PDF's native unit (used as is by the M3a export) and independent of the screen |
| `pages` | at least one. They are stacked vertically by the layout (§6), not by coordinates. Stroke ids are unique across the whole document |
| `paper` | `kind` is one of `blank`, `ruled`, `grid`, `dotted`, `millimetre` (all but `grid` are new in v3); `spacing` in pt. See "Paper and page sizes" below |
| `points` | `[x, y, pressure]` in the page's own coordinates, x/y rounded to 0.01 pt, pressure in [0, 1] rounded to 0.001. **The saved pressure is the one actually used**: if the device does not provide it, the pressure simulated from velocity is written. Rendering is therefore deterministic. With stabilization on, the stored points are the stabilized ones (§6) |
| `tool` | `"pen"`, `"highlighter"` (new in v2) or `"shape"` (v4). The eraser produces no strokes |
| `color` | `#rrggbb` |
| `width` | nominal diameter in pt, > 0 |
| `render` | required (v5): the geometry that draws the stroke. `1` = perfect-freehand outline with the per-tool parameters below (frozen; every stroke written before v5 is 1); `2` = incremental wet-ink geometry (§6). Any other value is rejected. Written right after `width`, before `points` |
| `id` | unique strings (UUID when available) |

Added in v4 (all optional on disk, written only when present, except `meta` and `assets` which are always written):

| Where | Field | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| document | `meta` | `{ tags, favorite, cover, bookmarks }`: library data and bookmarked page ids |
| document | `assets` | `{ [id]: { id, kind: "pdf" \| "image", path, name, bytes } }`: files in `<notebooks folder>/_assets/`, referenced by id |
| page | `background` | `{ kind: "pdf", asset, page }` or `{ kind: "image", asset }` |
| page | `elements` | text boxes (`type: "text"`: `x`, `y`, `width`, `text`, `fontSize`, `color`) and images (`type: "image"`: `asset`, `x`, `y`, `width`, `height`, `rotation`) |
| page | `infinite` | the page grows downwards while writing near the bottom |
| stroke | `tool: "shape"` | constant-width ink for lines, arrows, rectangles, ellipses and recognised shapes |

Added in v6 (optional, written only when present):

| Where | Field | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| page | `margins` | `{ left, right, bottom }` in pt, each ≥ 0: blank space added around the background (#104). The background is drawn in the box `x = left, y = 0, width = width − left − right, height = height − bottom` (`backgroundRect` in `model/margins.ts`); without `margins` it fills the page as before. Validation: `left + right < width` and `bottom < height`, so the box keeps a positive size. Key order: after `background`, before `infinite` |

Added in v7 (optional, written only when present):

| Where | Field | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `meta` | `bookmarkInfo` | `{ [pageId]: { title?, color?, category? } }`: details of bookmarks (#15, #85). Which pages are bookmarked stays in `meta.bookmarks`; details exist only for ids in it (others are dropped on read and write) and an entry with no field is dropped. `title` and `category` are strings (whitespace collapsed, trimmed, at most 200 / 60 characters, empty = absent); `color` is `#rrggbb`. Built-in categories are `"important"` and `"review"` (shown translated), any other string is a custom category. Written at the end of the meta line, entries in bookmark order, fields in the order title, color, category. A key `__proto__` is never stored. On read an invalid field, entry or map is dropped and counted (`ParseResult.dropped`, also an invalid `position` or its `y`) instead of rejecting the notebook; the session keeps a `dropped-details` backup before the next save rewrites it, and the check before writing stays strict. Helpers in `model/bookmarks.ts` |
| page | `tags` | array of strings (#119): leading `#` removed, whitespace collapsed, trimmed, at most 64 characters, case-insensitive duplicates removed (first spelling kept), at most 50; an empty list is absent. Key order: right after `paper`. Helpers and the `SetPageTags` command in `model/page-tags.ts` |
| document | `position` | `{ page, y?, at }` (#125): where the notebook was last read — page id, page y (pt, rounded to 0.01, only when > 0) at the top of the view, ISO time. Dropped (not rejected) when its page is not in the document. Key order: after `meta`, before `assets`. Not part of the undo history. Written only together with a content save: `DocumentSession` calls the `beforeSerialize` hook just before the first serialization of a changed revision (by the journal or by the save), never for a flush without changes, so reading or scrolling alone never writes the file and two devices that only read never fight; when both edit, the last writer wins. `model/position.ts`, `view/position-sync.ts` |
| page | `paper.kind` | new kinds `cornell`, `music`, `isometric`, `hexagonal`, `calligraphy`, `planner-day`, `planner-week` (#73), see "Paper and page sizes" |

Added in v8 (optional, written only when present):

| Where | Field | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| stroke | `hidden` | `true` (#100 invisible ink): the stroke is not drawn, erased, lassoed or exported unless the reader shows the solutions (view-only toggles per notebook or page, never saved; the invisible-ink pen mode shows them to the writer). Never in thumbnails and embeds. Any other value is invalid. Key order: after `render`, before `points` |
| page `elements` | `type: "marker"` | `{ id, type, kind, x, y, width?, height?, at?, done?, slide?, due?, text? }`: a badge (14 pt, `MARKER_BADGE`) whose top-left corner is (`x`, `y`), optionally about the region `width` × `height` from that corner (both or neither, each > 0). `kind`: `gap` (to complete, #201), `question` (#202), `important` (!), `critical` (!!), `star` (★) (#203), `homework` (#209). `at`: ISO time it was placed; `done`: only `true` (resolved question, filled gap, homework done); `slide`: integer ≥ 1, the reference PDF page in view (or the page's own PDF page) when a gap was placed; `due`: a real calendar date `YYYY-MM-DD`; `text`: one line (whitespace collapsed, trimmed, at most 300 characters, empty = absent). Line: required fields, then the optional ones in the order above |
| page `elements` | `type: "box"` | `{ id, type, kind, x, y, width, height, title? }` (#204): a labelled frame, `kind` one of `definition`, `theorem`, `example`, `proof`, `remark`; `title` follows the marker `text` rule. Drawn under the ink with a label tab above its top-left corner (12 pt) |

Markers and boxes share the id space of strokes and elements. Drawing order: boxes after images and before text boxes (under the ink); marker badges after the ink, so they stay visible and tappable. **Reading is lenient for the v8 details** (like the v7 ones): a marker or box whose kind, position or frame is invalid, or an invalid optional field of one (region, time, state, slide, due date, label, title), or a `hidden` value other than `true`, is left out and counted in `ParseResult.dropped` (also in staged loading, for the pages read later) instead of rejecting the notebook; the session keeps the `dropped-details` backup before the next save. A duplicate id still makes the file invalid. **Writing is strict**: `serializeChecked` re-parses without dropping (also the skeleton of the incremental path), so an invalid marker, box or flag is never written. Helpers in `model/markers.ts`; looks in `ink/marker-render.ts` (canvas, SVG and PDF share the badge geometry and colours).

**Where a notebook starts (v7).** A page asked for explicitly (a `#p3` link, a library search hit, *Continue last notebook*) always wins. The workspace layout stores the page in view with `pageAt`, the time it was in view; on a layout restore the stored `position` wins only when its `at` is later (another device read and saved the notebook afterwards). Opening without a page starts at the stored position. Only the first load of a file applies this; a reload after a sync change keeps the view where it is.

Added in v9 (wave 0.9: math; optional details written only when present):

| Where | Field | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| page `elements` | `type: "equation"` | `{ id, type, x, y, width, height, latex, fontSize, color, inline?, numbered?, outline? }` (#110): LaTeX `latex` (no `$` delimiters, at most 4,000 characters, not blank) typeset with Obsidian's MathJax at `fontSize` pt (≤ 400) in `color` into the box `width` × `height` whose top-left corner is (`x`, `y`). `inline: true` = text style (absent: display style). `numbered: true` = "(n)" drawn at its right, n counting the numbered equations in reading order over the whole notebook (`equationNumbers` in `model/math-elements.ts`: page by page, top to bottom, left to right within a row). `outline` = cached vector outline of the typeset glyphs: absolute `M x y`, `L x y`, `Z` commands only (numbers with at most two decimals, no exponent), in pt relative to (`x`, `y`), filled with the even-odd rule, at most 600,000 characters, checked by a linear scanner (`parseOutline` in `math/outline.ts`). Line order: required fields, then `inline`, `numbered`, `outline` |
| page `elements` | `type: "graph"` | `{ id, type, x, y, width, height, view, functions, params?, axes?, grid?, xStep?, yStep?, xLabel?, yLabel?, degrees?, table? }` (#113): a frame `width` × `height` showing `view` `{ xMin, xMax, yMin, yMax }` of the plane (finite, ordered, |values| ≤ 10¹², spans ≥ 10⁻⁹). `functions`: at most 12 `{ id, kind, color, …, min?, max?, width?, hidden?, roots?, extrema?, intersections?, tangent?, area?, label? }`, `id` unique in the graph (≤ 64 characters); `kind` `cartesian` (`expr`, y of x; `min`/`max` restrict x), `parametric` (`x`, `y` of t over `min`…`max`, default 0…2π), `polar` (`expr`, r of θ over `min`…`max`), `implicit` (`expr`, `lhs = rhs` or F(x, y) = 0), `piecewise` (`pieces`: 1 to 10 `{ expr, when? }`, the first piece whose condition holds, a piece without `when` otherwise); expressions are strings of at most 500 characters for the parser of `math/expr.ts` (an expression that does not parse is **not** a format error: the graph shows the message); `width` line width ≤ 20 pt; `tangent` an x; `area` `{ from, to }` (different). `params`: at most 12 `{ name, value, min, max, step? }` (`name` a letter then letters, digits or `_`, at most 16, not a function, constant, keyword or x, y, t, r; unique; `min < max`; `step > 0`). `axes: false`, `grid: false` hide them; `xStep`/`yStep` > 0 grid steps (absent: chosen from the view); labels one trimmed line of at most 60 characters; `degrees: true` trigonometric functions in degrees; `table` `{ fn, from, step ≠ 0, rows 1…50 }` for a function of the graph (a table whose function was removed is left out on write). One line, keys in this order; functions write only the fields of their kind |

Equations and graphs share the id space of strokes and elements. **Reading is lenient** like v8: an equation or graph whose required data is invalid (frame, source, size, colour, viewport, the function list itself) is left out and counted in `ParseResult.dropped`; each invalid optional part (the outline, a flag, a function, a parameter, a setting, the table) is left out and counted on its own; staged loading counts the same. A duplicate id still makes the file invalid. **Writing is strict** (`serializeChecked`, also on the incremental path). `freezeElement` freezes a graph's nested data too, so copies can share it. Pure helpers: `model/math-elements.ts` (limits, creation, numbering, `stableStringify`), `math/` (parser, sampler, marching squares, numerics, outline, scene, layout, convert to ink); drawing in `ink/math-render.ts`, the PDF and SVG writers; ink following a graph's view in `ink/graph-ink.ts`.

Added in v10 (anchored excerpts, #105; optional details written only when present):

| Where | Field | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| page `elements` | `type: "excerpt"` | `{ id, type, x, y, width, height, source, asset? }`: the picture of a region of a page shown in the box `width` × `height` (each > 0) whose top-left corner is (`x`, `y`). `source` `{ notebook, doc, page, x, y, width, height }`: the vault path of the source notebook when the excerpt was made (non-empty, at most 1,024 characters), its document id (`NotebookDocument.id`, at most 200 characters), the stable id of the source page (at most 200 characters) and the region of that page in its page coordinates (pt; `width`, `height` > 0; not checked against any page, which may be in another notebook or have been resized). `asset`: an image asset of the document holding a PNG snapshot of the region taken when the excerpt was made. One line, keys in this order, `source` keys in this order |

Excerpts share the id space of strokes and elements. **Reading is lenient** like v8 and v9: an excerpt whose frame or source is invalid is left out and counted in `ParseResult.dropped` (also in staged loading); an invalid or unresolved `asset` drops only the snapshot (counted), the anchor stays. A duplicate id still makes the file invalid. **Writing is strict** (`serializeChecked`, also on the incremental path), and the history guard (`checkContent`) refuses an edit that adds an excerpt the writer would reject (for example a snapshot reference to an asset the document does not have). `freezeElement` freezes the nested `source`. Pure helpers: `model/excerpts.ts` (region from a selection, placement, creation, badge geometry, source state, `elementAssetId`); look in `ink/excerpt-render.ts`.

**Live picture or snapshot (decision).** An excerpt is drawn from the best source available, in this order:
1. **Live**, when its source is a page of the notebook open in the view (`source.doc` equals the document id, so a renamed or moved notebook still counts as itself, and the page is found by its id wherever it is now): `NotebookMedia.excerpt` draws the region with the page raster (`ink/page-raster.ts` with `region`) from only the strokes and elements whose bounds meet the region (`regionContent` in `ink/excerpt-live.ts`; stroke bounds and `Path2D` shapes are cached per immutable stroke), at no more than the excerpt's size on screen (scale stepped down by √2, at most 2,048 px a side), using the page's PDF bitmap already rendered when there is one (asking for another scale of a page the view draws would cancel its render). A picture is drawn again only when its key changes: a fingerprint of the region's content (identity tokens of the culled items, so an edit elsewhere on the page changes nothing), the page size, paper, background and margins, the automatic numbers of the region's numbered equations (`liveContentKey`), the scale and an image revision bumped when a picture loads, fails or is evicted. After an edit, a check a moment later (never during the stroke) asks for a repaint only if the content key of a live excerpt changed, at most every 400 ms (a picture asked for again within 400 ms of being drawn gets its own repaint timer, so a loaded picture or a new zoom is never left stale); that repaint is the renderer's usual full redraw of the static layer (the static layer has no partial redraw, and the review asked not to change it here). Pictures are kept in an LRU cache with a 48 MB budget (`LiveCache`), released at once when evicted, and those of excerpts no longer in the document are dropped.
2. **Snapshot** otherwise: an excerpt of another notebook (reading and rendering another notebook and its PDF while drawing would be slow and depend on files that may be missing or still syncing), a source whose background is still loading, a source page that is gone, exports (PDF, SVG, Markdown page images), page thumbnails and the page raster itself. Exports and thumbnails are therefore deterministic and never read another file.
3. **Placeholder** (a light card with a dashed frame) when there is no snapshot and no live picture.

The snapshot is taken when the excerpt is made, from the open document (background, paper, pictures, graphs, boxes, equations, text and visible ink; **never hidden ink**, which is the solutions), at 3 px per pt (at most 2,048 px a side), and stored as a content-addressed PNG image asset through `storeAsset` (`_assets/<hash>.png`), so the same region excerpted twice is stored once. The page raster draws the excerpts of the page it draws from their snapshots, never live, so an excerpt of a page that holds an excerpt of it cannot recurse. A snapshot is not refreshed afterwards: an excerpt of another notebook keeps showing the region as it was (its badge still jumps to the current page).

Nothing is written when the region is chosen: the snapshot's PNG bytes and the asset record it will have (`assetRecord`: same content id and path as `storeAsset`) stay in memory; the file is written when the excerpt is placed (after the picker, so closing it leaves no file) or by the paste of a copied excerpt, like any copied picture. Only the pictures that meet the region are decoded for it, and every bitmap opened for it is closed. An armed region (no selection) is forgotten on a tool change, Escape, read-only mode and when a marker or box is armed (and arming an excerpt forgets an armed marker or box): one one-shot placement at a time. Large v9 files (≥ 2 MB) are staged like v10 ones: the v9 → v10 migration only refuses excerpts, so each page is checked for them as it is read (`v10OnlyElement`) and the result says `migratedFrom: 9`.

**The jump back.** A tap on the badge (`EXCERPT_BADGE`, 14 pt, top-right corner, inside the box; any tool but the lasso and the eraser). A contact on the badge is never taken from the ink: it is a tap only when it lifts within 500 ms and neither any sample nor what it drew (every point and the lift position) reaches further than 8 CSS px, and then the dot it drew is dropped (`takeBadgeTap`); a stroke that starts on the badge and moves is kept as ink or *Go to source* on the selection bar: when `source.doc` is this document, the view scrolls to the page (by id, in the shown document, so it works while a large file is still loading) and outlines the region on the live layer for 1.8 s; a page that is not in the document any more gives the notice "Source not found" and the badge is drawn grey. Another notebook must have the source's document id (read from the header of the file, `documentIdOf`): the stored path is tried first, then `resolveNotebookFile` (a notebook of the same name), then every notebook of the vault, those in the stored path's folder first (a copy made with *Make a copy* keeps the id; at most 400 files read, one search at a time, results and misses remembered for the session, a miss retried after a minute), so a renamed or moved notebook is found and a different notebook with the same name is not opened. When it is found under another path, the stored path of this notebook's excerpts of it is updated (one undo step, only when editable). It is then opened with `openNotebookAtPage(…, pageId, region)`: the view state carries `pageId` and `region`, the view resolves the page by id once loaded (a missing page says so, as for any page link) and outlines the region. Nothing is ever written by a jump, so it also works when the notebook cannot be edited (read-only mode, wave 0.10 #51, or a file opened read-only): the page takes no strokes then, and `ExcerptTools.attachReadOnlyTaps` watches the stage passively for a short contact (pen, finger or mouse) on a badge. With the wave 0.10 features: the PNG export and printing draw an excerpt from its snapshot (`view/page-images.ts`, through `elementAssetId`); the library check counts a snapshot as used because its id is written in the notebook text; the cross-device clipboard (stamp-format entries) and the lasso across pages carry the excerpt and its snapshot record.

Drawing order of a page: page colour, background (PDF page or image), paper pattern (none by default on PDF and scanned pages), images and excerpts (v10, in their element order), graphs (v9), structured boxes (v8), equations (v9), text, ink (hidden ink only while the solutions are shown), marker badges (v8) and excerpt badges (v10).

Unknown fields are ignored on read. Every schema change increments `version` and adds a tested migration (`src/model/migrations.ts`).

**Version history**

- **v0 → v1**: v0 is an unversioned test format (`{ strokes: [{ points: [{x, y, p}], color, size }] }`) that served to establish the mechanism.
- **v1 → v2** (M2): the only change is the new `highlighter` tool. The migration is the identity plus `version: 2`: every valid v1 document is a valid v2 document. A v1 file opened by 0.2 is migrated in memory and rewritten as v2 only on the first edit.
- **v2 → v3** (M3a): v3 adds the paper kinds `blank`, `ruled`, `dotted` and `millimetre` (the v2 reader accepted only `grid`); page sizes were already free numbers (`width`, `height`). The migration is the identity plus `version: 3`: every valid v2 document is a valid v3 document. A v2 file opened by 0.3 is migrated in memory and rewritten as v3 only on the first edit.
- **v3 → v4** (M3): adds `meta` (`tags`, `favorite`, `cover`, `bookmarks`) and `assets` (keyed by lowercase-hex id) to the document; `background`, `elements` (text and image) and `infinite` to pages, written only when present; and the stroke tool `shape`. Migration: default meta (`DEFAULT_META`), empty assets; a pre-v4 file that already contains `shape` is rejected. Validation: asset references (page backgrounds, image elements) must resolve to an asset of the right kind; ids are unique across strokes and elements; `meta.tags` are de-duplicated and `meta.bookmarks` filtered to existing pages on read and on write. Order of keys: `meta`, `assets` before `pages`; per page `background`, `infinite`, `elements`, `strokes`. `serializeChecked` validates meta, assets, backgrounds and elements on every save, also on the incremental path.
- **v4 → v5**: adds the required per-stroke `render` field (`1` = `RENDER_FREEHAND`, `2` = `RENDER_INK`). Migration: every stroke of every page gets `render: 1`, because every stroke written before v5 was drawn with perfect-freehand and must keep rendering exactly as before; v0→v1 still produces strokes without `render` and the v4→v5 step adds it. `readStroke` rejects a missing `render` or a value other than 1 or 2 (`pages[i].strokes[j].render`). `createStroke` defaults to `RENDER_FREEHAND`, so only a caller that asks for the new engine produces render 2. Key order: `id`, `tool`, `color`, `width`, `render`, `points`.
- **v5 → v6** (PDF wave p1a): adds the optional page field `margins`. Migration: the identity plus `version: 6`; a `margins` key already present in a v5 file is dropped, because a v5 reader ignored it (it was never ours and never had an effect). No other field changes, so every valid v5 document is a valid v6 document without margins. A 0.4.x plugin opens a v6 file read-only (future version) with a backup and never rewrites it, so margins cannot be lost by an older version saving over them.
- **v6 → v7** (wave 0.6): adds the optional `meta.bookmarkInfo`, page `tags`, document `position` and seven paper kinds. Migration: the identity plus `version: 7`, except that `meta.bookmarkInfo`, `position` and page `tags` keys already present in a v6 file are dropped (a v6 reader ignored them: they were never ours), and a pre-v7 file whose page uses one of the new paper kinds is rejected (a v6 reader rejected it, so no version of this plugin wrote it; like `shape` before v4). Every valid v6 document is a valid v7 document without the new fields. Paths that copy pages or metadata keep the new fields: `cloneMeta` (so `SetMeta` and library patches), `duplicatePage` (tags; the bookmark stays with the original page id), page restore (*replace* unites the tags of the current page and of the version, *insert* copies the version's), conflicted-copy merge (page tags of common pages united; bookmark details merged field by field: the main notebook's value of each field wins, missing fields come from the copy, differing fields are counted in `bookmarkInfoDiffer`; the main position is kept; `mergeCovers` also checks page tags, bookmarks and that every detail field of the copy is in the result with the same value), *Move to notebook…* (the moved pages' bookmarks and details go to the target under the new page ids, in the same write), repair (line scan reads the `tags` and `position` lines; tags keep their string entries, bookmark details keep their valid fields per entry, a position whose page was not recovered is dropped) and the companion note (details are not properties). A 0.5.x plugin opens a v7 file read-only with a backup.
- **v7 → v8** (wave 0.8): adds the element types `marker` and `box` and the optional stroke field `hidden`. Migration: the identity plus `version: 8`, except that a `hidden` key already present on a stroke of a v7 file is dropped (a v7 reader ignored unknown stroke fields, so it never hid anything), and a pre-v8 file with a marker or box element is rejected (a v7 reader rejected unknown element types, so no version of this plugin wrote it). Every valid v7 document is a valid v8 document. Paths that copy ink and elements keep the new data: `copyStroke` (`model/pages.ts`, used by `duplicatePage`, page restore, merge and the clipboard) keeps `hidden`; elements are copied whole. So duplicate page, page restore (*replace* and *insert*), the page trash (entries are one-page documents; a restore with an id clash goes through `duplicatePage`), *Move to notebook…*, templates, stamps and paste keep markers, boxes and hidden ink. Conflicted-copy merge: hidden ink is part of a stroke's content (the same ink shown on one side and hidden on the other is kept twice, nothing is revealed); a marker's resolved state is not content — the same marker resolved only in the copy is resolved in the result (`SetMarkerDone`, by id, `markersResolved` in the summary), never added twice; an edited label is content (both kept); `mergeCovers` also checks that every resolved marker of the copy is resolved in the result. Repair: `fixStroke` keeps `hidden: true`, `fixElement` keeps a marker or box with a valid kind and position and its valid optional fields. Companion note: boxes become callouts `> [!<kind>] <title>` holding `![[<notebook>#p<n>&id=<page id>&r=x,y,w,h]]` (the Z1 region embed), homework markers become tasks `- [ ] <text or "Homework"> 📅 <due> ^nb-<marker id>` (`- [x]` when done; the block id ties the line to its marker), in the generated region only, in reading order after the text boxes of the page; English callout types and fallback text keep the note language-neutral. Ticks are read back (`userTicks`, `homeworkFromNote` in `model/companion-content.ts`): the region state on the start marker lists the anchors the plugin wrote ticked (`done`); a task ticked in the note that is not in that list was ticked by the person or the Tasks plugin. It is written ticked again (with the Tasks plugin's `✅ <date>`), and the companion service marks the marker done in the notebook as one undo step (`SetMarkerDone` in a `Batch`) through the notebook's open, editable view on the device that updates the note; the notebook file is never written by the companion service, so when the notebook is not open there the tick only waits in the note. A tick the plugin wrote follows the notebook (reopened there, the task is unticked); unticking a tick of one's own before the notebook took it is respected; a tick is never undone otherwise. Practice copies (*Redo this exercise*) leave gap, question and homework markers out, so an attempt does not repeat the original's tasks; a plain *Duplicate* copies every marker with new ids on purpose (the copy is a page of its own: its homework is a task of its own, with its own block id). Exports draw boxes and markers; hidden ink only for the pages whose solutions are shown in the view (`PdfOptions.hiddenInk`, `SvgOptions.hiddenInk`). A plugin that reads up to v7 opens a v8 file read-only with a backup.
- **v8 → v9** (wave 0.9: math): adds the element types `equation` and `graph`. Migration: the identity plus `version: 9`, except that a pre-v9 file with an equation or a graph is rejected (a v8 reader rejected unknown element types, so no version of this plugin wrote it). Every valid v8 document is a valid v9 document. Copy paths keep the new data: elements are copied whole (`duplicatePage`, page restore, page trash, *Move to notebook…*, templates, stamps), the clipboard and the lasso transform them (`transformElement`: an equation scales uniformly with its font size and outline, a graph's frame follows the transform as a box while its view stays), a left margin translates them (`translateElement`). Conflicted-copy merge: a graph's content key sorts keys at every level (`stableStringify`; the replacer-array form of `JSON.stringify` used for the other elements would drop nested keys, so two graphs differing only in an expression or a parameter would look the same); an equation's cached outline is not content (the same LaTeX typeset on two devices is the same equation). Repair: `fixElement` reads a damaged equation or graph with the lenient reader, keeping its valid parts. Exports draw both as vectors. The companion note does not list them yet. A plugin that reads up to v8 opens a v9 file read-only with a backup.
- **v9 → v10** (0.11.0: anchored excerpts, #105): adds the element type `excerpt`. Migration: the identity plus `version: 10`, except that a pre-v10 file with an excerpt is rejected (a v9 reader rejected unknown element types, so no version of this plugin wrote it). Every valid v9 document is a valid v10 document. Copy paths keep excerpts and bring their snapshot asset: every place that gathers the assets of a page or a selection uses `elementAssetId` (an image's asset or an excerpt's snapshot): `duplicatePage`, page restore, the page trash, *Move to notebook…*, templates, stamps (a snapshot that cannot be stored again is left out, the anchor is kept), the clipboard (a snapshot stored again under another id is renamed in the pasted excerpt; a missing one is left out, the anchor is kept), the heavy-page load and *Page information*. The lasso transforms an excerpt like an equation: uniform scale around its centre, never rotated, the source unchanged. Conflicted-copy merge: the content key sorts the nested source (`stableStringify`); the snapshot is not content (the same region excerpted on two devices is the same excerpt). Repair: `fixElement` reads a damaged excerpt with the lenient reader (a bad snapshot reference is left out, the anchor is kept). Exports draw the snapshot as a picture (no badge). The companion note does not list excerpts. A plugin that reads up to v9 (0.10.x and older) opens a v10 file read-only with a backup.
- **Compatibility**: a plugin that supports an older version sees a newer `version` as a future version, so it opens the file read-only (and keeps a backup copy of it, as for any file it cannot edit) and never rewrites it. Once 0.2 has saved a file, 0.1.x cannot edit it; once 0.3 has saved a file, 0.2.x cannot edit it; once 0.4.0 has saved a file (v5), 0.3.x and earlier open it read-only; once a v7 version has saved a file, 0.5.x opens it read-only; once a v8 version has saved a file, versions that read up to v7 open it read-only; once a v9 version has saved a file, versions that read up to v8 open it read-only; once a v10 version has saved a file, versions that read up to v9 (0.10.x and older) open it read-only.

**Stroke rendering parameters** of `render: 1` (perfect-freehand) are code constants; the per-stroke `render` field selects the geometry, it does not carry parameters:
- pen (`PEN_V1`): `size = width`, `thinning 0.6`, `smoothing 0.5`, `streamline 0.35`, `simulatePressure false`, round caps. Unchanged since v1;
- highlighter (`HIGHLIGHTER_V2`): `thinning 0` (constant width, pressure ignored), `smoothing 0.5`, `streamline 0.35`, flat ends. Painted with alpha 0.35 and `multiply` compositing.

Changing these constants changes how existing files look: the `render: 1` parameters are frozen. A different look is a new `render` value (2 = wet ink, §6), never an edit of these constants.

### Paper and page sizes

Both are per page, so one notebook can mix them. Constants are in `model/types.ts` (sizes, default spacing) and `ink/paper.ts` (pattern geometry).

| Paper `kind` | Default `spacing` | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| `blank` | unused | nothing drawn |
| `ruled` | 8 mm | horizontal lines every `spacing`, the first at 3 × `spacing` from the top (`RULED_TOP_LINES`); a red vertical margin line at 25 mm from the left edge |
| `grid` | 5 mm (14.17 pt) | vertical and horizontal lines every `spacing` |
| `dotted` | 5 mm | a dot at every grid intersection |
| `millimetre` | 1 mm | lines every `spacing`; every 10th (10 mm) is a major line, drawn darker |
| `cornell` (v7) | 8 mm | ruled lines every `spacing` from 3 × `spacing`; a major title line at 2 × `spacing`, a major summary line at 80 % of the height and a margin-coloured cue-column line at 30 % of the width between them |
| `music` (v7) | 2 mm (gap between staff lines) | staves of five lines (outer two major, inner three minor) from 15 mm, one every 10 × `spacing`, 12 mm from the sides, 10 mm free at the bottom |
| `isometric` (v7) | 5 mm (triangle side) | vertical lines every `spacing` × √3/2 and lines at ±30° through the same lattice (all minor) |
| `hexagonal` (v7) | 4 mm (hexagon side) | flat-top honeycomb; every edge drawn once (each hexagon draws three edges); minor; at most 10,000 hexagons walked (see the segment budget below) |
| `calligraphy` (v7) | 6 mm (x-height) | groups of ascender (0.8), waist, baseline (major) and descender (0.8) lines, 0.6 x-height apart; slant guides at 55° every 2 x-heights inside each group (minor) |
| `planner-day` (v7) | 8 mm (row) | major title line at 3 rows; rows to the bottom, every second (hour) major across the schedule (62 % of the width) and minor in the notes column; a margin-coloured time column at 20 mm and a major line between schedule and notes |
| `planner-week` (v7) | 6 mm (ruling) | major title line at 3 rows and a bottom line; eight boxes (seven days and notes), 2 × 4 on a portrait page and 4 × 2 on a landscape one, with major borders and minor ruling inside |

The v7 kinds are built in `ink/paper-kinds.ts` (segments clipped to the page, only those crossing the visible rectangle, none on the page edge; at most `MAX_PAPER_SEGMENTS` = 30,000 segments per pattern: a denser one falls back to its major and margin lines, and when that leaves nothing or is still too much, to the same pattern with the spacing doubled until it fits, so a page is never exported blank; a lattice with more than 5,000 lines on an axis starts coarsened) and returned by the same `paperPattern`, so the canvas, thumbnails, PDF and SVG exports, the new-notebook dialog, the default-paper setting and the page menu offer them without further code. Lines and dots are never drawn on the page edge. `paperPattern(page, visible, { includeMinor })` is pure (no canvas): it returns lines (`minor` / `major` / `margin`) and dots in page coordinates, limited to the visible rectangle. The canvas renderer (`drawPaper`) and both exporters use it, with shared colours (`PAPER_COLORS`), so screen and export show the same pattern. On screen, minor lines and dots are left out when they would be closer than about 3 CSS px (`minorVisible`), which keeps millimetre paper cheap and readable when zoomed out; major and margin lines stay. Exports always draw the full pattern.

Page size presets (portrait, pt): A4 595.28 × 841.89, A5 419.53 × 595.28, Letter 612 × 792. Landscape swaps width and height; there is no stored orientation field, it follows from `width > height`. The format accepts any positive `width` and `height`; the UI offers only the presets, plus the size of imported PDF pages and of scanned or image pages (custom sizes are not implemented).

**Changing the size of a page does not move or scale its strokes.** They keep their page coordinates; strokes that end up outside a smaller page stay in the file (nothing is deleted) and are clipped when drawn and exported. Making the page large again shows them again.

A page added with **Add page** copies the size and paper of the page in view (0.2 always added an A4 grid page); `createPage()` without arguments still gives A4 grid.

## 3. Modules

```
src/
  main.ts                 plugin: views (notebook, library), extension, ribbon, commands, settings, integration, flush on unload. Since 0.12.1 every start-up step runs through `step()`: a step that throws is logged, listed in `startupFailures` and reported once in a notice, and the others still start; the settings tab is registered first. An unreadable data.json starts the plugin on the defaults and is never written over in that session
  settings.ts             settings types and defaults (paper, size, input, stabilization, predicted ink, version history)
  i18n.ts                 UI strings: English, Italian when Obsidian's language is Italian
  model/                  (does not import 'obsidian')
    types.ts              Document, Page, Stroke, elements, assets, meta, Tool, Paper, page margins, page tags, bookmark details, reading position, markers and boxes (v8), equations and graphs (v9), page size presets, CURRENT_VERSION (10)
    document.ts           document/page/stroke creation (size and paper as parameters), ids, quantization
    pages.ts              duplicatePage(): copy of a page with new page and stroke ids
    page-content.ts       SetPageContent command and pageContentCommand(): replace the strokes/elements of several pages (and append pages) in one undo step
    lenient-json.ts       tolerant JSON reader: keeps what was read in full up to the damage, reports cut-off containers (repair)
    repair.ts             repair of an unreadable notebook text: line scan, tolerant migration, per-item validation, summary (#140)
    merge.ts              merge of a conflicted copy as one Batch: union by id, conflicting items kept twice, no inferred deletions (#122)
    page-restore.ts       restore one page of an older version: ReplacePage command, insert or replace plan (#180)
    serialize.ts          deterministic serialize(), parse() with validation and migrations, serializeChecked() (save check), freezeStroke()
    migrations.ts         chain of vN → vN+1 migrations (v0→v1 … v5→v6)
    lecture-pack.ts       lecture pack page plans: slide geometry, notes layouts, notes page after every slide, stored options (pure)
    migrations.ts         chain of vN → vN+1 migrations (v0→v1 … v9→v10)
    math-elements.ts      equations and graphs (v9): limits, creation, labels, parameter names, numbering, stableStringify, deep freeze (pure)
    markers.ts            markers, structured boxes and invisible ink (v8): creation, edits, tap rules, bounds, lecture range, panel lists (pure)
    excerpts.ts           anchored excerpts (v10): region from a selection, placement, creation, badge geometry, source state, elementAssetId (pure)
    marker-commands.ts    SetMarkerDone: resolve a marker found by id (merge, panel)
    margins.ts            page margins (v6): background box, size and resize rules, translating strokes and elements
    page-tags.ts          page tags (v7): normalisation, input parsing, counts, union, SetPageTags command
    practice.ts           practice copies of exercises (#303): what to hide (`HideSpec`), the copy (`buildPracticePage`), `practice`/`attempt/<n>` tags, the sidecar records (parse, serialise, ops, queries, numbering), durations, remembered choices
    position.ts           reading position (v7): normalisation, position of the view, start-page choice
    bookmarks.ts          bookmark details (v7): set / patch / remove, outline entries, categories and colours
    history.ts            command-stack undo/redo (strokes, pages, paper/size, elements, meta, assets, infinite, Batch)
    companion-content.ts  companion note (O1): document → properties, tags and generated Markdown region; merge with the user's edits (pure)
    tags.ts               shared tags (O2): Obsidian-style normalisation, suggestions, the merge rule for the companion note's `tags` (pure)
    stress.ts             deterministic generated notebook (10,000 strokes / 10 pages by default) for benchmarks and the stress-test command
  math/                   (does not import 'obsidian'; wave 0.9: math, #110 #113)
    expr.ts               safe expression parser and compiler (no eval): grammar, implicit multiplication, degrees, conditions
    sample.ts             adaptive sampling with discontinuity handling, clipping, parametric, polar, implicit (marching squares)
    contour.ts            marching squares and polyline simplification (Ramer–Douglas–Peucker)
    numeric.ts            roots, extrema, intersections, derivative, adaptive Simpson integral
    graph-scene.ts        what a graph looks like (grid, axes, curves, areas, points, tangents, table, legend) for every renderer
    outline.ts            equation outline path (format, parse, scale), tracing a coverage raster, even-odd rasterising
    skeleton.ts           Convert to ink: thinning, distance transform, skeleton tracing
    layout.ts             equation size from nearby handwriting, palette snippets
  ink/                    (does not import 'obsidian')
    viewport.ts           screen ↔ page transform, fit-width, zoom around a point (scale 0.25–8), pan

    view-position.ts      page, zoom and scroll position that survive a different view size (O16, pure)
    paper.ts              paper pattern geometry (lines and dots in page coordinates) and paper colours (pure)

    clipboard.ts          copy/cut/paste/duplicate of a selection: deep-copied clip, new ids, paste placement, one-step paste command

    pen-controls.ts       toolbar slots for favourite pens, eraser size and pen-only; eraser circle on the stage (passive listeners, outside the writing pipeline)
    commands.ts           tool and action commands (checkCallback, no default hotkeys), registered from main.ts
    navigator.ts          the Pages panel: thumbnails, jump, drag to reorder, bookmarks, page-tag filter, outline mode
    outline.ts            outline list, bookmark editor (title, colour, category), page tags editor, page-menu items (v7)
    position-sync.ts      reading position glue: `pageAt` in the view state, start position of a fresh load (pure)
    media.ts              PDF pages, images and scans for the renderer and exports; asset storage; v10 live pictures of excerpts of this notebook's pages
    sync-safety.ts        sync safety UI: "open on another device" banner and read-only, "not downloaded yet" message and polling, sync check modal, notices and settings (wave 0.7)
    selection-actions.ts  floating bar over the lasso selection: restyle, copy, cut, duplicate, paste, white to transparent
    stamp-palette.ts      stamp palette over the page area: built-in and personal stamps, tap or drag to place, save / rename / delete (#142)
    object-ink.ts         view side of ink attached to objects: cached answer for the current selection, the offer to delete ink with a deleted object (#149)
    clipboard.ts          plugin clipboard (module singleton, survives notebook switches) and the system clipboard wrappers
    paste-logic.ts        pure paste decisions (plugin vs system clipboard), text normalisation, pasted image/text elements
    paste-modal.ts        paste box for WebViews that cannot read the clipboard (press and hold, Paste)
    pick.ts               choose a PDF or image from the vault or the device
    pdf-range-modal.ts    page range for "Insert PDF"

    pdf-reading.ts        PDF reading tools (wave 0.6): toolbar entries, hit overlay, snapping highlighter, hide annotations, dimming; pdf-search-panel.ts: the search bar and results list; reading-logic.ts / reading-settings.ts: pure helpers and the persisted options; reading-commands.ts: the commands
    text-editor.ts        in-place text box editor
    math-tools.ts         v9 equations and graphs in the view: insert, edit, typesetting queue, Convert to ink, pan/zoom in a graph, commit on leave
    math-typeset.ts       typesetting with Obsidian's MathJax into an outline (off-screen layout, painted raster, tracing)
    equation-editor.ts    the equation editor card (source, live preview, palette, style, size, colour)

    ruler-gesture.ts      two-finger ruler placement
    radial-menu.ts        radial menu at the pen tip (DOM, window-level pointer capture) and the pen's quick-tools list
    last-location.ts      debounced memory of the notebook, page, zoom and scroll position being read (instant start, O16)
    page-mode.ts          one page at a time: swipe gestures, drag, page-turn animation, wheel (DOM side of ink/page-mode.ts)
    input-settings.ts     plugin settings to input-policy settings

    benchmark.ts          in-app benchmark (off-screen render of a generated notebook, writing replay) and its modal

    view-helpers.ts       small pure helpers shared by the view

    pdf-sync.ts           synchronised PDF view (#106): command, "…" menu entries, reference picker, the controller that keeps the two sides together
    practice.ts           "Redo this exercise" (#303): the service (sidecar, finding pages and notebooks by document id, redo flow, timers), the per-view controller, the commands

```

**Interface (ui redesign).** `src/ui/` holds the action registry (`actions.ts`), the layout engine (`layout.ts`), use counts (`usage.ts`) and the radial dial's model (`radial-model.ts`), all pure and tested in Node, plus the sheet, contextual bar and dismiss components. The view builds a `UiContext` (`NotebookView.uiContext()`), `view/view-ui.ts` asks the engine for the quick slots and the contextual bar once per frame, and every action runs through `NotebookView.runAction()` → `view/view-actions.ts`. Commands are generated from the registry (`view/commands.ts`) with stable ids. Design and information architecture: `docs/design-ui.md`.

## 4. Save flow (rule 4: zero data loss)

Session state: `doc`, `revision` (incremented on every change), `savedRevision`, `diskContent` (last content read or written by us), `mode` = `ok` | `readonly` (future version) | `broken` (unreadable) | `deleted`. **All disk operations of a session (load, flush, external-change check) run one at a time through a single queue**, so a check never interleaves with a write.

**Open**
1. `vault.read(file)` → `parse()`.
2. Success → `mode = ok`, any migration happens in memory (the migrated file is rewritten only on the first change).
3. Failure (invalid JSON, invalid schema, empty file) → `mode = broken`; future version → `mode = readonly`. In both cases: **a backup of the raw content is created** in `<notebooks folder>/_backup/<name> <date-time> <reason> <hash>.bak` (no duplicates: a backup is skipped only if a file with the same hash in its name has exactly the same content), the view shows a message and **no write path is active**.

**Change** → `revision++` → scheduled save: 1.5 s debounce from the last change, at most 10 s of waiting during continuous writing.

**Flush** (debounce, view close `onUnloadFile`/`onClose`, plugin `onunload`, `visibilitychange` → hidden, `pagehide`, `workspace 'quit'` best effort):
1. If `mode ≠ ok` or there are no changes → nothing.
2. `serializeChecked(doc)` produces `next` and **re-validates** it (see "Save check" below). If it fails: no write, warning, changes kept in memory.
3. `current = vault.read(file)`; if `current ≠ diskContent` the file changed outside of us (Sync, another device) → `current` is saved as a `conflict` backup **before** anything is overwritten, and the user is notified. If the backup fails, the write does not happen.
4. `vault.process(file, latest => …)` (atomic): if `latest ≠ current` the file changed again since step 3 → it is returned unchanged (nothing overwritten) and steps 3–4 are repeated, at most 3 times; then the save fails and is retried later.
5. `diskContent = next`, `savedRevision = revision` captured at step 2. Changes arriving during a flush schedule another one. A failed write keeps the changes in memory and retries after 5 s, then with a growing delay (wave 0.7, see *Resilience* below); every write is read back first.

**Save check** (`serializeChecked`). Committed strokes are frozen (`freezeStroke`: immutable; an edit creates a new stroke), so their serialized line can be cached safely. A **new** stroke (drawn in this session) is serialized, parsed and validated, re-serialized, and the text must be identical; the verified line is cached per stroke object, so each stroke goes through this once. A stroke **read from a file** was already validated field by field by `parse()` and is marked as such (a `WeakSet`), so on the first save it is only serialized, without a second parse. After a successful load the session warms the line cache one page per timer tick (`DocumentSession.warmUp` → `warmStrokeLines`), so that first save is cheap. Duplicate stroke ids are rejected. Then:
- documents whose text is under `FULL_CHECK_LIMIT` (1 MB) are **fully re-parsed** on every save and must satisfy `serialize(parse(next)) === next`, as in 0.1;
- larger documents get the **incremental check**: the skeleton (header, pages, paper, page ids) is parsed and validated on every save, plus the strokes not seen before, plus the duplicate-id check. The full re-parse is skipped, so saving a notebook of several MB no longer re-parses all of it each time.

The text is still assembled in full on every save, so the cost of a save stays proportional to the document; only the parse and validation work is avoided. Opening a file always does a full `parse()`.

**Closing a view or switching file** (`onClose`/`onUnloadFile`): the stroke in progress is committed, then the flush is attempted up to 3 times; if the changes still cannot be written they are saved as an `unsaved` backup before the session is released.

**Deleted file**: the session stops writing (`mode = deleted`); unsaved changes, if any, go to an `unsaved` backup.

**External modification** (`vault.on('modify')` on the open file): the check is queued after any flush in progress and re-reads the file; if the content matches `diskContent` it is the echo of our own write → ignored. Otherwise, with no pending local changes → reload the document (if unreadable → `broken` + backup, without overwriting); with pending changes → ours are kept and on flush the external content ends up in the `conflict` backup.

**Stroke in progress**: on `visibilitychange`/`pagehide`/`pointercancel` the stroke in progress is closed and saved as a normal stroke before the flush, so leaving the app mid-writing does not lose it.

Known limit: if Android kills the process without notifying `visibilitychange`, at most the last ~1.5 s of changes are lost (or 10 s during continuous writing). Closing from recent apps first goes through the "background" state, so the flush happens; this needs to be verified on hardware.

**Instant save journal** (`src/storage/journal.ts`, M8 #31). 200 ms after a change (never during a gesture) the session records the document in `<config dir>/plugins/<plugin id>/journal/<doc id>.<0|1>.journal`, through `app.vault.adapter` (plugin-owned technical file: no vault events, no sync, no indexing). The entry is the serialized text itself: whole for notebooks under 256 KB, otherwise a line diff against `diskContent` (one stroke per line, so adding a stroke journals a few hundred bytes); a huge diff falls back to a full snapshot throttled by size, and nothing is journaled above 8 MB. Two slot files are written alternately, so a torn write leaves the previous entry intact; entries carry fingerprints of the base and of the resulting text. On open, an entry whose base is the file just read is applied in memory and the session is dirty, so the normal save writes it; the journal is deleted only after that save (and after every successful flush that leaves no newer changes). An entry already contained in the file is just deleted. An entry whose base differs (the file changed elsewhere) or that cannot be rebuilt is **never applied**: a copy goes to the backup folder (`journal` reason) before the journal is deleted, and if that copy fails the journal is left untouched. Journal I/O has its own queue; its failures are reported once and never affect saving. A notebook that cannot be parsed has no journal lookup (its document id is unknown). Entries are keyed by document id **and** file path (`journalKey`), so a copied file with the same id, or two sessions, cannot replay each other's entries; when a notebook goes from unreadable to readable (`broken` → `ok`) the journal lookup is done again.

**Resilience (wave 0.7; `storage/safe-mode.ts`, `storage/write-guard.ts`, `storage/integrity.ts`, `view/resilience.ts`).** No format change. Three per-device stores, all in Obsidian's vault-scoped local storage (`app.loadLocalStorage` / `saveLocalStorage`: never synced, so another device's state can never be mistaken for this one's):
- *Safe mode* (#346). `SafeModeTracker.begin(path)` writes an "opening" marker synchronously right before `DocumentSession.load()`; `ViewResilience` clears it (`succeeded`) `SETTLE_MS` (1 s) after the second animation frame following `onLoaded`. A marker still present at the next `begin` means the previous open crashed or froze (the user killed the app): failures + 1 and safe mode. Closing the view or `visibilitychange: hidden` before the open settled calls `abandoned` (not a failure). Safe mode keeps the count until *Open normally* (`requestNormal`, one forced normal open; a normal open that settles deletes the entry). In safe mode the view turns off `lowLatencyInk`, raw pointer updates and prediction, returns a blank page-panel thumbnail, refuses PDF text prefetch (`ReadingHost.prefetchAllowed`), and gives the renderer its media only after the first paint (marker stage `media`; a safe open that dies in that stage makes the next one skip media). Companion notes skip a notebook while a view shows it in safe mode (`isOpenInSafeMode`, a module-level registry). The renderer itself is unchanged: "progressive" means ink first, backgrounds later.
- *Storage full* (#347). In `flushRevision`: after the pre-read and the conflict backup, if the last failure was a full disk or a partial write, `SpaceProbe.probe(growth + 64 K)` writes a scratch file in the plugin folder (adapter, read back, deleted) before the notebook is touched. A `process()` that throws is followed by a read: unchanged → the error stands; the new text → success after all; anything else that is not a valid notebook → `repairTorn` writes the previous text back (only if the file still holds the partial text) and checks it; if that fails, the partial text is remembered (`tornContent`) so it is neither backed up as a conflict nor reloaded as an external change. A `process()` that returns is read back too (`verifyWrite`: compared in full up to `verifyLimitChars` = 2M characters and always while failing, a larger write by length and its first and last 64 K characters). Only what can be left of our own write (`isPartialOf`: empty, NUL bytes, a strict prefix of what was written, optionally followed by U+FFFD from a cut multi-byte character and NUL bytes) is a partial write that is repaired; the same rule holds after a write that threw. A read-back equal to the text before the write (or the same notebook with the `modifiedAt` of the version last loaded or saved) is a write that did not land: a failure, retried, with the journal and the document untouched. Another valid version of the same notebook found instead is someone else's write right after ours: it is backed up as a `conflict`, our document stays in memory and in the journal (deleted only once our content is verified on disk), and the merge banner (`onExternalNewer`) is offered; a different notebook is backed up and written over on the next attempt. Anything else (another app writing the file in place) is never repaired over: the save fails (`ForeignWriteError`) and is retried. After a failing streak, a valid version of the same notebook found on disk with a `modifiedAt` later than the version this session last loaded or saved is backed up as a `conflict` and not written over: `onExternalNewer` makes the view offer *Merge it into this notebook* (`planMerge`, one undo step, as for conflicted copies) or *Keep only this version*; both call `accept()`, and saving waits until then (closing still falls back to an `unsaved` backup). `WriteHealth` counts consecutive failures, classifies them (`classifyWriteError`: `ENOSPC`, "no space", quota, `SQLITE_FULL`… → no-space; `EROFS`, `EACCES`, `EPERM` → read-only/permission), and gives the retry delay (`retryMs` doubling to `retryMaxMs`, 5 s → 60 s); timed saves (debounce, max wait) wait for it, explicit flushes (close, background, *Retry now*) do not. Events `onWriteHealth` (the view's banner) and `onError` once per streak or change of cause. The document is never dropped: the session stays dirty, the journal keeps being written, the close path still falls back to an `unsaved` backup.
- *Integrity ledger* (#390). `onWritten(text)` fires after every verified write; `IntegrityLedger` keeps per vault path the last `RING_SIZE` (5) records `{fp, body, id, modifiedAt, at}` (`fp` = journal fingerprint of the whole text; `body` = fingerprint from the `"assets"` line on, i.e. without metadata and reading position), at most 200 notebooks, saved at most every 2 s and on unload. `onDiskContent` (first load and every reload, before journal recovery) is assessed by `assessDisk`: same `fp` → intact; does not parse → unreadable (the broken page as before); other document id or a newer format → not reported; `fp` equal to an *older* record and an older `modifiedAt` → `rolled-back`, unless a `before-restore` backup of this notebook newer than our last write exists (`restoredSince`). A version-history restore writes the snapshot with a new `modifiedAt` (`restoredText`), so it is a new edit for every device and never matches an older record, whatever the order of the backup files; the banner of a `rolled-back` verdict has no primary action (it may be a deliberate restore by an older plugin version) and says to choose *Keep this version* in that case, *Restore the newer version* is the primary action only for `altered`; same `modifiedAt` as our last write but a different `body` of the re-serialized document → `altered`; anything else → a normal change, never reported. The rule relies on every content save stamping a new `modifiedAt` (session, page move, API) and on metadata patches (library) leaving the body unchanged. On a reported reload, or a reload to an unreadable file, the text that was open is first written as an `integrity` backup. The command *Check notebooks integrity* runs `checkNotebookText` on every notebook of the notebooks folder (parse, `serializeChecked(…, true)`, ledger verdict, each asset's vault file present and of `asset.bytes` size). An empty notebook file is reported as *not downloaded* (Repair is offered, not as the primary action, and asks first), an asset file that is empty or smaller than recorded as *waiting* (`assetAvailability`), a larger one as damaged.

**Format layer** (`storage/format.ts`). The only module that knows what a notebook file is (`NOTEBOOK_EXTENSIONS`, `isNotebookFile`) and how the document text is taken from and put back into the raw file text (`readNotebookText`, `writeNotebookText`). The session, library, index, embeds, history, scanner and PDF import use these helpers instead of testing `.notebook` or reading raw text, so a later container change (M4: Markdown, §5) is confined here. The version snapshots and the journal store the document text as `readNotebookText` returns it.

**Version history** (`storage/history.ts`, `integration/history-service.ts`). On `file-open` and `modify` of a notebook, a snapshot is written (the file is read at once, so the snapshot is the content as it was on open, but it is parsed and written only after `whenIdle`, so a second full parse does not compete with the first paint; see Instant start) if the newest one is older than `snapshotIntervalHours` (default 24; 0 = off): `<notebooks folder>/_backup/history/<document id>/<YYYYMMDD-HHmm>.snapshot`, the document text byte for byte. Beyond `snapshotsToKeep` (default 10) the oldest go to the Obsidian trash. The command **Restore a previous version** lists the snapshots (date, page count, size), asks for confirmation, then: flushes the open notebook (restore stops if that save fails), checks that the snapshot's document id matches the file (a snapshot of another notebook is refused; if the file is unreadable it asks again), saves the current content as a `before-restore` backup, and writes the snapshot through the same checked path as a normal save. Open views reload the result.

**Instant start** (`view/last-location.ts`, `storage/idle.ts`, #271). The page being read goes into the workspace layout through `getState()` (`{ file, page }`, the same `page` that links use, applied by `setState`), saved by `requestSaveLayout` a second after it changes; the plugin also keeps the last notebook path and page in its settings for the command *Continue last notebook*. Neither is part of the notebook file. Work nobody is looking at is kept out of the first paint: the version-history snapshot parses after `whenIdle`, the stroke-line warm-up of the save check starts `WARM_UP_DELAY_MS` (400 ms) after the load, the page panel and thumbnails only work when open, PDF pages and images load on demand. Nothing in the load path was weakened: the document is still parsed, migrated and validated in full before it is shown, and a save before the warm-up has finished computes the lines it needs itself. Measure with `npm run bench:open` (headless Chrome, in-memory vault: CPU time only; not Android).

**Input routing** (`ink/input-policy.ts`, `ink/edge-zones.ts`, `ink/radial.ts`, `view/radial-menu.ts`, #151, #152). Routing is policy around the pointer controller; the stroke pipeline (sampling, wet ink, geometry, stabilizer) is untouched. A touch that starts in an enabled edge zone (`edgeRejection`, `edgeRejectionPx`, one switch per edge) routes to `ignore`. The pen side button (`button 2` or `buttons & 2`) follows `penButtonAction`: `radial` routes the pen-down to `menu` (no stroke, and the pen is not marked down for palm rejection because no stroke will end it; a press while hovering is detected on `pointermove` once per press), `eraser` draws as the eraser while held, `off` keeps the previous behaviour (a contact with the button held draws, a bare button press is ignored). The eraser end and the eraser button (`button 5`, `buttons & 32`) always erase. **0.5.1:** the detection is in `ink/pen-button.ts` (`BarrelDetector`, pure). Pen events (`pointerdown` with `button 2` / `buttons & 2`, `buttons & 2` appearing in `pointermove` / `pointerrawupdate`), a pen `contextmenu`, a pen `auxclick` (button 2) and a mouse right button within 700 ms of pen activity each start at most one menu per press; touch never does, and a pen `contextmenu` while the nib is on the page without a recent barrel signal (a long-press) is ignored. The eraser bits are never the side button. While a pen stroke is in progress the menu opens only if the stroke is younger than 350 ms and has not left a 6 px radius (the stroke is discarded, no dot); otherwise the press is ignored. The optional still-hold fallback (`penLongPress`, off by default) discards the stroke and opens the menu after 600 ms within 6 px. **0.5.2:** a Samsung tablet reports the side button pressed while the pen hovers as a primary press with pressure exactly 0 (`pointerdown`, `button 0`, `buttons 1`), and never as `buttons & 2` or a `contextmenu`. `BarrelDetector` now takes the pressure and the pointer id: such an event is a press only for a pointer seen hovering (`buttons 0`) before, and starts a "pressure-0 session" with transitions `press` / `contact-start` / `contact-end` / `release-click` / `release`. The controller never starts a stroke from the pressure-0 phase: a click (no contact) opens the menu on the release (`MenuSource "click"`); the nib touching while the button is held starts a stroke with the action's held tool (eraser through the existing `drawStart(sample, eraser)`, lasso or previous tool through its new third argument); the `penSideButton` action is `radial-eraser` (default), `radial`, `eraser`, `lasso`, `previous` or `off` (the 0.5.x key `penButtonAction` is not migrated except "eraser" / "off"). A `ChordDetector` (experimental) treats a dispatched pen `pointermove` with `button 0` during contact as a side-button toggle: the stroke is finished with `drawEnd(false)` (never discarded) and a new one starts with the held tool until the next toggle or the lift. The still-hold fallback is now 700 ms within 6 px of the first contact (`StillHold`, radius per instance), resolved by `effectivePenHold(choice, sideButtonSeen)`; a finger long-press (`fingerHoldMayStart`, 500 ms within 10 px, single finger, pen-only, no pen near) opens the same menu beside the finger (`fingerMenuCenter`). In all three cases the contact that opened the menu leaves the controller's bookkeeping (pen no longer "down", finger removed from `nav`, the detector's touch count released) because the menu swallows its lift.
The *Pen button test* modal (`view/pen-test-modal.ts`, log logic in `ink/pen-button.ts`) shows the raw events. While the radial menu is open it listens on `window` in the capture phase and stops the pointer events, so nothing reaches the page; the ring is placed by `clampCenter`, sectors are hit-tested by `sectorAt` (sector 0 at the top, clockwise), and a sector is chosen by lifting or tapping on it or by releasing the button over it.

**0.11.2: S Pen in Obsidian for Android** (`ink/pen-hover.ts`, pure). Obsidian's Android container drops stylus hover, and a click of the side button while hovering leaves one bare `contextmenu` (no `pointerType`, `button` -1, `buttons` 0, derived from touch, no pointer in progress). `HoverClickTracker` is fed every pointer event over the view (window watch and element; idempotent, stale pointers dropped after 5 s, a pen move without the contact bit ends that pen) and classifies a `contextmenu` as `hover-click` (no pointer down, no long finger contact ended within 600 ms, no mouse button within 600 ms, no text field focused), `long-press`, `pointer`, `mouse` or `text`. The controller judges the hover click before the existing detector (`hoverClick`): `BarrelDetector.claimClick` makes sure a press already handled is not repeated; the action is the radial menu (`MenuSource "click"`, position from `hoverMenuPosition`) or, with `penButtonEraserNext`, `EraserNextArm` (the next ordinary pen stroke starts with `drawStart(sample, eraser = true)`; a second click, a tool change or Escape disarms; `eraserNextChanged` updates the toolbar). The pen key (`penButtonKey`, `penButtonKeyAction`) is a window keydown listener in the view (capture phase), gated by `penKeyMatches` and `penKeyMayAct`, calling `PointerController.penKey`. With the nib touching, `InputPolicy.route` / `penErases` take the pressure: the barrel bit with pressure above 0 is the held tool, not the menu; `buttonStep` switches an ordinary stroke to the eraser (or the held tool) when `buttons & 2` / `button` 2 or `buttons & 32` / `button` 5 appear on a move (the ink so far is kept with `drawEnd(false)`). The settings UI is in `view/pen-button-settings.ts` (declarative definitions for the *Pen & input* section and a classic renderer used by the *Pen button test* until then); hotkey conflicts read `app.hotkeyManager` defensively (`hotkeyConflicts`). **Device finding (same release):** the pen's pointer events never carry the button (`buttons` 1); its only trace is the bare `contextmenu`, at or during the contact. `buttonContextMenu` handles it first: during a pen stroke, `contactVerdict(t - contactDownT, still, windows)` decides `held-at-contact` (within `afterMs`: `drawEnd(true)` drops the live ink, `drawStart(first sample, eraser)` and one `drawMove` replay the samples kept in `trail` since the landing), `during-stroke` (`drawEnd(false)` commits, the eraser starts at the last sample) or `none` (a nib resting still: a long-press). With no contact, the click is held as `pendingClick` for `beforeMs`: a pen pointerdown within that time starts as the eraser (`heldBeforeContact`), otherwise the hover-click action runs. Defaults 150 / 350 ms (`ButtonWindows`, settings `penButtonContactBeforeMs` / `penButtonContactAfterMs`). The Pen button test runs the same rules in `ButtonTimeline` and logs the Δ between pointerdown, contextmenu and the first move. **Review fixes (wave 0.12):** (1) every mid-stroke switch (`contactButton` `during-stroke`, `buttonStep`, `chordStep`) calls `drawStart(…, continued = true)`; the view keeps the ids of what `addStrokes` just committed (`justCommitted`) and the eraser of that contact (`ActiveErase.spare`, or `Lasso.spare` in Area mode) never touches them, so the kept ink is really kept. (2) `HoverClickTracker` remembers the last pen down and up: a bare `contextmenu` within `afterMs` of the landing of a contact that has already lifted (`liftedPenContact`) is the button held at that contact; for an ordinary stroke whose whole path is in `trail`, `retractStroke()` undoes its one history step (only while it is still the latest) and the trail is replayed as one eraser stroke. (3) `contactVerdict` returns `none` for a nib resting still (within `STILL_RADIUS_PX` = 12 CSS px, above Android's 8 dp slop; one CSS px is one dp in the WebView) for at least `NIB_LONG_PRESS_MS` (350 ms), even inside the window; `afterMs` is capped at `BUTTON_AFTER_MAX_MS` = 400 (settings clamped on read, `clampWindows` in the controller); the event is still cancelled. (4) During a pen stroke a `contextmenu` is ignored (still cancelled) while a finger or palm is on the view or touched it less than `TOUCH_RECENT_MS` (600 ms) before (`touchRecent`).

**0.12.4: the side button as one decision per pen contact** (`ink/button-contact.ts`, pure, no `obsidian`). On Obsidian for Android the button reaches the page only while the nib touches. `ButtonContact` tracks the pen contact the button may belong to (landing time and place, whether the nib left `STILL_RADIUS_PX`, the pen up or `pointercancel`) and every signal: a bare `contextmenu` with no contact (`contextMenuNoContact`, linked to a pen down within `beforeMs`, default 500), during the contact (`contextMenuDuring`: `contactVerdict` within `afterMs` of the landing is held-at-contact, later is ignored as mid-stroke, a nib resting for 350 ms is a long-press), after the lift (`contextMenuAfter`, within 400 ms of the pen up: a tap that never moved is the menu, a short drag is retracted and replayed, anything later is ignored), a `pointerdown` with the barrel bit and pressure above 0 (`barrelAtContact`), `buttons & 2` or the chord `button 0` move within `afterMs` (`buttonMove`), and the `pointercancel` after a `contextmenu` (`penCancel` makes an orphan that the next pen pointerdown, or a pen move of the same pointer, within 250 ms continues as the same contact; if none comes, a contact that never moved is a tap). The decision is two cases: a contact with the button held that never leaves the 12 px radius and lifts is `menu` (the controller discards the live ink with `drawEnd(true)` and opens the menu with source `click` at the landing); one that leaves the radius is `eraser`: `toButtonTool` drops the live ink (`drawEnd(true)`, nothing committed) and replays the whole `trail` (kept from the first sample while the contact waits) with `drawStart(first sample, eraser | held tool)`, so the tool covers the contact from its first point. While it waits the ink is live but never committed. With a setting that has no menu (`menuAllowed` false: erase, lasso, previous, gestures while held) there is nothing to wait for and the tool starts at once. A button pressed later in an ordinary stroke is ignored (ink goes on); the pen eraser end (`buttons & 32`, `button` 5) still switches an ordinary stroke. `requestMenu` never acts while a stroke is in progress. `EventLog` (Pen button test) feeds its own `ButtonContact` with the logged events and the modal shows `signal` / `decision` (`describeSignal`, `describeDecision`; localized in `view/pen-test-modal.ts`). Why 0.12.3 opened the menu and then inked: a bare `contextmenu` more than 150 ms before the pen down (or after the pen up, outside 350 ms, even after a long drag) fell to the hover-click timer, whose menu opened on contact while the stroke that followed was plain ink.

**Repair of a damaged notebook** (#140; `model/lenient-json.ts`, `model/repair.ts`, `storage/recovery.ts`, `integration/reliability.ts`). A `broken` view shows a **Repair…** button (also the command *Repair damaged notebook*). Nothing is written to the damaged file; it already has its `unreadable` backup. The repair reads the text in four steps: (1) `parseLenient` reads the JSON up to the first damage, closing the open objects and arrays there; a value read only in part (a cut number or string) is dropped, never guessed; (2) if the JSON was not complete, a line scan reads the layout `serialize()` writes (one stroke, element and asset per line, page fields on their own lines), so strokes after a damaged region in the middle are recovered too, matched by id (a stroke cut off in step 1 is replaced by its intact line); (3) the result goes through the regular migrations, and if the whole document does not migrate, pages and then single strokes are migrated one at a time; (4) every page, stroke, element, asset and the metadata are checked by the regular `validate()` on a one-item document, so the rules live in one place and a later format version is handled by its own migration and validator. Items that fail get conservative fixes (invalid points dropped, pressure clamped, unknown tool → pen, invalid colour → black, missing render → 1, duplicate id with different content → new id, exact duplicates kept once; page size and paper fall back to A4 grid, an unusable background is dropped and the page kept) and are kept only if they then validate; everything else is counted as lost. Unknown fields (e.g. from a newer version) cannot be carried over, because the model and the serializer only know the current fields: they are counted and stay in the original. Further sources: version-history snapshots, backup copies of the same file name whose document id matches, and journal entries of the file (full entries as they are; delta entries only on top of a text whose fingerprint is their base). Each source is repaired the same way and ranked by recovered items (strokes + elements + pages), then by date; the dialog shows every source with its summary, preselects the first and also offers *the damaged file and the best other source together* (merged with the rules below). The result is written with a **new document id** into a new notebook `<name> (repaired)` next to the original (so journal, version history and the library keep the two apart), read back and opened. A newer-version file is not repaired (it opens read-only).

**Conflicted copies** (#122; `storage/conflicts.ts`, `model/merge.ts`, `integration/reliability.ts`).
