A file in the same folder is treated as a conflicted copy of `Name.notebook` only if its name matches a sync-client pattern (`Name (1)`, `[Conflict] Name`, `Name [Conflict]`, `Name-<DEVICE>[-n]` from OneDrive, `Name (… conflict …)`, `Name_conflict…`, `Name.sync-conflict-…`; sources in `docs/research.md`) **and** it holds the same document id (a copy made by sync keeps the id; an unrelated notebook does not). When a notebook loads, or such a file is created while it is open, a notice offers **Merge…** (also the command *Merge conflicted copies*). The dialog shows, per copy, what would be added (`planMerge` summary). Merge rules: union of pages, strokes, elements and assets by id; the same id with different content keeps the main version and adds the copy's as a copy with a new id, unless that page already holds identical content (which makes a second merge a no-op); nothing is ever removed (deletions are not inferred); page settings (margins included) and the cover of the main notebook are kept (differences are counted); when a common page has a different left margin in the copy, the copy's strokes and elements are first moved by the difference of the left margins into the main page's frame, so ink identical relative to the background is recognised as identical and new ink lands where it was drawn on the background (`mergeCovers` compares in the same frame); when new ink of the copy would then fall off the main page (left of it because the copy has the wider left margin, or beyond its right or bottom edge, also with equal margins; text boxes count with their wrapped height), the main page grows just enough in the same Batch, counted as `pagesEnlarged`: wider margins through `SetPageMargins` on a page with a background (its own ink moves with the background), more height through `SetPageSize` (margin rule `marginsAfterResize`) on an infinite page, a larger size on a plain page; tags and bookmarks are united, favourite is "either". Pages only in the copy are inserted after the page that precedes them in the copy. Properties covered by tests: nothing of either side is lost (`mergeCovers`), idempotent, commutative for the content of each page, and one undo step that restores the exact previous file. The copy stays where it is; optionally it is moved (never deleted) to `_backup/conflicts/`, only after `flushNow()` succeeded and the file read back from disk contains everything of the copy. A copy that cannot be read is offered for repair instead.

**Restore one page** (#180; `model/page-restore.ts`, `integration/reliability.ts`). Page menu → *Restore page from a previous version…* (or the command): pick a version-history snapshot or a backup copy of this notebook (same document id), see its pages as thumbnails (the page with the same id, else the same position, is preselected), then *Replace current page* (`ReplacePage`: same page id, so bookmarks and links still work; content ids are kept unless another page uses them) or *Insert as new page* (`duplicatePage`, new ids). Assets the old page needs and the notebook no longer lists are added back (`AddAsset`). One `Batch`, one undo step; the rest of the notebook is untouched. The journal is not a source here: for a notebook that opens normally its entries are either already applied at open or belong to the live session.

**Sync and data safety** (wave 0.7, #342–#345; `storage/sync-state.ts`, `storage/presence-logic.ts` (pure), `storage/presence.ts`, `view/sync-safety.ts`, `view/media.ts`; no format change). The vault is synced by an app outside Obsidian (Google Drive or OneDrive through a folder-sync app such as Autosync or FolderSync), which the plugin cannot query: everything below is inferred from the files themselves.

- *Open on another device* (#342). While a notebook is open, each device keeps `<notebooks folder>/_meta/presence/<notebook id>.<device id>.json` (`{ kind: "notebook-presence", version: 1, notebookId, deviceId, deviceName, path, openedAt, heartbeat }`). It is written when the notebook loads, refreshed every 5 min (`HEARTBEAT_MS`; each write is an upload for the sync client) **only while the app is visible**, and on resume only when its last write is that old (`visibilitychange`, `pagehide`/`pageshow`: a phone in the background writes nothing), and removed when the last view of that notebook on the device closes and when the plugin unloads. The file name carries the device id, so a file is only ever written or removed by one device: the sync client never sees two writers and has no reason to make a conflicted copy of it. At startup a device removes its own leftover files (the app was killed); other devices' files are never touched. A record is *fresh* when its heartbeat is less than 15 min old and not more than 10 min in the future (folder-sync apps often sync every 5–15 min, so a shorter window would miss devices that really are open; a device that crashed keeps showing for up to 15 min; clocks that disagree by more than 10 min make the hint unreliable). Opening a notebook (and every create/modify/delete of another device's presence file for it) checks the fresh records of other devices and shows a non-blocking banner above the page: *Open read-only* (the view's `editable()` becomes false, so nothing is changed or saved; *Edit anyway* turns it back on) or *Open anyway* (hides the banner for those devices). The device id (12 random letters and digits) and name live in Obsidian's per-device local storage (`app.loadLocalStorage("notebook-device")`), never in the synced plugin settings; the name is editable in the settings (*Device name*). `_meta/devices/<device id>.json` records when each device was last seen (rewritten at most every 12 h or when the name changes, never removed) for the sync check. `_meta` is a reserved library folder.
- *Assets waiting for sync* (#343). An asset is *waiting* when its file is not in the vault, or is smaller than the size recorded in the document (`asset.bytes`, still arriving; `assetAvailability`). `NotebookMedia` then draws a dashed "Waiting for sync…" card for images (in the shape of the image element) and the label "Waiting for sync: <name>" on PDF and image backgrounds, logs no error, and the page stays editable (the ink and the reference are untouched). Vault `create`, `modify` and `rename` events for the asset's file clear what was cached (`assetFileChanged`: image state, placeholder, `PdfStore.evictAsset`) and the page is drawn again. A file of the right size that still cannot be decoded keeps the existing "Missing file" label.
- *Online-only notebooks* (#344). A notebook file whose text is empty, only whitespace or only NUL bytes (`isAwaitingDownload`: a 0-byte placeholder, or space reserved by a download in progress), or that does not parse but is still arriving (`downloadState` → `partial`: a NUL tail, JSON cut off at the end (`isTruncatedJson`), or a JSON syntax error in a file changed less than `RECENT_WRITE_MS` = 30 s ago, looked at again after that), puts the session in mode `not-downloaded` (`session.awaiting` = `{ kind: "empty" | "partial", damaged, kept }`): never written, and a save over an empty placeholder does not back it up as a conflict. A complete notebook followed by NUL bytes is read without them. A cut-off file found while waiting is backed up once per session, only after it has not changed for `PARTIAL_STABLE_MS` (30 s) and only up to `PARTIAL_BACKUP_MAX_CHARS` (1M characters), so a slow download leaves no backup per state; before it is ever written over, always. `damaged` is set when there is evidence of damage rather than a download: our own failed write left it, the journal holds entries for the notebook, or the text the empty or cut-off content replaced is this device's last write in its integrity ledger (`ownWrite`: same fingerprint) made less than `OWN_WRITE_RECENT_MS` (2 min) ago; a ledger record alone is not evidence, since a newer version downloading in place looks the same. When the file of an **open** notebook becomes empty or cut off, the version that was open is first backed up (`integrity`); with evidence, or when that backup failed, the document stays in memory and editable (`kept`; the last readable text stays the save and journal base, the empty or cut-off content is only the hold's echo; a verdict that rests on the change time is reviewed after it while holding too), nothing is written until the content arrives (then it is loaded when there are no edits, or backed up as a conflict and written over when there are) or the user chooses *Write it back* (`writeBack()`, after a confirmation that says a newer version may be downloading; it is the primary button only with evidence; the current content is backed up first, and a complete version that arrived meanwhile is not written over); without evidence the session goes to `not-downloaded`. A dirty notebook never writes over a file that is empty or still arriving: the save waits in the same `kept` state. The view shows *Not downloaded yet* or *Still arriving* with *Recover…* (the repair, highlighted when `damaged`; it asks first, uses the notebook id from the document or the ledger for snapshots and the journal, and builds a new notebook; the file is never written); the kept state shows a banner with *Write it back* and *Recover…*. The repair command asks for a confirmation when the file is empty, cut off or changed in the last `RECENT_CHANGE_MS` (3 min). the status says *Not downloaded*, and while the app is visible the file is read again every 4 s (mobile Obsidian does not always report changes) besides the usual `modify` event; when the content arrives it loads like a first open (journal recovery included). The library shows *Not downloaded yet* for a 0-byte file; embeds say the notebook is not downloaded yet. On Windows, OneDrive *Files On-Demand* placeholders report their real size and are downloaded by the read itself, so this mainly concerns Android folder-sync apps.
- *Sync check* (#345). Command and settings row *Check sync safety*; at startup (8 s after the layout is ready) a notice appears only when the set of issues differs from the last check on this device (stored in local storage); opening a notebook warns once per session about its own name, path or size. What **can** be detected: the notebooks folder inside the config folder (`.obsidian`) or a hidden folder, a `*.nosync` folder; names with `" * : < > ? | \` or control characters (refused by OneDrive, Windows and Android shared storage), names starting or ending with a space or ending with a dot, reserved names (`CON`, `NUL`, `COM1`…, `desktop.ini`, `~$…`, `_vti_`), names over 255 characters, vault paths over 200 characters (the vault's own location is unknown, so Windows' 260 and OneDrive's 400 characters may be exceeded), names that differ only in case, files of 10 MB or more (some sync apps skip them, for example in a free version) and 100 MB or more (above GitHub's limit for Obsidian Git users; backups in `_backup` are not counted); and whether files written by other devices (`_meta/devices`) have ever arrived in the folder, which is the only evidence available that the folder is really synced. What **cannot** be detected: the sync app's own configuration (excluded folders, selective sync, file size limits, Wi-Fi-only or battery rules), the cloud quota, whether an upload finished or failed, and files the provider rejected. The limits quoted are from the providers' public documentation as known at the time of writing and are given as hints, not guarantees.

## 5. Format evolution (toward M4)

- The container is isolated in `storage/format.ts` (§4), so the options below change that module and the migration, not the library, journal, history or embeds.
- **C (hybrid)**: implemented as O1 (companion notes, §12): a `.md` per notebook with properties and the text of its text boxes; the JSON does not change. Its content generator does not depend on the container, so it also serves B.
- **B (`.md` with data block)**: if B is chosen, the migration will be a file → file conversion: the JSON becomes the content of a code block, with `format`/`version` unchanged. The parser stays the same.
- Many strokes: v2 keeps a single JSON file (a generated 10,000-stroke notebook is about 6.8 MB on the PC). If that proves too slow on the device, a later version may split pages across files; `version` enables the migration. Decide with benchmark numbers from real hardware.

## 6. Input, rendering and tools

### Input
- `touch-action: none` on the container; `pointerType` distinguishes `pen`/`touch`/`mouse`.
- **Palm rejection.** Time-based: if the pen is active or was seen (even hovering) in the last N ms (default 500, configurable), touch does not draw and only pans (one finger) or zooms (two fingers). "Write with finger" mode is a setting (default off). If the pen touches down while a finger is drawing, the finger stroke is discarded. Contact size (experimental, default off): a touch whose `max(width, height)` exceeds the setting (CSS px) is ignored completely, scrolling included; it depends on what the device reports.
- **Samples.** With *Low-latency ink* on, the drawing pointer is read from `pointerrawupdate` where the WebView has it (Chromium in a secure context: samples are dispatched as the OS delivers them, not once per frame) and from `pointermove` otherwise, always with `getCoalescedEvents()`. Both events report the same samples, so each sample is delivered once: a sample is new only if its timestamp is later than the last delivered one (or equal with a different position; once `pointerrawupdate` has delivered samples in a stroke, `pointermove` only adds strictly later ones, so a batch sharing one timestamp is not repeated). Samples go into preallocated typed arrays (`SampleBuffer`: x, y, pressure, time), converted to page coordinates inline: no object per sample on the writing path. Navigation (pan, pinch) stays on `pointermove`.
- **Prediction** (`getPredictedEvents()`): setting *Predicted ink*, **default off**. Predicted samples are only drawn at the tip of the wet ink and erased at the next sample; they are never stored. Off by default because they overshoot at sharp turns and at the end of a stroke (the tip "jumps" ahead and back), which is one of the reported problems; the gain cannot be measured without the device. The setting key changed (`prediction` → `inkPrediction`), so the new default applies to everyone.
- **Pressure** is decided **once, when the stroke starts**, and never switches in the middle of it (the previous model trusted real pressure after three distinct values, which changed the width inside the first stroke). Real pressure is used if the device already proved it (≥ 3 distinct values in (0, 1), 0.5 excluded, in earlier strokes) or if the stroke's first sample is already in (0, 1) and not 0.5; otherwise the stroke uses pressure simulated from speed, and a device that proves real pressure meanwhile uses it from the next stroke. A trusted pen that then reports one value for 200 samples in a row loses the trust for later strokes. Real pressure gets a light causal smoothing (factor 0.6 per sample). "Always simulate pressure" setting.
- **Stabilization** (setting 0–10, default 2): an adaptive low-pass, the 1€ filter (`one-euro.ts`, after Casiez, Roussel and Vogel, CHI 2012; own implementation), on screen coordinates before conversion. Its cutoff rises with speed: when the pen is nearly still it removes tremor and digitizer jitter, when it moves the lag drops towards zero. Level L maps to `minCutoff = 20/L Hz`, `beta = 0.2/L Hz per px/s`, `dCutoff = 4 Hz`; 0 turns it off. On synthetic 240 Hz input, level 2 leaves 35% of the jitter at rest (the old exponential stabilizer at level 2: 53%) and lags 1.2–1.5 px at 300–1000 px/s (old: 1.5–5 px); level 10 leaves 15% (old: 27%) and lags 6–7 px (old: 7.5–25 px). The filtered point is what gets stored; the drawn tip follows the raw pen position, and when the pen lifts the raw position is added as the last point, so the line always ends where the pen did.
- Samples closer than 0.5 CSS px to the last stored point are not stored (the tip still follows the pen).
- **Fling**: when a one-finger pan ends with `pointerup`, the speed over the last 80 ms continues as an inertial scroll with exponential decay (time constant 325 ms); a new touch or wheel event stops it. Two-finger pan/zoom has no inertia.

### Pages: layout and virtualization
- `layoutPages` stacks the pages vertically in "document space" (pt) with a 24 pt gap, each page centred horizontally on the widest one. Strokes stay in page coordinates; a page box is only an offset, so layout changes never touch stroke data.
- The viewport maps document space to the screen. Fit-to-width uses the layout width; zoom is clamped to 0.25–8.
- **Virtualization**: the static redraw asks `visiblePages` for the pages intersecting the screen and draws only those; within a page it skips strokes whose cached bounding box is outside the visible area. Stroke `Path2D`s and bounds are cached per (frozen) stroke object and built the first time a stroke is drawn.
- **Add page** (toolbar button and command) inserts a blank page with the size and paper of the page in view **after the page at the centre of the view** as an undoable `AddPage`, then scrolls to it. The toolbar shows `n / total` for the page at the centre of the view. Further page commands are in "Page commands" below.

**Scroll modes (#14)** (`ink/page-mode.ts`, `view/page-mode.ts`, setting `scrollMode`). *Continuous* is the layout above. *One page at a time* uses `layoutPagesHorizontal`: the same page boxes side by side (64 pt gap, top aligned), so turning a page is just a viewport move and the renderer, hit tests and everything else keep working on document-space boxes. Each page is fitted to the view width on its own (`fitPageViewport`), the viewport is clamped to the *anchor* page (`clampToAnchor`) plus a sideways drag that rides on top. The pointer controller is unchanged: its `pan` handler goes to `PageMode.pan`, which tells a finger (a capture-phase listener counts touch and middle-mouse contacts; the pen is never one), the wheel and the fling after a lift apart. `SwipeLock` locks the direction after 10 px: sideways drags the page, vertical scrolls inside it, and a page wider than the view (zoomed in) pans freely, turning only after an overscroll. On lift `decideTurn` turns the page for a drag over 22 % of the width or a quick flick, and an animation (220 ms, none with reduced motion) settles on the page's own viewport. A pinch cancels the drag; a finger put down during the animation finishes it first. The setting and the toggle in the "…" menu are the only persistent state; the file format does not change.

**Reopen where you left off (O16)** (`ink/view-position.ts`). The view state carries `view: { page, zoom, x, y }` next to `page`: `zoom` is the scale relative to the fit-to-width scale and (x, y) the page point at the top-left corner of the view, so the same part of the page is shown after a rotation or in a different pane (a zoom within 1 % of the fit stays "fitted" and keeps re-fitting). The position only applies when its `page` equals the state's `page` (a link or the page panel that changes the page drops it); a position that needs the view size waits for the first measurement. It goes through the same debounce as the page (`LocationKeeper`), into the workspace layout, and into the plugin settings (`lastNotebookPos`, for *Continue last notebook*; a change of position alone is written at most every 5 s). Never into the notebook file.

**Staged loading (#329)** (`StagedParse` in `model/serialize.ts`, `DocumentSession.load({ staged, page })`). Over `STAGED_MIN_CHARS` (2 MB) a notebook at the current version is parsed in stages: JSON parse, then `validateWith` with a *shell* page reader (header, assets, metadata, and every page's id, size and paper; duplicate page ids are found here), then the wanted page in full, then `onPartial` shows that document read-only. The other pages are read with the real `readPage` (one shared set of ids, so duplicate stroke ids across pages are still found) in slices of about `STAGED_SLICE_MS` (10 ms), nearest first, each in its own task (`nextTask`, MessageChannel); the first slice waits for the frame that paints the page. The partial document is `session.partialDoc` for display only: `session.doc` stays null and `mode` "loading" (so `markChanged`, `flush`, the journal and every edit path refuse) until the last page has passed validation; then the same document object (stand-ins replaced in place) becomes `session.doc`, `mode` "ok", and `onLoaded` runs as before. A failing page ends the load like a damaged file (backup `unreadable`, nothing written). Not staged: small files, older versions (migration), files with a journal entry (recovery needs the complete document), reloads. `flushForClose` stops a load in progress. The parse itself (JSON.parse of the whole text) is still one block.

### Page commands

The toolbar's "more" menu has *Add page* and *Page options* (the page menu). It acts on the page at the centre of the view. Every item is a history command, so each is one undo step; the selection is cleared first, and nothing runs while a stroke is being drawn.

| Menu item | Command |
|---|---|
| Duplicate page | `AddPage` with `duplicatePage(page)` after the current page: new page id, new stroke ids, copied points, same size and paper |
| Move page up / down | `MovePage`; disabled at the first / last page |
| Delete page | `DeletePage`; disabled when the notebook has one page (a document always keeps at least one). Undo re-inserts the same page object |
| Paper: blank / ruled / grid / dotted / millimetre | `SetPaper`; the spacing becomes the default of the new kind |
| Size: A4 / A5 / Letter | `SetPageSize`; keeps the current orientation; strokes are not moved or scaled |
| Portrait / Landscape | `SetPageSize` swapping width and height; shown only when the page has a preset size |
| Export page as PDF / notebook as PDF / page as SVG | see §8 (not history commands) |
| Page margins… (pages with a background) | `SetPageMargins`, or a `Batch` of them with *All pages of this PDF* (§10) |
| Update PDF from a new version… (PDF pages) | one `Batch` from `planRepublish` (§10) |
| Export PDF annotations to Markdown (PDF pages) | not a history command (§10) |

After a command the view scrolls to the affected page. The commands tolerate repeated apply/revert: no page is inserted twice and a refused delete is not "undone".

### Rendering
- Canvases stacked in the view, sized to it × `devicePixelRatio`: **static** (paper pattern, saved strokes of the visible pages; a new stroke is appended without redrawing everything), **live** (lasso, floating selection, ruler, shape-tool preview and recognised shapes; redrawn with `requestAnimationFrame`) and **wet** (the pen or highlighter stroke being written, see "Writing engine"). Only the pen's wet canvas can ask for `desynchronized: true` (low latency: Chromium may present it without waiting for the compositor), and only with the *Low-latency ink (experimental)* setting (default off since 0.4.1: on some Android GPUs a desynchronized canvas can be promoted to an overlay composited as opaque); if the low-latency context cannot be created, a regular one is used, and `getContextAttributes().desynchronized` tells what was granted. A second, regular wet canvas is created on first use for the highlighter.
- **Guards** (0.4.1). Each step of a frame (static layer, live layer, wet-canvas release) and each part of a page (background, paper, every element and every stroke) and of the live layer (selection, ruler, lasso, preview) runs in its own try/catch: an exception skips that part, is logged once per kind as `[notebook] render <part>` and leaves the context reset before the next full redraw, so one bad stroke, image or PDF page cannot blank the page. If painting wet ink throws, wet ink is turned off for the view and strokes being written are shown on the live layer at the next frame (the pre-0.4 path), so the pen always leaves ink. The pointer controller guards each event the same way (`[notebook] input <event>`) and always closes the stroke on pointer-up.
- **Highlighter** strokes are filled with alpha 0.35 and `globalCompositeOperation = "multiply"` in stroke order, so they darken what is under them (paper, grid, earlier ink) instead of covering it. While writing, the highlighter's wet canvas holds the stroke opaque and CSS gives the canvas `opacity: 0.35; mix-blend-mode: multiply`: the same result over the page as the committed stroke, and overlapping parts of the stroke never darken twice.
- **CSS-transform fast path**: pan and zoom gestures call `setViewport(v, interactive = true)`. If the last full static redraw took more than 12 ms, the existing bitmap is moved and scaled with a CSS `matrix(...)` transform instead of being redrawn, and a full redraw runs 120 ms after the last gesture update. Any non-interactive change redraws immediately. While only the transform is applied, areas the old bitmap did not cover are blank and detail is not re-rendered for the new zoom. Pages that redraw in 12 ms or less never use it.
- **Level of detail** (roadmap 387, `ink/lod.ts`). Below 50 % zoom (`Viewport.scale` < 0.5) a full static redraw fills committed strokes from simplified geometry. The level is the largest tolerance of 0.25, 0.5, 1 or 2 pt that is at most 0.5 device px (`0.5 / (scale × devicePixelRatio)` pt); 0 (exact) when none fits. Render 1: the perfect-freehand outline is computed as usual, flattened (3 samples per quadratic piece) and simplified as a closed ring with Douglas–Peucker at 0.8 × the tolerance (the points are not decimated: perfect-freehand smooths by point index, so fewer points give another shape). Render 2: the nodes of the exact geometry (disc centres and radii) are simplified with Douglas–Peucker, the error of a node being its distance to the chord plus its radius difference, and the committed path is built from the kept nodes with the same `addInkPath`. A simplified shape is used only if it keeps at most 80 % of the vertices and the stroke has at least 8 points. Paths are cached per stroke and level (`WeakMap`, `null` when the exact path is to be used); the saved points and the exact path cache are untouched; the live layer, the wet canvas and the stroke just committed by `appendStroke` while the level is 0 use the exact geometry. Measured worst difference over handwriting-like strokes: 0.44 device px (test). `Renderer.setLodEnabled(false)` exists for comparisons (benchmark); there is no user setting. Thumbnails (`library/thumbnail.ts`) apply the same simplification at their pixel scale.
- Initial zoom: fit to the view width; recomputed on resize/rotation until the user zooms. Pinch, wheel and Ctrl+wheel zoom around the focal point.
- **Performance overlay** (setting): frame interval and input→frame (time from the last input sample to the next drawn frame), and for the wet ink input→paint (event timestamp to the end of the handler that painted it) and the processing time per input event, whether `pointerrawupdate` is in use ("raw"), the last static redraw time and the strokes and pages drawn; refreshed twice a second. These are software measures, not display latency.

### Writing engine

What was wrong (measured or confirmed in the code, PC numbers; see "Benchmarks"):
1. The live stroke was rebuilt **whole** every frame: perfect-freehand outline of all points (`last: false`), SVG string, `new Path2D(string)`, full-canvas clear and fill. On the PC a 1.5 s stroke at 240 Hz cost 11.4 ms of outline building in total (90 rebuilds, growing with the stroke) against 0.03 ms for the new geometry, and a lot of garbage per frame (a 7,200-sample replay in Node: 56 minor collections, the new path 1).
2. Visible **jumps**: perfect-freehand does not draw the start of a stroke until the pen has moved one stroke width, rounds the end differently with `last: false` than with `last: true`, and the predicted points (on by default) were part of the same outline, so the tip overshot and pulled back; at pen-up the committed outline (`last: true`, stabilizer catch-up points) replaced the live one; in the first stroke of a session the pressure switched from simulated to real in the middle of the line.
3. **Main-thread work during writing**: the debounced save (1.5 s after a stroke) and the 10 s max-wait save did not check for a gesture in progress, so a full `serializeChecked` (30 ms on the PC for 10,000 strokes) could run in the middle of the next stroke; the journal (200 ms after a stroke) waited only while the pen was down, so it typically serialized in the short gap between two strokes and delayed the first samples of the next one.
4. Frame-aligned input: `pointermove` is dispatched once per frame, so a sample waited on average half a frame before anything was drawn.

The new pipeline:
- **Geometry** (`ink-geometry.ts`, render version 2): the stored points are joined by a centripetal Catmull–Rom curve (no cusps or loops between uneven samples), subdivided into pieces of at most 1.5 pt (≤ 16 per segment). Along it sit *nodes*: discs whose radius follows the pressure with PEN_V1's response (`width × (0.2 + 0.6 p)`; highlighter: constant `width / 2`). The stroke is the union of the discs and the tangent trapezoids between consecutive discs. The curve between points k and k+1 depends only on points k−1…k+2, so its nodes are **final** as soon as point k+2 exists (or the pen lifts): the geometry is causal and incremental, and the nodes built while writing are exactly the nodes built later from the saved points (a test checks equality at every step).
- **Wet ink** (`wet-ink.ts`): each input event filters and stores its samples, then paints at once, in the handler (no animation frame): (1) the capsules (disc + trapezoid) that just became final, once; (2) the previous provisional tip is erased by clearing its dirty rectangle and repainting, clipped to it, only the final capsules whose boxes meet it (found through the bounding boxes of chunks of 64 capsules, so long strokes do not slow it down); (3) the new tip: straight capsules from the last final node through the stored points not final yet, the raw pen position and, if enabled, the predicted samples. Nothing is redrawn whole: the work per event stays constant as the stroke grows (a test counts the canvas calls and the capsules looked at over 2,000 samples). Each event fills its own capsules, so where two events' capsules meet, antialiased edge pixels are composited twice: a sub-pixel difference from the single fill of the committed path. The first point shows as a dot immediately.
- **Commit** at pen-up: the raw end point is added, the last segment becomes final and is painted, and the frozen stroke (render 2) is drawn on the static layer with the committed path of the same nodes; then the wet canvas is cleared. The committed path is the same area with far fewer edges: the node chain is cut into runs where it bends gently (< 25° between consecutive trapezoids, no side stepping backwards), each run is one polygon through the tangent points, and a disc is kept at each end of a run; inside a run it differs from the union of capsules by at most 2.4% of the radius on the outer side of bends (a test fills both on a fine grid: under 0.4% of the area differs). The pen's wet canvas is cleared one frame after the static layer shows the stroke, because a desynchronized canvas may be presented before the compositor frame (meanwhile the same opaque ink is simply shown twice); the highlighter's regular canvas is cleared at once. Copying the wet pixels to the static layer was tried and dropped: reading back a desynchronized canvas cost 2–4 ms on the PC. The static layer is never redrawn whole for a single stroke (except when an infinite page grows, as before).
- **Recognised shapes** (hold still 0.6 s) leave the wet layer: the shape is drawn on the live layer with perfect-freehand like before and committed as render 1; the shape tool is unchanged.
- **Main thread while writing**: the session's timed work (debounced save, max-wait save, journal entry) waits while a gesture is in progress and for 350 ms after it ends (`quietMs`), but never more than 4 s after it was due (8 s while the pen stays down or a text box is open); a deferred save keeps its due time (new strokes do not push it back), and the save and the journal have separate clocks, so continuous writing cannot hold saving back; explicit flushes (closing, `flushNow`) never wait. Page-panel thumbnails are not refreshed while writing.
- **Render versions**: every stroke records its geometry (`render`, format v5). Strokes written before v5 are render 1 and keep perfect-freehand with the frozen PEN_V1/HIGHLIGHTER_V2/SHAPE_V4 parameters, so existing notebooks look exactly as before; the renderer, SVG and PDF export pick the geometry per stroke (PDF writes discs as four cubic Béziers, SVG as two arcs, both nonzero fills). Library thumbnails (`library/thumbnail.ts`) use the same render-aware path (`strokeSvgPath`).

### Tools
- **Whole-stroke eraser**: 10 screen px radius converted to pt; the segment travelled by the eraser between two events is tested against the stroke's segments (with half the stroke width), with a bounding-box prefilter.
- **Segment eraser** (`eraseSegment`): same hit test, then the stroke is densified (no segment longer than half the radius, so a cut cannot slip between two distant points) and the points within `radius + width/2` of the eraser path are removed. Surviving runs of at least two points become new strokes with new ids and the original style; single-point crumbs are dropped. The original is replaced in place by a `ReplaceStroke`. The eraser acts on the page under the pointer, so one gesture can cross pages.
- **Gesture undo**: everything erased in one gesture (either mode, any number of pages) is recorded as a single `Batch`, so one undo restores it.
- **Lasso** (`lasso.ts`): the gesture polygon is tested with an even-odd rule; a stroke is selected when at least 50% of its points are inside. A selection belongs to one page. It shows a dashed frame, a **resize handle** at the bottom-right corner (uniform scale about the top-left corner of the frame, clamped to 0.05–20×) and a **rotate handle** above the top edge (rotation about the centre of the frame). Dragging inside the frame moves it.
- **Selection actions** (`view/selection-actions.ts`, no format change): a bar outside the stage (so its taps never reach the pen input) over the selected items. *Restyle* builds one `Batch` of `ReplaceStroke`/`ReplaceElement` (`ink/restyle.ts`); a request names a group (ink or highlighter) and never touches the other. *Copy* deep-copies strokes and elements into a module-level clip (`view/clipboard.ts`, shared by every notebook view of the session) and reads the image bytes; it also writes a marker text (`[Notebook selection: …]`) to the system clipboard so a later paste can tell whether something newer was copied elsewhere (`decidePaste`). *Paste* stores the images again through `storeAsset` when the target document lacks them (content-addressed, so a shared `_assets/` stores nothing twice), instantiates the clip with new ids at the centre of the view and adds it as one `Batch` (`AddAsset`, `AddStroke`, `AddElement`); *Duplicate* does the same inside the notebook without touching the clipboard. Pasting a system-clipboard picture or text goes through `navigator.clipboard` or the paste event, with a paste box where the WebView cannot read the clipboard. *Make white transparent* decodes the image (at most 2048 px), clears near-white pixels (`ink/image-alpha.ts`), stores a PNG as a new asset and replaces the element (`AddAsset` + `ReplaceElement`); no field is added to the file.
- **Objects wave 0.6** (no format change). *Ink attached to objects* (`ink/attach.ts`, `view/object-ink.ts`): a stroke belongs to the topmost element (image rotated box, laid-out text box) that holds at least `ATTACH_SHARE` (60%) of its points; there is no stored link, so the answer is recomputed from the geometry. `commitSelectionTransform` adds a `ReplaceStroke` for every such stroke that is not selected itself to the same `Batch` as the selection, and the live overlay draws them with the dragged matrix (the answer is cached while the selection is unchanged). Deleting elements computes the ink on them before the `Batch` runs and offers a second, separate undo step (`deleteInkCommand`) in a notice; the button does nothing if the elements are back. Setting `inkFollowsObjects` (default on) turns both off. *Personal stamps* (`ink/stamps.ts`, `storage/stamps.ts`, `view/stamp-palette.ts`): a stamp is a `.stamp` file in `<notebooks folder>/_stamps/` that contains a notebook document with one page (moved to the origin), so parsing, validation, asset records and future migrations are the notebook's own; `serializeStamp` re-validates the text before anything is written and `saveStamp` reads the file back. Placement (`planStampPlacement`) is `instantiateClip` + `pasteCommand`: new ids, one `Batch` that also registers the assets the document lacks; images whose file cannot be found are left out with a notice. Built-in stamps are shape-tool strokes generated in code with the current pen colour and width.
- **Transforms** are kept as an affine matrix (`transform.ts`) while the pointer is down: the selected strokes are hidden from the static layer and drawn on the live layer with the matrix, so a drag redraws only the selection. On release the matrix is baked into new strokes (`transformStroke`: points transformed and re-quantized, width scaled by the matrix's uniform factor, same id and style) and applied as a `Batch` of `ReplaceStroke` commands: one undo step. Delete (toolbar button, Delete or Backspace) is a `Batch` of `ReplaceStroke` with no replacement. Escape or changing tool clears the selection. Transformed strokes stay on their page and are clipped to it when drawn.

### Heavy page warning (roadmap 380)

`ink/heavy-page.ts` (pure): `pageLoad` counts strokes, points and the bytes of the distinct image assets a page uses (image elements and an image background; PDF files are shared by their pages and not counted); `heavyReasons` compares them with `HEAVY_LIMITS` (3,000 strokes, 120,000 points, 20 MB; strictly above). `planPageSplit(page, pageIndex, assets)` returns a `PageContentPlan` for `pageContentCommand`: strokes and elements sorted by the middle of their bounds, weighted by point count (text box 40, image at least 200 or its bytes / 256), cut where the lower part holds 35-65 % of the weight at the widest blank gap (else the cut closest to half); every item goes whole to the side of its middle; the moved content keeps its x positions and mutual distances and is translated up so its bounds start 36 pt from the top (never down). The new page is inserted right after the page with its size and paper (no background, no margins) in the same `Batch`, so one undo step restores the serialized document exactly. No plan for a page with a background or fewer than two items. `view/heavy-page-banner.ts` is the bar (`role=status`, *Split page*, *Dismiss*); `NotebookView` checks the page in view 800 ms after a viewport, history or stroke change (not while writing) and keeps the dismissed page ids for the lifetime of the view.

### Gestures around the writing engine (wave 0.6)
- The stroke pipeline is untouched; gestures only route or observe input. `view/gesture-input.ts` listens (never stops) to the stage's pointer events.
- **Finger taps** (`ink/finger-taps.ts`): `FingerTapDetector` follows the touch contacts of one series (landing within 150 ms, ≤ 12 px movement per finger, lifting within 150 ms, ≤ 350 ms in all); 2 fingers → undo, 3 → redo (`tapActionFor`, setting `tapGestures`). Touches the writing engine would not treat as fingers (pen recently active, palm size, edge zone) or a pen contact in the series make it invalid. At the tap the view snapshot taken at the first landing is restored, then undo / redo runs. In `PointerController` a touch that lands while other touches navigate joins them instead of starting a stroke.
- **Scribble** (`ink/scribble.ts`): at pen-up, before a pen stroke is committed (`NotebookView.eraseByScribble`), the stroke is resampled every 2 pt, turn-backs are found (angle > 120° over 8 pt on each side), and the longest run of legs ≥ 12 pt is checked: ≥ 4 reversals, legs within 45° of a common axis, drift across the axis ≤ 0.8 of the mean leg, ≤ 300 ms per leg, box ≤ 320 pt, ≥ 60 % of the stroke is the scratch. Victims are the strokes crossed by ≥ 2 legs (not larger than 4× the scribble's diagonal); they go in one `removeStrokes` batch and the wet ink is cancelled, so the scribble never reaches the document. No victims → the stroke is kept as ink.
- **Auto-scroll** (`ink/auto-scroll.ts`, `AutoScroller` with an injected clock): `strokeEnded(tipY)` schedules a scroll after 700 ms when the tip is in the bottom 18 %; every frame re-checks that no contact is active; a pointer down or wheel cancels it.
- **Pen side button**: `PenButtonAction` also has `lasso` and `lastTool`. `InputPolicy.penHeldTool` names the tool; `PointerController` passes it to `drawStart(sample, eraser, held)`; the view resolves `last` with `LastTool` (`ink/last-tool.ts`).
- **Side button at window level** (0.11.1): `PointerController` takes an optional `scope` (the view's `contentEl`). With it, it also listens on the window in the capture phase (`pointerdown/move/up/cancel`, `contextmenu`, `auxclick`, `mousedown`). Events over the stage are left to the stage's own listeners when they reach it (a passive capture listener on the stage marks them in a `WeakSet`; the window copy is checked after dispatch with `setTimeout(0)`), so each event feeds `BarrelDetector` once. Events that target another part of the view, or that were stopped before the stage, feed the detector only: a press opens the radial menu when the pen is over the stage, they never start a stroke, pan or zoom. Events outside the scope are ignored. `BarrelDetector.mouseDown` accepts a `mousedown` that no `pointerdown` announced (500 ms), with no finger near (1 s, `sourceCapabilities.firesTouchEvents`), while the pen hovers. The Pen button test (`view/pen-test-modal.ts`) logs `PEN_TEST_EVENT_TYPES` on the window in the capture phase (passive) and the document's `selectionchange`. In 0.11.1 `contextmenu` moved from the window to the scope (capture, not passive): it is cancelled when `BarrelDetector.blocksContextMenu` says so (from the pen, no `pointerType`, or the pen seen within 700 ms) unless the target is a text field, then judged by `contextMenu()` (on the stage by the stage's listener, elsewhere directly). The stage's pen `pointerdown` is cancelled after routing when its target is the stage or a canvas. The test area cancels `contextmenu`, `auxclick` and the pen `pointerdown`; `.notebook-pentest` has `touch-action: none` (the log `pan-y`).

### Pen gestures (roadmap 139; wave 0.8)
- **No format change, normal ink untouched.** A pen contact becomes a gesture only when `gestureStarts` (`ink/gestures/trigger.ts`) says so: gesture mode is on (`PenGestureLayer.mode`, view-only state), the side button is held and `penSideButton` is `gesture` / `radial-gesture` (the new held tool `"gesture"` of `heldToolOf`, delivered to `drawStart` like `lasso`), or the opt-in rest trigger fired (`dwellMayStart` + `DwellTracker`: the nib stayed within 5 px of its first contact for 0.65 s; the view drops the dot with `cancelWet` and starts the gesture; it also switches the hold-still radial menu off in `policySettings`). Touch and the eraser never start one. In `NotebookView.drawStart` the check comes before any tool code and the gesture branch writes nothing; `drawMove` / `endGesture` route to `PenGestureLayer` while a gesture is active.
- **Recognition is pure** (`src/ink/gestures/`, no `obsidian`, CSS px of the screen so it does not depend on zoom): `geometry.ts` (resample, smooth, Douglas-Peucker corners, crossings), `shapes.ts` (circle: 300-500° of one-way turning, radius within 27 %, end meets start; "^": exactly two legs, tip on top, 35-125°; "#": two upright and two level legs that all cross; star: five legs, 105-170° turns the same way, closed, ≥ 4 leg crossings; line / arrow: one long straight leg with an optional short head; retrace), `question.ts` + `dollar.ts` + `glyphs.ts` ("?": a hook of ≥ 170° of bending and the best template score ≥ 0.8, at least 0.03 ahead of the look-alikes 2, 3, 5, 7, 9, S, J, 6), `custom.ts` (3 samples, mutual score ≥ 0.74, not a built-in shape; match ≥ 0.82 and 0.04 ahead of the next gesture), `resolve.ts` (shape + context from a `GestureWorld` supplied by the view: ink inside the loop, strokes under the scribble, a page under the point, a selection under the tail and a navigator thumbnail under the tip). `session.ts` has the trail and the preview clock (at most every 70 ms).
- **View** (`view/pen-gestures.ts`): an SVG trail and a label beside the nib over the stage (`pointer-events: none`), a mode badge in the body; the preview is the same `recognise` on the partial trail; at pen-up the final result runs through `view/pen-gesture-actions.ts` (the world, the actions, `movePlan` in `ink/gestures/move.ts`). Select reuses `selectFromLasso`; delete reuses `scribbleEraseSteps`; space reuses `planSpaceChange`; tags open the existing `PageTagsModal`; bookmark is `SetMeta` with `toggleBookmark`; search is `PdfReading.openSearch`; move is a `pageContentCommand` over two pages; a custom gesture runs `app.commands.executeCommandById` (an internal API, guarded in `view/obsidian-commands.ts`).
- **Gesture ink and undo.** The drawn line is never added to the document by the gesture itself. For the effects that go through the history (delete, space, bookmark, move) the view records `AddStroke(line)` and then the effect as two steps (the same shape as scribble erase), so a single Undo restores the page and leaves the line as ordinary ink, and a second Undo removes it; the SetPageContent of the effect is planned on the page without the line and its `before` snapshot includes it. Effects that only open UI or select (select, tags, search, custom commands) record nothing and the line is dropped. The choice is deliberate: a false positive costs one Undo and the user's writing is not lost; the line is saved only if the user stops at that Undo.
- **Hidden strokes (format v8).** Gestures act only on what the user can see. When strokes can be flagged `hidden: true`, every gesture query and effect skips them: the circle's `enclosedInk` and the selection it makes, the scribble victims (`scribbleVictims` input), the "^" and move plans (hidden strokes stay where they are and are neither moved to another page nor counted as content below the line), and `movePlan`. The single place to apply it is the set of strokes handed to `gestureWorld` and to the plans (`page.strokes` filtered by visibility), so the pure recognisers need no change.
- **Registry.** The three actions (`pen-gesture-mode`, `pen-gesture-sheet`, `pen-gesture-custom`) are entries of `ui/actions.ts` (command ids unchanged; handlers in `view/view-actions.ts`); the mode toggle is a quick slot and in the All actions sheet, the other two are in the All actions sheet. `view/pen-gestures.ts` calls `refreshUi()` when the mode changes so the toggle shows its state.
- **Settings** (flat keys, no migration): `penGesturesOff` (built-in ids), `penGestureLongPress`, `penGestureSpacePt`, `penGesturesCustom` (templates are 48 normalised points per sample). The cheat sheet, the custom gesture list and the recorder are Obsidian modals (`view/pen-gesture-ui.ts`); the settings rows are in `view/settings-schema.ts` (*Pen & input* → *Pen gestures*), and `view/pen-gesture-settings.ts` maps the per-gesture switch keys. The only toolbar entry is the *Gesture mode* toggle in the "…" menu; the commands are `pen-gesture-mode`, `pen-gesture-sheet`, `pen-gesture-custom`.

### Words and lines (no OCR)
- **Segmentation** (`ink/words.ts`, pure, no `obsidian`): `segmentText(strokes)` returns a `TextLayout`: `lines` (top to bottom) of `words` (left to right) of stroke ids, each with a point-box (`bounds`, no pen-width padding), plus per line a `baseline` (endpoints and slope) and the median `letterHeight`; `ignored` lists strokes that are not handwriting text. This is the foundation for reflow (#92), alignment (#93) and headings (#102): they read the layout and never re-derive it.
- **Method.** Only `pen` strokes count (highlighter and shape strokes are ignored, as are strokes whose larger side exceeds 8× the page's median stroke size). Strokes sorted left to right join the open line whose recent strokes predict their vertical position (centres, not extents, so descenders and ascenders of neighbouring lines may overlap; a short robust slope estimate handles slanted writing; dots, commas and hyphens are matched by their centre with a wider tolerance) and that ends within 5 letter heights to their left, otherwise they start a line (so columns stay apart). Each line's baseline is fitted through the stroke bottoms with a RANSAC over pairs, so descenders do not tilt it. Along the baseline, a gap wider than 0.45× the line's median letter height starts a new word. Everything is relative to the handwriting size; nothing is in absolute pt. Tunable through `TextOptions`.
- **Cost and caching.** Roughly linear in the number of strokes (open lines are kept sorted by y, so a stroke only looks at nearby lines); per-stroke boxes are cached in a `WeakMap`. `TextLayoutCache` keeps the layout of one page and returns it while the stroke objects are the same (O(n) pointer comparisons); the view creates one per `Page` lazily, only when a double tap happens, so writing never pays for it. `npm run bench` (`tests/words.perf.ts`) prints figures for 1,000 / 3,000 / 10,000 strokes.
- **Gesture** (lasso tool only; `ink/multitap.ts`, `view/notebook-view.ts` `selectByTaps`). A lasso-tool contact that moves less than 8 CSS px is a tap; taps within 400 ms and 30 CSS px of the previous one count up (1, 2, 3). Tap 2 selects `wordAt` (the word with a stroke nearest the point, within 12 CSS px), tap 3 `lineAt` (the line of that word, or the nearest line box). It also works when the tap lands inside the previous selection (that would otherwise start a move); a single tap behaves as before (picks the element under it). The lasso tool was chosen over the pen because it never writes: with the pen, a double tap would leave dots and the first tap of the series would already be a stroke to undo. Pan, zoom, palm rejection and the text, shape and pen paths are untouched.
- Known limits: handwriting with very wide letter spacing may split into several words and tightly spaced words may merge (the gap threshold is relative to the line, not learned from the page); text written over a drawing may be grouped with it; rotated pages of text are not detected (lines are near-horizontal, slopes up to about 35°).

### Objects, pages and the navigator
- **Shape tool** (`shapes.ts`): drag out a line, arrow, rectangle or ellipse; points are generated every 3 pt along the outline and stored as a `shape` stroke (constant width, pressure 0.5). `constrain` offers 15° angle snapping for lines and arrows and equal sides for rectangles and ellipses (a modifier key). **Recognition** (`recognize.ts`): after the pen is still for 600 ms (radius 4 px, `isHold`), the stroke is fitted as a line, arrow, rectangle or ellipse and replaced by the clean shape if the fit is good enough; otherwise it stays as written.
- **Text boxes** (`TextElement`) and **images** (`ImageElement`) are page elements, drawn above the background and below the ink. Layout and measurement are shared (`text-layout.ts`); the text box is edited in place (`view/text-editor.ts`). Inserting an image stores the file as an asset and adds the element in one `Batch` (`AddAsset` + `AddElement`) and selects it. The lasso selects strokes and elements together (`elementsInLasso`); a transform is baked into new elements with `ReplaceElement`, deletion is `RemoveElement`, both inside the same `Batch` as the stroke changes.
- **Ruler** (`ruler.ts`, `view/ruler-gesture.ts`): placed by two fingers (centre and angle from the two touches, 20 cm long); pen points within a snap distance of the edge are projected onto it (`projectToRuler`), so a stroke started on the edge stays on the edge; the ruler draws mm/cm ticks.
- **Infinite pages** (`infinite.ts`): when ink reaches the bottom fifth of an infinite page its height grows by half a page of its width, up to 20 times the width; the growth is part of the same undo step (`SetPageInfinite` for the flag, the growth as a `SetPageSize` inside the stroke's `Batch`).
- **Selective eraser** (`erase-filter.ts`): the toolbar state `eraserFilter` (`all`, `highlighter`, `pen`, `color` + `eraserColor`) decides which strokes the eraser may hit; `pen` means every stroke that is not a highlighter (shapes included). Applied before the hit test, so it works with whole-stroke, segment and area erasing.
- **Erase area** (`erase-area.ts`, eraser mode `area`): the outline is drawn like a lasso; on lift every stroke with a point inside is cut at the outline (the outside runs keep their style and get new ids; runs under 2 points are dropped) and text boxes and images whose centre is inside are removed when the setting is on and the filter is *All ink*. One `Batch`.
- **Insert or remove space** (`space.ts`, `model/page-content.ts`, tool `space`): the pages are treated as stacked without gaps from the page touched. Items whose centre is below the line, and all items of the following pages, move by the dragged distance; each lands on the page that contains its new centre (pages are appended, like the last page, when it falls past the end). Removing is limited to the empty gap between what stays and what moves. Pages with a PDF or image background are barriers: the cascade runs only over plain pages, a background page never gets content from another page nor gives any up (overflow from it, or from the plain page before it, goes to a new blank page inserted right next to it, and removing never pulls content across it). An infinite page also ends the cascade and grows. `analyseSpace` decides the moves and is shared by the live preview (`Renderer.setSpacePreview`: the moving content is drawn shifted on the live layer and hidden from the static layer, like a floating selection, by the clamped amount; content that falls on a page that does not exist yet is only hidden) and by `planSpaceChange`. The result is a list of per-page content changes applied by `SetPageContent` (arrays changed in place, previous arrays kept for undo) plus `InsertPages`, in one `Batch`. Nothing in the document format changes.
- **Automatic next page** (`auto-page.ts`): when a committed stroke reaches the bottom tenth of the last page (setting `autoNextPage`, default on; not for infinite pages or pages with a background) an `AddPage` joins the same `Batch` as the stroke. On a page that is not the last, the same ink only offers a page (wave 0.9, 389; see *Text and objects* below).
- **Page navigator** (`view/navigator.ts`): side panel of page thumbnails rendered with the thumbnail code; tap to jump, press and hold then drag to reorder (`MovePage`), star to bookmark (`SetMeta`, `meta.bookmarks`), filter to bookmarked pages. Thumbnails are not refreshed while the pen writes.
- **Insertion**: `InsertPages` adds several pages in one step (PDF range, scanned sheets, blank pages, an image page).
- **Operations on several pages (wave 0.8, 378)**: the navigator keeps a selection by page id (`view/navigator.ts`: select mode, ticks, range, select all; touch long press starts it and, as before, arms drag-to-reorder). The actions are in `view/page-ops-menu.ts` (menus, prompts, the delete through the page trash) and every command is built by `model/page-ops.ts` from the selected ids: `ReorderPages` (new command: a list of ids before and after, so undo restores the exact order whatever happens meanwhile), and `Batch`es of the existing `SetPaper`, `SetPageSize`, `SetPageTags`, `SetMeta`, `InsertPages`, `DeletePage`. One call to `applyCommand` = one undo step. Move to another notebook reuses `library/move-pages.ts` (copy, save, then remove). The view only passes itself as a `PageOpsHost` (an extension of `PageToolsHost`).
- **Do not disturb while writing (wave 0.8, 374)**: `ink/writing-guard.ts` (pure) keeps the pens that are down and the time the last one was lifted; `writing()` is true while a pen is down and for 1.5 s after (a pen down for 15 s without an up event is assumed lost). `whenQuiet(task, key?)` runs a task now or at the first pause; `idle()` is its promise form. `view/writing-quiet.ts` feeds it from capture-phase, passive pointer events of type `pen` (all windows, blur and hidden tab release the pens). `view/notice.ts` `showNotice()` replaces `new Notice(` in the plugin: while writing a notice is queued (same text = one notice) and the returned handle (`setMessage`, `hide`) works either way. Background work waits through `storage/idle.ts` `whenIdle`, the library's reconcile and cover thumbnails, the navigator's thumbnails and the timetable offer. Companion writes keep their own pointer watch (`InputWatch`, same 1.5 s). New code must call `showNotice`, not `new Notice`.

### Equations and function graphs (wave 0.9: math; #110, #113)
- **Pure core** (`src/math/`, no `obsidian`, no DOM). `expr.ts`: tokenizer and Pratt parser (numbers with exponents, `+ − × ÷ ^ **`, brackets, `|x|`, `!`, `√`, implicit multiplication including split identifiers such as `ax` and `sinx`, functions with or without parentheses — `sin 2x` = sin(2x) —, comparisons with chains and `and`/`or`/`not`), compiled into closures over variable slots with parameters bound at compile time; names resolve only through internal `Map`s, so nothing reaches a global or an object property; at most 1,000 characters and 60 levels of nesting. `sample.ts`: adaptive sampling (an initial pass, then bisection where the midpoint leaves the chord by more than 0.15 pt of the frame, up to depth 10); a jump at the finest scale whose change sits in one half (floor) or exceeds half the frame (a pole) ends the line, undefined values end it too; polylines are clipped to the view plus one view size on each side (Liang–Barsky); parametric and polar curves the same way; implicit curves by marching squares on about 17,600 square cells with the saddle resolved by the centre value, a segment rejected when |F| at its midpoint exceeds the cell's corners (a sign change through a pole). `numeric.ts`: roots (sign changes refined by bisection, discarded when |f| grows: a pole; touching roots as zero minima of |f|), strict local maxima and minima refined by golden-section search, intersections as roots of f − g, a fourth-order central difference, adaptive Simpson integration. `graph-scene.ts`: the one description of a graph (frame, grid, axes with nice 1-2-5 steps and labels, curves, shaded areas, marked and labelled points, dashed tangent with its equation, table panel, legend with labels and parser errors), in page pt, cached per element object; the canvas, PDF and SVG draw it. `outline.ts`: the outline path grammar, tracing a coverage raster at half coverage, even-odd rasterising. `skeleton.ts`: *Convert to ink* (rasterise at 3–8 px/pt, Zhang–Suen thinning, a 3-4 chamfer distance for the widths, tracing from end points and junctions, spurs shorter than the stroke width dropped, constant-width `shape` strokes). `layout.ts`: the size of a new equation from the nearest handwritten line (`segmentText` of `ink/words.ts`: 1.9 × its median letter height, 8–72 pt) and the palette snippets.
- **Typesetting** (`view/math-typeset.ts`). `renderMath` output is laid out off screen at `fontSize` CSS px (1 px = 1 pt), painted into a canvas at up to 8 px/pt (6 megapixels at most) with a 0.12 em margin, and the alpha channel is traced into the outline (simplified to 0.04 pt; 0.15 pt if the path would be too long). Obsidian's MathJax produces HTML output: every `mjx-c` glyph is drawn with the font, size and character of its `::before` content at its measured box (stretched delimiters with their scale and the clip of an `overflow: hidden` ancestor), and every CSS border (fraction bars, root overlines) as a rectangle; MathJax's SVG output, when present, is drawn as an image. A TeX error (`mjx-merror`) is reported with MathJax's message. **Not run in Obsidian by the developer tooling**: the harness has a stand-in `renderMath` that draws the source as text in an SVG, which exercises the plumbing, not MathJax.
- **Drawing** (`ink/math-render.ts`). An equation is filled from its cached outline; without one, from an outline typeset this session (`storeTypeset`, keyed by source, style and size, scaled to the element's box), requested in the background the first time it is drawn (the canvas shows the LaTeX source meanwhile, and a source that fails is not retried in the session). Its number is drawn at its right. Exports typeset what has no outline first (`ExportOptions.prepare`) and number over the whole notebook (`equationNumbers`); with no maths engine they write the source as text.
- **Editing** (`view/math-tools.ts`, `view/equation-editor.ts`, `view/graph-panel.ts`). *Insert equation* opens the editor (LaTeX, live preview, palette, inline, numbered, size, colour) next to the centre of the view; *Done* typesets and adds or replaces the equation (one undo step, selected). *Insert graph* adds a graph of sin(x) and opens its panel; the panel edits a working copy and the renderer draws the draft live (`Renderer.setElementDraft`: the original and the ink on it are hidden from the static layer, the draft and the moved ink drawn on the live layer); *Done* commits graph and ink as one `Batch` (`graphEditCommand`), *Cancel* or Escape drops it. While the panel is open, pointers inside the graph pan (one) or pinch-zoom (two) its view and the wheel zooms (Shift: x only, Alt: y only); the view's commit-on-tap and the pen do not see those events. Anything open is committed by `commitText()` (saving, closing, another command), synchronously, so a flush right after writes it (an equation committed this way keeps no outline and is typeset when shown).
- **Ink on a graph** (`ink/graph-ink.ts`). Ownership is the *Ink follows objects* rule (`attachedInk`). Moving or scaling the frame with the lasso carries the ink with the same matrix (the view is kept, so graph coordinates stay). Panning or zooming the view moves the ink by `graphViewMatrix(before, after)` (page → plane under the old frame and view → page under the new ones), keeping pen widths; ink pushed outside the frame by a zoom stays where it lands and no longer belongs to the graph.
- **With the other wave 0.9 features.** Equations and graphs are *content*: an export with *ink only* draws them (with the ink), *background only* leaves them out (`LayerFlags.content`, PDF, SVG and the Markdown page images). Night mode maps them at render time like the rest (an equation's colour through the ink map; a graph's paper, grid, axes and border through the paper and line maps, its curves, points and labels through the ink map: `GraphPalette` in `ink/math-render.ts`); exports never use it. Layer order: `elementBand` has graphs between images and boxes and equations between boxes and text boxes, as the renderer stacks them, so *To front*/*Forward*… reorder them among their own kind. Ink can belong to them (`ownsInk`: texts, images, equations, graphs; not markers or boxes). Markdown export: equations are written in reading order with the text boxes as `$$…$$` (display; `\tag{n}` when numbered, numbers over the whole notebook) or `$…$` (inline), a `$` in the source escaped; graphs are in the page image (`page-raster.ts` draws graphs and equations; the equations without a cached outline are typeset first). *Page information* counts equations and graphs.
- **Limits (review fixes).** Every edit goes through `History.execute`, which runs `checkContent` (strict check of the strokes and elements not checked yet, cached per frozen object, as `serializeChecked` would before writing) and undoes and refuses a command that fails, with a notice; the graph panel's draft is `normalizeGraph`d and `checkElement`ed before commit. Transforms clamp an equation's or text box's size to `MAX_FONT_SIZE` (an outline that would exceed `EQUATION_OUTLINE_MAX` once scaled is dropped) and a graph frame to `GRAPH_MAX_SIDE` (4,000 pt; the reader clamps a larger one and counts it). A graph scene has a budget (`createBudget` in `math/sample.ts`): 250,000 evaluations for the curves (refinement stops, initial samples thin out) and 150,000 for the analysis (a counting wrapper ends it); at most 1,024 initial samples per curve, implicit grids at most 400 × 400 and aspect 1:50; `GraphScene.simplified` adds "≈" and a hint in the panel. Ink moved by a pan or zoom is bound to the graph by its id (`<graph id>~<id>`, `BOUND_SEPARATOR` in `ink/attach.ts`): it belongs to the graph wherever it is and is drawn clipped to its frame on screen and in every export; in one session moves are computed from the original points (`lineage` in `ink/graph-ink.ts`). Unsafe LaTeX commands (`UNSAFE_LATEX_COMMANDS` in `math/layout.ts`) never reach MathJax; typeset outlines are kept in an LRU cache.
- **Registry** (`ui/actions.ts`): `insert-equation`, `insert-graph` (sheet, Insert section; commands), `math-edit` (contextual bar of a selection with exactly one equation or graph), `math-convert-ink` (contextual bar of a selection with equations); `UiContext.selection.equations`/`graphs`.

### Text and objects (wave 0.9; roadmap 357, 361, 379, 389)

No format change: all four work on what the file already holds. The rules are pure (`model/layers.ts`, `model/text-search.ts`, `model/page-info.ts`, `ink/auto-page.ts`) and tested in Node; the panels are Obsidian modals and a chip (`view/find-replace.ts`, `view/page-info.ts`, `view/page-offer.ts`).

- **Layer order (357)**: the order of `page.strokes` and `page.elements` is the order they are drawn in, and it is already saved, so there is nothing new to store. The renderer draws elements by kind first (images, then v9 graphs, structured boxes, v9 equations, then text boxes, all under the ink) and in array order inside a kind, and marker badges above the ink. Therefore a move is defined per layer: strokes are reordered among strokes (so a highlighter can go behind pen ink, which is the main use), elements among elements *of the same kind* (`elementBand` mirrors the renderer; an element never changes kind band, so *To front* on a picture does not put it above a text box), and ink never goes under an element. A selection of both reorders each group inside its own layer, in one `Batch`. `reorder()` returns the new order or null when nothing would change: *front* / *back* move the selected items to the end keeping their relative order; *forward* / *backward* step each selected item past the nearest unselected item that overlaps it (bounding boxes; a selection moves as a block and never passes its own members), so one press changes what is seen. The command is `ReorderLayer { pageId, layer, before, after }` (history.ts): it places the items of the list into the slots they occupy now, so a list that has become stale (an item erased in between) can never duplicate or lose an item. The view (`moveSelectionLayer`) releases the selection after the move, because a selection is drawn on the live layer above everything and would hide the result. Entries: `layer-front` / `layer-back` / `layer-forward` / `layer-backward` in the registry (contextual bar of a selection, All actions sheet, commands that apply only with a selection). The bar holds at most eight entries, so on the widest bar *To front* and *To back* fit beside the basics; *Forward* and *Backward* are in the sheet and on hotkeys.
- **Find and replace (361)**: `findInDocument` walks the text boxes (`type: "text"`) of every page in reading order; the query is matched literally (escaped, never a pattern), case-insensitive unless asked, *whole word* meaning not glued to a letter, digit or underscore (Unicode aware, so accents count). Handwriting is ink and is not searched; marker labels and box titles are not text boxes. The panel lists at most 500 results but counts and replaces all of them. A replacement is a `ReplaceElement` (same id, position, width, size and colour; only `text` changes), *Replace all* is one `Batch` for the whole notebook (one undo step), and *Replace* changes one occurrence (`replaceHitCommand` refuses a hit whose text box was edited or removed since). The command goes through `applyCommand` like every other page command. A text box left empty is kept.
- **Page information (379)**: `pageInfo(doc, index, editedHere)` computes everything from the document: size and preset, paper, background kind, strokes by tool (and invisible ones), points, elements by kind, the distinct files the page uses (background asset and images) with their size from `doc.assets` (files the notebook does not list are counted separately), tags, bookmark (title, category), markers (open of total, by kind). **Dates**: the file stores no dates per page, so the panel shows the notebook's `createdAt` / `modifiedAt` and says so; the page's own last edit is only known on this device, from the recent-pages log (`RecentPagesService.pageEditedAt`, device-local, 100 pages), and is shown as *not recorded* otherwise. No devices are shown: nothing in the file records which device wrote.
- **Page offered in the middle (389)**: `needsPageOffer` is `needsNextPage` with `isLast` inverted (same bottom tenth, same setting `autoNextPage`, same exclusions: infinite, PDF and image pages). When a committed stroke qualifies the view shows a chip (`PageOffer`) near the bottom of the page area; accepting it calls `addPageAfter`, the existing insert (same size and paper, one `AddPage`, scrolled to). It never adds anything by itself, because a page inserted in the middle shifts every later page number. The chip steps aside when the pen goes down, after 9 s, on a page change or an undo; `OfferGate` remembers a page whose offer was taken, closed or timed out so it does not come back for every later stroke (per view, forgotten when the document is replaced).

### Selection tools (wave 0.10; roadmap 38, 63, 64, 65)

No format change: every operation replaces strokes and elements by transformed copies, exactly as dragging a selection does, so nothing new is stored. The geometry is pure and tested in Node (`ink/lasso.ts` `rectanglePolygon`, `ink/align.ts`, `ink/selection-transform.ts`, `ink/select-similar.ts`; none imports `obsidian`); `view/selection-arrange.ts` finds the selection, asks for a choice or a number, and runs one command; `view/arrange-modals.ts` has the picker and the size panel.

- **One routine for every transform.** A `Placement` says which matrix moves each stroke and each element (a stroke or element that is not in the maps stays). `applyPlacement(page, selStrokes, selElements, placement, measure, label)` turns it into one `Batch` of `ReplaceStroke` / `ReplaceElement` (`transformStroke` quantises the points to 0.01 pt and keeps pressure; `transformElement` is the one the drag uses) and returns the new objects to keep selected, or null when nothing changes. Dragging the handles (`commitSelectionTransform`) and all the operations below use it, so each is one undo step by construction. Ink written on a selected object (setting *Ink follows objects*) is passed as "followers" that move with it; `inkOwnership` finds them with the same rule as the drag (`attachedInk`, `inkOwner`).
- **Rectangle selection (63).** `ToolbarState.selectMode` ("lasso" or "rectangle", not persisted, like the eraser mode). In rectangle mode the lasso contact keeps its anchor and rebuilds the outline as the four corners of the box from the anchor to the pen (`rectanglePolygon`) on every move, and the pen lift calls the same `selectFromLasso` as a freehand outline: same 50 % rule for strokes, same element rule, the same visible-ink set (`inkInView`, so invisible ink stays out unless the solutions are shown), the same tap-to-pick. The stroke pipeline is not touched. UI: registry toggle `select-rectangle` (lasso bar, sheet, command), and a *Lasso | Rectangle* row in the tool options of the lasso (the lasso now has options, so tapping it again opens them; the pen-colour chip is not shown for it).
- **Select by colour or tool (64).** `selectSimilar(ink, elements, seed, "colour" | "tool")` and `selectByStyle(ink, elements, {colour?, tool?})` work on the strokes the reader can see; colours compare case-insensitively; a colour also takes text boxes of that colour, a tool never does. `styleCatalog` feeds the picker (tools, colours, tool-and-colour pairs with counts). The result replaces the selection (`selectItems`, which also turns the lasso on).
- **Align and distribute (38).** "As groups": `alignItems` makes one item per element and gathers the strokes into items by `clusterStrokes` (bounding boxes, grown by half of `GROUP_GAP` = 6 pt on each side, connected by a sweep along x; strokes owned by a selected object join its item). Items move rigidly. Align moves each item's edge or centre to that of the union of all items; distribute sorts items by their centre, keeps the extent of the union and makes the gaps between neighbours equal (needs three items). Movements under 0.02 pt are dropped, so aligning twice does nothing and says so.
- **Rotate, mirror, size (65).** `rotate90Matrix` is exact (entries 0, 1, -1), about the centre of the union of the bounds; a picture's `rotation` changes by a quarter turn and boxes, graphs and markers swap their sides, as the drag's rotation does. `mirrorPlacement` flips ink with an exact reflection about the centre line and moves each element to the mirrored position by a translation: the format has no flip field, so pictures, text boxes, equations and graphs are not flipped (the view says so in a notice), and the ink written on such an object is translated with it instead of flipped, so a picture and its annotations stay together. `sizeMatrix` scales about the top-left corner with separate x and y factors (limits 0.05 and 20 per operation, 20 000 pt on a side), then corrects the factors up to three times against the real result, because stroke thickness scales too and is part of the bounds, and finally puts the top-left corner back; text boxes and equations scale in proportion only (`transformElement` uses the geometric mean), so for them the other side can differ. `lockedSize` keeps the proportions in the panel.
- **Registry** (`ui/actions.ts`): `select-rectangle`, `select-by-style` (contextual bar of the lasso, sheet while the lasso is on or something is selected, commands); `selection-similar`, `selection-align-menu`, `selection-transform-menu` (the **second row** of the selection bar, see design-ui.md; each opens a native menu whose entries are registry actions); `select-same-colour`, `select-same-tool`, `selection-align-{left,centre,right,top,middle,bottom}`, `selection-distribute-{h,v}`, `selection-rotate-{cw,ccw}`, `selection-mirror-{h,v}`, `selection-resize` (sheet and commands; the palette shows them only with a selection, `SELECTION_ONLY` in `view/view-actions.ts`). `ActionDef.second` marks the second row and `ContextBarPlan.second` carries it.

### Clipboard and stamps (wave 0.10; roadmap 356, 359, 362)

No format change: a date stamp is an ordinary text element, a cross-page selection only moves strokes and elements between pages with the commands that exist, and the shared clipboard is a sidecar file.

- **Date and time stamp (356)**: `ink/date-stamp.ts` (pure) formats the date (`formatStamp`, tokens YYYY MM DD HH hh mm ss A MMMM dddd, `[literal]`, names through `Intl` in Obsidian's language), cleans the saved format (`cleanStampFormat`), picks the place (`stampSpot`: the last pen contact when it is at most 5 minutes old and its page still exists, else the middle of the page in view) and builds the text box (`stampElement`, sized with the renderer's `measureText`, kept inside the page). The view remembers the last contact (`lastPen`, by page id) in `drawStart` and `insertDateStamp()` adds the box with one `AddElement`.
- **Selection across pages (359)**: `ink/cross-page.ts` (pure). The lasso polygon is in the frame of the page it started on; `pagesTouchedBy` finds the pages its bounding box meets, `selectAcrossPages` runs `strokesInLasso` / `elementsInLasso` on each with the polygon moved into that page's frame (`frameOffset` = difference of the two boxes). The view's `Selection` keeps the first page that has items as `pageIndex` with `strokes` and `elements`, plus `others: PagePart[]` for the rest; `bounds` and the dragged `matrix` stay in the frame of `pageIndex`, and the renderer draws the other parts with `partMatrix(matrix, offset)` (`ink/transform.ts`) and without the page clip. `planCrossPageTransform` turns the committed matrix into one `Batch`: items whose centre ends inside a page go to that page (in its coordinates, ids kept), items that end outside every page stay; commands are ordered replacements, then additions (appended), then removals by descending index, so undo restores the original order. Delete, restyle, layer order, copy (`captureCrossPageClip`, one frame) and duplicate loop over the parts. A single-page selection has no `others`, so its code path is the one it always was. Ink that follows objects is not carried across pages.
- **Cross-device clipboard (362)**: `ink/shared-clip.ts` (pure) defines the file (`format: "notebook-shared-clipboard"`, `version: 1`, `entries`), each entry holding the selection as the text of a one-page notebook document (the format of personal stamps: validated by the notebook parser, with the asset records of its pictures) and counts for the list; unknown entries are kept when the file is written. Limits: 5 entries per device, 3 MB per file, 20000 pt on a side. `storage/shared-clip-store.ts` reads and writes `<notebooks folder>/_meta/clipboard-<device>.json` with the per-device helpers of `storage/device-files.ts`: `publish` (explicit action only) reads this device's file again, adds the entry, re-parses the serialized text and writes; a file that cannot be read, is empty or is from a newer version is never overwritten (one copy is kept, except for an empty file) and the copy is refused; `listOthers` only reads and skips unreadable files. The pictures are not copied: they are content-addressed files in `_assets`, found by id when pasting (`pasteInternal`, shared with the ordinary paste, which never replaces this device's own plugin clipboard).
### Navigation: history, fast scrollbar, status bar (roadmap 18, 50, O15; wave 0.10)

No format change: nothing here is stored in a notebook or in the settings (except the *Fast scrollbar* switch).
