- **Page history (18)**: `model/nav-history.ts` is the stack (`NavHistory`): places `{ path, pageId, page }`, at most 50, an index on the current one. `jump(from, to)` records a deliberate jump: the place the reader actually left replaces the current entry (so Back returns to where they were, not to where an earlier jump had put them), the forward places are cut, neighbours that are the same place merge, and a jump to the page in view records nothing. `peek`, `move`, `drop`, `rename` and `removeNotebook` keep it consistent with the vault. `view/nav-controller.ts` (`NavController`, no 'obsidian') adds the rules: the last place seen anywhere (`seen`) is the origin of a jump made by a view that has just opened; `go(-1 | 1)` skips entries whose notebook or page no longer exists (`exists`) and shows the target (`open`); the arrival that Back or Forward itself causes in another view is recognised (the expected place, valid for 5 s) and not recorded. `view/nav-service.ts` wires it to the vault (renames, deletions), the workspace and the mouse side buttons (`mouseup` in the capture phase, only while the pointer is over a `.notebook-view` and there is somewhere to go, otherwise Obsidian keeps the button). `NotebookView` records the jumps it makes itself (`goToPage`: the page panel, outline, find results, Go to page number; the marker panel; the fast scrollbar when a drag ends) and the explicit requests that arrive through the view state (`recordArrival` in `applyPendingPage` and `applyStartPosition`: links, the switcher, recent pages; restoring the workspace layout is not a request). Scrolling and `goToRelativePage` (page keys, swipes) are never recorded. Back and Forward are registry actions (`nav-back`, `nav-forward`; quick slots, page sheet, All actions, commands), relevant only when `UiContext.nav` says the history has somewhere to go.
- **Fast scrollbar (50)**: `ink/scrollbar.ts` is the geometry (no DOM): the vertical scroll range is the one `clampToPage` allows, the thumb position and size come from the viewport and the layout height, `positionAtPointer` and `offsetForPosition` turn a drag back into a viewport offset, and `pageAtOffset` names the page in the middle of the view (the page the indicator shows). `view/scrollbar.ts` (`FastScrollbar`) is a child of the view body, not of the stage, so the pointer controller never sees its events: a 24 px thumb that is shown for 1.6 s after a scroll (not while the pen writes) or while a mouse is near the edge, and only then takes the pointer; the rest of the track ignores it. Dragging sets the viewport through `setViewport(..., interactive)`, the same call as a pan. The bubble shows the page number at once and the thumbnail from the page panel's cache (`PageNavigator.cachedThumbnail` / `thumbnailOf`, the same cache and the same key as the panel's list); when the page is not cached it is drawn once the thumb has rested for 110 ms and the pen is quiet (`writing.whenQuiet`), so a drag draws at most one thumbnail per resting place. Hidden in one-page-at-a-time mode and when the notebook fits the view.
- **Status bar (O15)**: `view/status-bar.ts` (`NotebookStatusBar`) is the content of an item from `Plugin.addStatusBarItem()`, created only when `Platform.isMobile` is false. `NotebookView` keeps the save text and kind it already shows in the toolbar (`setStatus`) and the page in view, and tells the host (`statusChanged`) only when the combined text changes; the plugin shows the active notebook view's `statusSnapshot()` and hides the item when another kind of view is active. A click or Enter opens the page panel.

### Benchmarks
- `npm run bench` (`vitest run --project perf --silent=false`): `tests/model.perf.ts`, a custom timing loop (not vitest's bench API) over `stressDocument(10_000, 10)`: serialize, open (parse + validate), open plus first save with a cold cache, next saves, eraser hit test, segment cut, lasso, stroke outline. It is excluded from `npm test`. Dev-PC reference (Windows, Node 24; 10,000 strokes, 10 pages, 6.69 MB): serialize 153 ms; open 227 ms; open + first save, cold cache, 424 ms in total; next saves 16–19 ms; eraser hit test 0.04 ms per move (1,000 strokes, cached bounds); segment cut 0.005 ms; lasso over a quarter page 2.4 ms; stroke outline 0.04 ms. **These are PC numbers**: Android will be slower and must be measured with the in-app benchmark.
- In-app command **Run performance benchmark** (`view/benchmark.ts`): generates the same notebook, then times serialization, the first and later save checks and parse, and renders it with an **off-screen** `Renderer` at the current view size: first draw (builds paths), 30 redraws at fit width, 150 scroll frames, 30 redraws at 3× zoom. It also times the eraser hit test and segment cuts and reports the JS heap where the WebView exposes it. Then the **writing replay** (`ink/writing-bench.ts`): static redraw of a page with 2,000 strokes in render 1 and render 2 (JavaScript time, and with rasterization forced by reading one pixel back), then 10 synthetic strokes of 1.5 s at 120 and 240 Hz written on that page through the old pipeline (frame-aligned events, EMA stabilizer, whole outline rebuilt each frame on the live canvas) and the new one (one event per sample, wet ink painted in the handler): work per sample, per-frame cost, an input→paint estimate (old: half-frame alignment wait + frame work; new: handler work) and the pen-up commit. Nothing is written to the vault; the results can be copied.
- Writing replay on the dev PC (Chromium 152 in the desktop app's browser pane, 1280×800 @2x, off-screen): static redraw of 2,000 strokes with rasterization ≈ 60 ms for both render 1 and render 2 (JavaScript ≈ 1 ms); old pipeline 0.2 ms per frame and an input→paint estimate of 8.5 ms on average (p95 13–15 ms), almost all of it the wait for the frame-aligned event; new pipeline 0.03 ms per sample (p95 0.1 ms), nothing per frame; pen-up commit 0.3–0.6 ms (old 0.4–0.5 ms). `npm run bench` adds: perfect-freehand outline of a stored stroke 0.044 ms vs 0.006 ms for the wet-ink path of the same points; 360 samples through the 1€ filter 0.008 ms (old EMA 0.002 ms); wet-ink geometry for 360 samples 0.03 ms vs 11.4 ms for the old per-frame outlines. **None of this is display latency**, and the PC is much faster than a tablet: Android must be measured with the in-app benchmark and the overlay.
- Command **Create stress-test notebook**: writes the generated notebook (10,000 strokes, 10 pages) to the notebooks folder and opens it, to test real interaction on the device. The in-app benchmark does not measure real input latency; the performance overlay does, in the software sense above.

### Automatic page header / footer (0.15, #75)

The page number, the notebook's creation date and the notebook title are not stored: `ink/page-header.ts` (pure, no `obsidian`) derives one line from the settings (`headerMode` off / header / footer, which fields, `headerAlign` left / centre / right, `headerInExport`), the page's position and the notebook, and `drawHeader` paints it. On screen the view hands the renderer a painter with `Renderer.setPageDecor`; the renderer calls it once per visible page inside the static-layer redraw, right after the paper and before elements and ink, so it costs one `fillText` per visible page whenever that layer is redrawn and nothing per frame. A DOM overlay was rejected: it would need repositioning on every pan and zoom frame and would not appear in the page rasters. Pages carry no timestamp, so the date is the notebook's `createdAt`. The line is skipped on pages with a PDF or picture background (unless margins leave the strip free), and when an element (for example the lecture header, #210/#438, which starts 28 pt from the top) reaches into the 24 pt strip it would use, the line moves to the other edge; if both are taken, or the footer of an infinite page, it is left out. Exports use the same `headerLayout`: `PageImageRenderer` / `drawPageRaster` (PNG, printing), `pagesToPdf` (`PdfOptions.header`, Helvetica) and `pageToSvg` (`SvgOptions.header`) draw it with the background layer, so "Ink only" leaves it out; the setting *Include in exports* turns it off for all of them. No change to the document format.


## 7. Undo/redo

Command stack, not snapshots. Each command knows how to apply and undo itself; a new command clears the redo stack; maximum 500 commands. The history lives in memory (it is not saved in the file) and is reset on reopening or on reload after an external modification.

**Read-only mode (wave 0.10, roadmap 51).** `History` takes an optional `locked` predicate: while it is true `execute` does not apply the command (it returns false), and `undo` and `redo` return null; the stacks are untouched, so the history is intact when the mode ends. The view passes `() => this.userReadOnly`. See "Read-only mode" in section 6.

| Command | Used for |
|---|---|
| `AddStroke { pageId, stroke, index }` | pen and highlighter strokes |
| `ReplaceStroke { pageId, before, after[], index }` | segment erase (cut), whole-stroke erase (`after` empty), transforms, delete selection |
| `AddPage { page, index }` | add or duplicate a page; undoing never removes the last page; applying it twice never inserts the page twice |
| `DeletePage { page, index }` | delete page; a no-op (and so is its undo) on the only page |
| `MovePage { pageId, from, to }` | move up/down; undo puts the page back at the exact position it moved from |
| `SetPaper { pageId, before, after }` | change paper kind and spacing; strokes untouched |
| `SetPageSize { pageId, before, after }` | change page size / orientation; strokes untouched. v6: on a page with margins the background keeps its box and the right/bottom margins take the change (an infinite PDF page grows below the PDF); undo restores the previous margins |
| `SetPageMargins { pageId, before, after }` | v6 page margins: the page grows or shrinks by the change, the background keeps its size; a change of the left margin moves every stroke and element of the page by the same amount (same ids, new frozen objects), so ink never moves relative to the PDF. Undo restores the same objects and the exact previous size |
| `SetPageBackground { pageId, background, size }` | switches a page to another PDF page (a re-published slide) and size; ink, elements and margins stay; undo restores background and size |
| `AddElement { pageId, element, index }` / `RemoveElement` / `ReplaceElement { pageId, before, after }` | text boxes and images: insert, delete, move/scale/rotate/edit |
| `SetMeta { before, after }` | tags, favourite, cover, bookmarks (the library applies it to an open notebook through the view; a closed notebook is rewritten by patching its JSON) |
| `AddAsset { asset }` | registers a PDF or image asset in the document |
| `InsertPages { pages, index }` | several pages at once (PDF range, scanned sheets, image page) |
| `SetPageInfinite { pageId, before, after }` | the infinite-page flag |
| `ReorderLayer { pageId, layer, before, after }` | wave 0.9 (357): the strokes or elements of a page in a new order, nothing else; places the listed items into the slots they occupy now, so a stale list never duplicates or loses an item |
| `Batch { label, commands }` | one gesture or action made of several commands (eraser gesture, transform, delete); undone in reverse order |
| `EraseStrokes { pageId, items }` | multi-stroke whole erase from M1; kept and tested, no longer used by the view |

Committed strokes are immutable, so commands refer to stroke objects and indices, never to data that can change underneath them.

## 8. Export (PDF and SVG)

Export reads the document and does not go through the history. Files are written with the Vault API next to the notebook (`<name>.pdf`; single pages `<name> p3.pdf` and `<name> p3.svg`), adding ` 2`, ` 3`… if the name is taken; a notice shows the path or the error. The generators are pure functions in `ink/` (no `obsidian`, no DOM), so they run in tests. Entry points: the page menu (page as PDF, notebook as PDF, page as SVG) and the command **Export notebook as PDF**. Both formats always include the paper pattern.

Both exporters reuse `paperPattern`, the render-aware stroke geometry of the renderer (perfect-freehand polygon for render 1, the committed disc-chain path for render 2) and the same colours, so ink has the same geometry as on screen. Strokes stay vector.

**Images.** Before writing, `view/export.ts` rasterises what the writers cannot draw: each PDF page background is rendered at 200 dpi and each image (asset or scan) is re-encoded, all as JPEG; the same asset used on several pages is embedded once. The PDF writer embeds them as `DCTDecode` image XObjects below (backgrounds) or between (images, with rotation) the paper, text and ink; the SVG writer uses `<image>` with a data URL. An image that cannot be loaded is skipped and a notice says how many. Text boxes are written with Helvetica metrics (`pdf/helvetica.ts`) in the PDF and as `<text>` in the SVG.

**PDF** (`pagesToPdf`, PDF 1.4, written by hand, no library):
- One PDF page per notebook page. **Units: the PDF user unit is 1/72 inch = 1 pt = our document unit**, so `MediaBox` is `[0 0 width height]` with no scaling.
- Objects: 1 catalog, 2 page tree, 3 info (`Producer`, and `Title` = notebook name), then a page object and a content stream per page. The classic `xref` table is built from real byte offsets. Everything is ASCII (the title is a literal string when it is printable ASCII, otherwise UTF-16BE hex with a BOM), so string length equals byte length and the offsets are exact.
- **y-flip.** PDF has its origin at the bottom left, the document at the top left. Each content stream starts with `1 0 0 -1 0 H cm`, so coordinates are written as they are in the document.
- Content: white page rectangle, paper (lines per kind with width and colour via `w` and `RG`; dots as tiny filled squares, to avoid four Béziers per dot), then the strokes in order.
- **Strokes as cubics.** `strokeOutline` returns a polygon; the screen draws it as a chain of quadratic curves (control point = vertex, end point = midpoint to the next vertex). PDF paths have only cubics, so each quadratic is raised exactly: `c1 = p0 + 2/3 (q − p0)`, `c2 = p + 2/3 (q − p)`. The shape is the same as on screen. Each stroke is one closed filled path.
- **Highlighter.** Pages that contain a highlighter stroke declare `/GS1 << /ca 0.35 /BM /Multiply >>` in their resources, and highlighter strokes are wrapped in `q /GS1 gs … Q`: same alpha and multiply blending as the canvas.
- Numbers have at most 2 decimals and never use exponent notation.
- **No compression.** Content streams are not Flate-compressed: no dependency, and the file can be read and checked by eye. The price is size, roughly 100–200 bytes per stroke point, so a page with a lot of ink is several times larger than the same page from a compressing PDF producer. Compression can be added later without changing the structure.

**SVG** (`pageToSvg`): one standalone file per page. `width` and `height` are in mm (it prints at real size), the `viewBox` is in pt. White background rectangle, one path per paper line kind, one path for all dots (circles), one path per stroke; the highlighter has `fill-opacity` 0.35 and `mix-blend-mode: multiply`. The output is deterministic (no ids, dates or random values) and attribute values are escaped.

**What was and was not checked.** Unit tests cover the PDF structure (header and trailer, one page object per page, `xref` offsets and `startxref`, `/Length`, MediaBox, y-flip, colours, ExtGState only with a highlighter, paper on/off, title escaping, two decimals) and the SVG (size, paper, highlighter, escaping, determinism). Beyond the unit tests, an exported PDF was parsed with pdf.js (3 pages, Unicode title, fills, stroked paper lines, highlighter ExtGState) and an exported SVG was rendered with resvg. **Neither was opened on Android**, and how the device displays or shares the files is untested.

### Export choices, Markdown export, night mode and cropped margins (roadmap 381, 382, 367, 376; wave 0.9)

No format change. Pure logic is in `ink/`, `model/` and `pdf/` (tested in Node), the Obsidian side in `view/`.

- **Export layers (381).** `ink/export-layers.ts`: `ExportLayers` (`both`, `ink`, `background`), `layerFlags` and `planExport`. The *background* is the white sheet, the PDF or picture background and the paper pattern; the *ink* is everything placed on the page (strokes, text boxes, picture elements, boxes, markers). `pagesToPdf` and `pageToSvg` take `layers`; with `ink` the SVG has no white rectangle (it is transparent) and the PDF page just has no fill (a viewer shows it white); with `background` the content part of the page is skipped. `collectPageImages` skips what the choice leaves out, so an ink-only export never rasterises a PDF page. Hidden ink is untouched by the choice. `view/export-dialog.ts` (`ExportModal`) collects format, scope and layers; `runExport` in `view/export.ts` writes the files (one PDF, or one SVG per page for a notebook); names get ` ink` / ` background` and, when taken, ` 2`, ` 3`…. The registry action `export-dialog` (page sheet, sheet, notebook menu, command) opens it; from a page sheet it starts on that page.
- **Markdown export (382).** `model/export-markdown.ts` builds `notebook.md` (title, bookmarks from `outlineEntries` as `##` headings, `![](page-001.png)`, text boxes in reading order with user text escaped so it cannot become a heading or a list), picks the file names (`planMarkdownFiles`: three-digit page numbers, a name that exists or was chosen earlier in the same export gets ` 2`…; the check is case-insensitive) and sizes the renders (`renderSize`: dpi 100, 150 or 200, capped at 8192 px a side and 16 million pixels). `ink/page-raster.ts` draws one page on a canvas in the same order as the PDF export (it never uses night colours). `view/export-markdown.ts` draws and writes one page at a time through `app.vault.createBinary` (the canvas is shrunk to nothing after each page), then writes `notebook.md` last, so it exists only if all the images were written. A failed background (a PDF that cannot be rendered) leaves the page without it and is counted in the usual "images could not be included" notice. No files are deleted on failure.
- **Night mode (367).** `ink/night.ts`: pure colour mapping, no state. `nightInk` mirrors a colour darker than mid-grey to the light side (lightness `0.5 + 0.9 (0.5 - l)`), keeps hue and saturation, then raises the lightness until the contrast with the night paper (`#1b1d22`) is at least 4.5:1. `nightHighlighter` holds lightness in 0.45-0.62 and saturation at 0.6 or more; `nightLine` mirrors paper lines. `Renderer.setNight` applies the mapping where colours are used (`setInk` for static, live and wet ink, text boxes, paper lines, the paper fill, a dark veil over backgrounds); the highlighter uses `screen` at 0.45 instead of `multiply` at 0.35, and the wet highlighter canvas gets the same through the CSS class `notebook-night` on the stage. Colours are memoised per renderer. The setting `nightMode` is part of `ReadingSettings` (plugin settings, not the notebook). Exports, thumbnails and the companion notes never use it.
- **Cropped margins (376).** `pdf/content-box.ts`: `detectContentBox` finds the non-white bounds of an RGBA bitmap (channel below 245, at least 2 pixels per row and column, 2 % padding, minimum 30 % width / 15 % height; transparent pixels are white; a blank bitmap gives null) and `pageWindow` turns the box into a horizontal range in page coordinates, widened to include every stroke and element of the page (so ink in a margin stays reachable). `view/content-boxes.ts` renders each PDF page once at about 100 px wide through the media's `PdfStore`, reads it back with `getImageData` and caches the box for the session, one page at a time with a yield between pages, nearest pages first. `ink/layout.ts` gets an optional `window` on `PageBox` (box-local) and `Layout` (document space, the union), built by `withWindows` (windows of 97 % of the width or more are dropped; a page without a window counts as whole, so a notebook with a plain page keeps the whole width in continuous mode). `fitWidth`, `clampToPage`, `fitPageViewport`, `panRoom` and `turnViewport` read the window; nothing else does, so page boxes, ink coordinates, hit testing and the renderer are unchanged and exports ignore it. `view/display.ts` (`ViewDisplay`) owns both toggles; `NotebookView.reflow` rebuilds the layout when a measurement moves a window by more than about 1.5 % of the width. The setting `cropMargins` is part of `ReadingSettings`. Only side margins are cropped; saved reading positions are relative to the fit scale, so they shift slightly if the toggle changes between visits.

### PNG images, page ranges and Print (roadmap 81; wave 0.10)

No format change. `ExportFormat` is `pdf | svg | png | print` and `ExportScope` is `page | notebook | range` (`ink/export-layers.ts`); `planExport(format, scope, pageIndex, count, layers, range)` returns the jobs: one PDF (named ` p2-4`, ` p1+3+5` or ` selection` for a range), one SVG per page, and for `png` and `print` one job holding every chosen page. `model/page-range.ts` reads typed ranges (`1-3, 5, 8-`, `-4`; pages from 1; a page outside the notebook, a backwards range or a syntax error is a typed error, never a silent drop) and `model/export-pages.ts` has the scales (`PNG_SCALES` = 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4 px per pt, passed on as dpi to `renderSize`, so the caps of 8192 px a side and 16 million pixels apply) and `planPageImages`, which names images by the real page number (`page-003.png`) with " 2", " 3"… for a name that exists or was chosen earlier in the same export.

`view/page-images.ts` (`PageImageRenderer`) draws one page on a canvas through `drawPageRaster` and encodes it as PNG; it loads the PDF page or picture a page needs (only what the *Contents* choice keeps), keeps picture bitmaps between pages, shrinks the canvas after each page and counts what could not be loaded. The Markdown export, `view/export-png.ts` (writes into a vault folder with `createBinary`; the folder is created and "taken" names are checked case-insensitively by `prepareExportFolder`) and `view/print.ts` all use it. `runExport` (`view/export.ts`) dispatches the new formats; `ExportModal` (`view/export-dialog.ts`) shows the folder and scale for PNG and the page field for a range, validates both before closing, and offers Print only when the host says the device can print. The registry has `export-png` and `print` (sheet, notebook menu, commands) that open the same dialog on that format; `print` is relevant only when `UiContext.canPrint` and its command only applies where `canPrint()` is true.

**Print** (`view/print.ts`). `canPrint()` is `Platform.isDesktop && !Platform.isMobile` and a `window.print` function: an Android WebView has no print dialog, so there the action is hidden rather than left to do nothing. `printPages` renders each chosen page at 150 dpi to a PNG blob, appends one `<div class="notebook-print-page"><img></div>` per page to a `div.notebook-print-root` in `activeDocument.body` (`display: none` on screen), waits for the images to decode, adds `body.notebook-printing` and calls `window.print()`. `styles.css` (`@media print`) hides every other child of the body, breaks the page after each sheet and scales an image to the sheet width and height (`max-height: 100vh`, `object-fit: contain`); a page taller than 1.9 times its width (an infinite page) gets `is-long` and flows over sheets at its own size. The container, the class and the blob URLs are removed on `afterprint` (with a ten-minute timer as the last resort), also when something fails. Nothing is written to the vault. Not verified: how the print dialog of the real Obsidian (Electron) paginates this; the harness stubs `window.print` and checks the container, the decoded images and the clean-up.

### Read-only mode (roadmap 51; wave 0.10)

A per-view switch, `NotebookView.userReadOnly` (`setReadOnlyMode`, `toggleReadOnlyMode`, getter `readOnlyMode`), reached by the registry action `toggle-read-only` (All actions → View, command) and by the lock button of the toolbar while it is on. It is a **view state only**: no field in the document, nothing stored, reset in `onUnloadFile` (a notebook opened next is editable). It is independent of the sync-safety "Open read-only" flag and of hidden PDF annotations, but it joins them in `editable()`, the one test the view, the registry (`UiContext.editable`), the selection, insert, page and text flows already use, so every existing guard applies. On top of that:
- the input policy takes `fingerDrawing: false`, so a finger only pans and zooms (and a tap still opens a link); a pen or mouse contact that would draw is refused by `drawStart` (it returns false while not editable), so nothing is written, erased, selected or dragged;
- `History` is locked (above) and `markChanged` returns at once, so even a stray call can neither change the document nor schedule a save;
- switching on first ends what is in progress (stroke, text box, open equation or graph, selection, armed marker, link chip, page offer, radial menu) and calls `flushNow()` so a change made just before is saved by the normal path; the mode itself never calls the session's backup or write-back paths (`readonly` mode of the session, which is about newer-format files and keeps `doc` unloaded, is not involved);
- the toolbar's pen indicator and favourites are hidden while it is on (CSS `.notebook-toolbar.is-read-only`) and the lock button, which switches it off, takes their place; on a phone it is the lock alone.
Pan, zoom, the page panel (read-only parts), links, search, page-by-page mode and the export actions (all `readOnly: true` in the registry) keep working.

## 9. Library

Samsung-Notes-like screen (`library/library-view.ts`) opened from the ribbon or the command **Open library**. Library folders are real folders under the notebooks folder (reserved: `_assets`, `_backup`, `_trash`, `_templates`); a notebook is one file; moving, renaming and syncing work with any tool. The vault is the source of truth.

- **Index** (`library/index.ts`, `library/host.ts`): a cache `library-index.json` in the plugin folder (`INDEX_VERSION` 3 since 0.6; an older version is rebuilt, a newer one is left as it is and handed back unchanged on save, while this device works from an index rebuilt in memory). One entry per notebook: path, folder, name, file mtime and size, created/modified, page count, tags, favourite, cover colour, text of the text boxes (capped at 20,000 characters, with the offset where each page's text starts, for the page jump of a search hit) and the first-page thumbnail (a data URL, with the mtime it was drawn from). An entry is stale when the file's mtime or size differs; only stale or new files are read (`filesToRead`), a few at a time, with progress shown in the library ("Indexing…"). Deleting the index only costs a rebuild.
- **Queries**: scope (all, favourites, recent = the 20 last modified, a folder, a tag), search and sort (modified, name, created). Search ignores case and accents, matches title, tags and text, and returns a snippet and the page of the first text hit; the scopes *Everywhere*, *This folder* and *This view* narrow it.
- **Metadata** (tags, favourite, cover) lives in the notebook (`meta`). The host changes it with `patchNotebookMeta` on the document JSON, or through the open session when the notebook is open (so it is saved with the checked path and is undoable there); if the notebook is open and refuses the change the user is told. Duplicating a notebook copies the file and gives it a new document id (`patchNotebookId`), so the journal and version history of the two never mix.
- **Operations**: create (notebook, folder), rename, move (menu or long-press drag onto a folder), duplicate, delete (moves to the library trash, see "Library trash, page trash and templates" below; folders move whole), import a PDF, scan into a new notebook.
- **Thumbnails** (`library/thumbnail.ts`): paper, page background (PDF page or image, loaded through the media source), elements and ink of the first page, drawn on an off-screen canvas; strokes thinner than a minimum are widened so handwriting stays visible, and drawing stops after a time budget.

### Pinned, archived and manual order (roadmap 54; wave 0.10)

No change to the document format and no notebook file is ever moved or renamed to archive it. The state is a sidecar, `<notebooks folder>/_meta/library-<device>.json` (`{ format: "obsidian-notebook-library-state", version: 1, device, items: [{ key, pinned?: { v, at }, archived?: { v, at }, rank?: { v, at } }] }`, one item per line, sorted by key).

**Where it is stored, and why.** Three places were possible. (1) A field in the notebook: refused, it needs a format change and would rewrite a file for a view preference. (2) The plugin settings (`data.json` in the plugin folder): it is one file that every device rewrites, so two devices syncing through Google Drive or OneDrive make conflicted copies of it, and a notebook-by-notebook list does not merge. (3) The per-device sidecar pattern that the Inbox, the practice copies and the textbook configuration already use: **chosen**. A device writes only its own file; the sync service never sees two writers of one file. Reading merges every device's file: per notebook and per field (`pinned`, `archived`, `rank`) the entry with the latest `at` wins, a tie goes to the larger device id. So pinning one notebook on the phone and archiving another on the tablet both survive, and un-pinning on one device overrides an older pin made on the other. A device that writes a field takes a time later than the one it has just read for that field, so a device whose clock is behind still wins with a later change. A change equal to the merged state is not written. `LibraryStateStore.update` reads every file again first, applies the changes to this device's file, re-validates the text and writes only if it differs; an unreadable or empty own file (a sync app may not have finished downloading it) or one from a newer version is never overwritten (`library.unreadable-<hash>.json` keeps one copy and the change is refused with a notice); other devices' unreadable files are skipped and reported in `blocked`; items a version does not understand are written back unchanged. Looking at the library never writes. The library view reloads the state when any `library-*.json` file is created, modified or deleted (a sync arrived).

**Identity.** A notebook is keyed by its document id (`id:<id>`), which survives renaming and moving the file on any device (duplicating gives the copy a new id, `patchNotebookId`). The library index entry gained an optional `id` (no index version bump: the index is a disposable cache); entries from an older index are filled in the background from the start of the file (`headerField`, no full parse) and an action on one of them reads it first. A notebook whose id cannot be read (unreadable, not downloaded) is keyed `path:<path>`, so its state is lost if it is renamed before it can be read. Two files with the same document id (a sync conflict copy, a copy made outside the plugin) would share their state, so archiving the original would hide the copy and a hidden conflicted copy is never seen or merged: `stateKeys` (library-state.ts, used through `LibraryIndex.stateKey`) keeps `id:<id>` for one file of such a group (among those that do not look like a conflicted copy of another, the first by path) and keys the others `copy:<id>:<path>`, so a copy is never hidden by, or hides, the original; a copy is keyed by its path, so it loses its state if renamed.

**Order.** Archived notebooks are filtered out of every scope, the counts, the tag list and the search (`filter` of `LibraryIndex.query/search/tags`); the scope `archived` shows only them. Pinned notebooks form a group at the top of every list except *Recent* and the archive. The new sort `manual` orders each group by `rank` (a number): notebooks never placed by hand come first in the order of the base sort (a new notebook shows at the top), then the placed ones by rank. `planMove` changes one rank to the middle of the new neighbours when that shows the wanted order (checked by simulation), and otherwise numbers the whole list afresh in steps of 1024 (only the ranks that change are written); it is used by dragging a card onto another (the dragged card takes the target's place, within its group) and by *Move up* / *Move down*, which from another sort first fix the order as shown.

### Creation dialog and folder metadata (wave 0.12)

**Dialog.** `view/create-dialog.ts` (DOM) over `view/create-logic.ts` (pure: name checks, page sizes, the folder list, the new document). The host (`library/host.ts`, `openCreate(tab, folder?)`) gives it the vault (folders, `exists`), the defaults (the settings' page size, orientation and paper), this device's last used folder (`app.loadLocalStorage("notebook-create-last-folder")`, not synced), the template gallery and the image picker (`view/pick.ts`). A notebook is created through the usual path: `notebookDocument(spec)` (or `documentFromTemplate`), the cover image copied to `_assets/` by `storeAsset`, `serializeChecked` before the file exists, then `storage/notebooks.ts createNotebook` and `openNotebook`. The registry actions `new-notebook` (Notebook tab) and `new-folder` (Folder tab, also a command) run `host.createNotebook()` / `host.createFolder()`; the 0.5 command id `create` (*New notebook*) still works and opens the same dialog, and so do the library's *New notebook* buttons and the file menu's *New notebook here*, which already went through `host.createNotebook(folder)`. Name rules: not empty, none of `\ / : * ? " < > | # ^ [ ]`, no leading dot, at most 120 characters, and not taken (a notebook `<name>.notebook` in that folder, or anything at the folder path): errors show under the field, nothing is renamed silently. Custom page sizes are 50–1000 mm per side; the orientation decides which side is longer.

**Cover and description: in the notebook (format v11).** The description and the cover choice describe the notebook itself and must travel with it (copied, moved, synced, taken to another vault), like the cover colour and tags already do (`meta`), so they are stored in the document, not in a sidecar: `meta.description` (non-empty, at most 500 characters) and `meta.coverArt`, either `{ kind: "art", style, seed }` (one of the seven `ArtStyle` names of `ui/art/generative-art.ts` and a seed, drawn in the colour `meta.cover`: the same three values give the same picture on every device and no pixels are stored) or `{ kind: "image", asset }` (an image asset of `doc.assets`, so the library check, copies and repair treat it like a page image; `referencedAssets` includes it). Both are written only when present, so a notebook without them is written as in v10 apart from the version. The v10 → v11 migration only drops these two keys from a v10 file (a v10 reader ignored them, so they were never ours); large v10 (and v9) files are still loaded page by page (`StagedParse`, which drops them too). Lenient read: an invalid description or cover (unknown style, missing or non-image asset) is dropped and counted; strict write: `serializeChecked` refuses it. A conflicted copy keeps the main notebook's description and cover and takes the copy's only to fill a gap (never the copy's image cover, whose asset may not be in the main notebook). Tests: `tests/model-v11.test.ts`.

**Folder metadata: a per-device sidecar.** A folder is a plain vault folder with no file of its own, so its colour, icon, description and art style live in `<notebooks folder>/_meta/folders-<device>.json` (`{ format: "obsidian-notebook-folder-meta", version: 1, device, items: [{ path, color?, icon?, description?, art?: { v, at } }] }`, one item per line, sorted by path; `v` null = cleared), exactly like the library state: a device writes only its own file, every device's file is merged on read, per folder and per field the latest `at` wins (a tie goes to the larger device id), a write takes a time later than the one it read, a no-op is not written, an unreadable, empty or newer own file is never overwritten (`folders.unreadable-<hash>.json` keeps one copy and the write is refused with `FolderMetaBlockedError`), other devices' bad files are skipped and reported in `blocked`, items a version does not understand are written back. Keyed by vault path: `library/folder-meta-events.ts` follows the vault's `rename` event of a folder (`planRename`: the fields of the folder and of every folder inside it are set at the new paths and cleared at the old ones; a rename of a folder without metadata writes nothing), and reads the files again when a `folders-*.json` is created, modified or deleted (a sync arrived). Deleting a folder clears nothing (a folder restored from the trash comes back by a rename); the dialog writes every field of a new folder, so a new folder never inherits the look of an old one with the same path. Known limit: a rename that reaches another device as a delete and a create (some sync services) is followed only by the device that renamed, which is enough because its file carries the move.

**Read API for the library and home** (`library/folder-meta-store.ts`, reached as `host.folderMeta`): `getFolderMeta(path): { color?, icon?, description?, art? } | null` (synchronous, from the last read; `load()` runs once the layout is ready) and `onFolderMetaChange(fn): () => void` (after a write here or a re-read that changed something). Absent fields fall back to the defaults (colour `colorFor(name)`, icon `folder`, art `artStyleFor(name)`). Writing: `set(path, { color, icon, description, art })` (`undefined` leaves a field, `null` clears it) and `rename(from, to)`.

### Library check (roadmap 27; wave 0.10)

`library/library-check.ts` (pure, over a `CheckSource`: the file list with sizes and times, and a text reader), `library/library-check-live.ts` (open notebooks and the journal), `library/library-check-seen.ts` (this device's record) and `library/library-check-modal.ts` (the vault as the source, the report, the confirmation). It never writes a notebook. **Notebooks**: every `.notebook` of the vault outside the plugin's reserved folders is read once and parsed. A text that does not parse is classified with the same detection as the notebook view (`storage/sync-state.ts`: empty or NUL-only, NUL tail, JSON cut off at the end, a syntax error in a file changed seconds ago) as *not downloaded yet*, a newer format as *saved by a newer version*, anything else as *unreadable*. A read that throws (an online-only file) is *not downloaded* too, never *unreadable*. **Assets**: only files directly in `<notebooks folder>/_assets/` named `<24 hex>.<pdf|png|jpg|jpeg|webp|gif>` are looked at. An asset is *in use* when its id appears (as a run of hex digits, anywhere in the text, so an unreadable notebook protects what it names) in any notebook; otherwise, and only then, the plugin trash, version history and restore copies, templates, stamps and `_meta` files are read, except the device sidecars that cannot name an asset (`folders-<device>.json`, `library-<device>.json`, this check's own record and their `.unreadable-…` copies, `isNonHolderSidecar`), so an unsynced or empty one does not make the check incomplete (every one read must be a whole JSON text: empty, a NUL tail or a text that does not parse counts as unavailable), and what they name is *kept* (reported, never offered). **Open notebooks and the journal** (`RunOptions.live`, given by `library/host.ts`): the document of every open notebook view, the commands of its undo and redo stacks and its pending media (`NotebookView.libraryCheckRoots`, walked for 24-digit hex runs in strings and object keys by `assetIdsIn`), and every `*.journal` file of the instant-save journal in the plugin's config folder (read through the Vault adapter by `journalAssetIds`; a slot that cannot be read or is not a whole journal entry is unavailable). What they name is *in use*. They are asked once before judging and once more right before the first move. **How long unused**: the modification time of a file is the one of the device it came from (Google Drive and OneDrive keep it), so it says nothing about when this device first saw the file; a picture can arrive before the notebook that uses it. Each device keeps its own record of when it first saw an asset unused, `<notebooks folder>/_meta/library-check-<device>.json` (`{ format: "obsidian-notebook-library-check", version: 1, device, seen: { <asset id>: <ms> } }`, one file per device like the library state, only this device's own file is read or written, written only by a complete check and only when it changes, never over a file that cannot be read; the check skips it when it reads `_meta`). An asset is an *orphan* (offered) only if the record says it was first seen unused at least ASSET_GRACE_MS (24 hours) ago and it is still unused; an asset used again leaves the record. The first sighting, a modification in the last hour (a guard that remains), no record or an unreadable one are *recent* (not offered yet); an asset with no content yet is *waiting*. **No guesses**: if any notebook is unreadable or not downloaded, any holder file was not downloaded, cut off or did not parse, or the open notebooks or the journal could not be inspected (or no inspection was given), `complete` is false and no orphan is reported (their count and size is shown as not judged). Results are cached per path, modification time and size for the session, so a second run reads only what changed. **Moving**: `moveOrphans` runs the check again, asks the open notebooks and the journal once more, moves a confirmed file only if it is still an orphan, still the size that was listed, not named by a view, and still passes `isAssetFilePath`, and does it with a plain rename (`vault.rename`) to `<notebooks folder>/_trash/assets/<name>` (`<id>-<n>.<ext>` if the name is taken): inside the vault, so a sync service sees a move and not a delete, and putting a file back is a move back into `_assets/`. Not `vault.trash` or `fileManager.trashFile`: both are deletions from the vault's point of view, and the second follows the *Deleted files* setting, which can be permanent. The plugin trash's own listing and expiry (`_trash/items`, `_trash/pages`) do not touch `_trash/assets`. Known limits: reading version history and backups on a very large library takes time (it only happens when something is left to judge, and can be cancelled by closing the dialog); a view that is not loaded yet (a deferred tab) holds nothing in memory, so only its file and the journal protect its assets; a torn journal slot (left by a crash) makes the check unsure until the notebook is opened and saved again; walking a notebook with a very long undo history is capped (then incomplete).

### Class timetable (roadmap 115)

`src/timetable/`, no document format change. **File**: `<notebooks folder>/_timetable.json`, plain JSON chosen over a Markdown table because it round-trips exactly, is easy to edit by hand and syncs like any file: `{ "version": 1, "weekOne": "YYYY-MM-DD"?, "entries": [{ "id", "course", "day" (1 = Monday ... 7), "start" "HH:MM", "end" "HH:MM", "room"?, "notebook"? (notebook path or course folder), "weeks": "all" | "odd" | "even", "from"?, "to"? }] }`. `parseTimetable` is strict: anything it cannot understand is an error and the file is left alone (the editor says so and offers to start a new timetable, copying the old file to `_backup/` first); saves are serialized, re-parsed and compared, written with `vault.process`, then read back. Only the Vault API is used.

**Logic** (`schedule.ts`, pure, tested): local wall-clock time (`Date` getters), end exclusive; alternating weeks count from the week containing `weekOne` (week 1 is odd), else ISO week numbers; `from`/`to` are inclusive; `classesAt(now, lead)` counts a class from `lead` minutes before it starts, overlapping classes: the one started last is current; `nextClass`, `findOverlaps`, `courseForNotebook`, `pickTarget` (linked file, else a folder's notebook named like the course, else its latest, else a new notebook).

**Runtime** (`service.ts`): `TimetableService` reloads on file change, registers a course provider (`current.ts`) that `runNewLecture` asks for the default course, offers the class in progress on layout ready and when the library opens (once per class and day per session; a notice with a button, or directly with *Open it without asking*), and `openClass` opens the notebook, waits for its edit context and adds the lecture page with `addLecturePage` (shared with New lecture), or shows the existing one. The library host exposes `nowClass()` for the *Now: <course>* shortcut.

**Course page tag**: `applyCourseTag(page, course)` in `page-tag.ts` is called for lecture pages created with a known course (New lecture prefilled from the timetable, *Open the current class*). It returns a copy of the page whose v7 page tags (`model/page-tags.ts`) include the course, normalised as page tags are (`normalizePageTag`, case-insensitive union, existing tags first); `AddPage` inserts that page, so the tag is in the same undo step. `PAGE_TAG_HOOK_ACTIVE` is true.
### Library trash, page trash and templates (wave 0.6; #160, #26, #74)

No change to the document format. All three live in plugin-owned folders under the notebooks folder, reserved like `_assets` and `_backup` (`RESERVED_FOLDERS`: `_trash`, `_templates`, `_meta`), so the library tree, index, search, companion notes and conflicted-copy detection never see them (`isInTrash` in `storage/trash-paths.ts` is the root-independent test used outside the library).

- **Library trash** (`storage/trash-paths.ts` pure, `storage/trash.ts`, `library/trash-modal.ts`). Deleting a notebook or folder from the library moves it with `vault.rename` (links in the user notes are not rewritten) to `_trash/items/<id>/<original name>` and writes `_trash/items/<id>/meta.json` first (`kind`, `name`, `originalPath`, `deletedAt`). One folder per item, so items never collide, a trashed notebook has no sibling to be taken for a conflicted copy, and nothing is renamed on the way in. Open tabs that show the item are closed first so they flush. A non-empty folder can now be deleted: it moves whole. Restore moves the item back to `originalPath`, creating missing parent folders, or to the same name with " 2", " 3"... when the path (compared without regard to case) is taken (`restorePath`). Permanent deletion and Empty trash ask for confirmation and go through `fileManager.trashFile`, so Obsidian's own *Deleted files* setting decides what happens to them. A meta file that cannot be read, or an item that is not where the meta says, is left alone and not listed. Setting `trashRetentionDays` (default 0 = never) empties items older than that many days when Obsidian starts (`expiredIds`). The trash view is a dialog opened from the *Trash* entry of the library sidebar.
- **Assets.** Nothing deletes files in `_assets/` today (there is no library check yet, #27), so assets used only by trashed notebooks or deleted pages stay. When a clean-up of `_assets/` is written it must treat the notebooks in `_trash/items/` and the assets listed in `_trash/pages/` (`trashedAssetIds`) as references.
- **Page trash** (`model/page-trash.ts` pure, `storage/page-trash-store.ts`, `view/page-tools.ts`). *Delete page* first writes the page to its own file, `_trash/pages/<notebook id>/<entry id>.json`: `{ format: "obsidian-notebook-page-trash-entry", version: 1, notebookId, entry: { id, deletedAt, index, afterPageId, document } }`, where `document` is a one-page notebook document (validated and migrated by the normal reader) carrying the asset records the page needs and, when the page was bookmarked, its bookmark and v7 bookmark details in `meta`; the asset files stay in `_assets/` and are referenced by id. The page is deleted only once that file is written; if the copy fails, the page stays and a notice says so. An entry file is created once and never modified (sync safety: two devices that delete pages before syncing never write the same file, and a conflicted copy of an entry file is just read as the same entry, matched by id). Files that cannot be read are skipped and never overwritten, moved or deleted. Files of the first layout (`_trash/pages/<notebook id>.json` with every entry, `format: "obsidian-notebook-page-trash"`, and conflicted copies of it) are still read and moved over lazily: each entry gets its own file, and the old file is deleted only once every entry in it reads back from an entry file. Retention: the 50 most recent pages per notebook, none older than 30 days, applied on each deletion by deleting entry files (`PAGE_TRASH_MAX_ENTRIES`, `PAGE_TRASH_RETENTION_DAYS`); deleting the same page again drops its older entry. *Deleted pages...* in the page menu lists the entries whose page is not in the notebook (so an undone delete does not show up) with thumbnails; *Restore* is one undo step (asset records added back, the bookmark added back, the page keeps its ids unless the notebook already uses one, and then the bookmark follows the new id) and inserts after the page that preceded it if that page still exists, else at the old position clamped. A restored entry is discarded (its file deleted) once the notebook is saved with the page in it, so it cannot be restored twice; if the save fails the entry stays.
- **Templates** (`model/templates.ts` pure, `storage/templates-store.ts`, `view/page-tools.ts`). A template is a normal one-page notebook file in the templates folder (setting `templatesFolder`, default `<notebooks folder>/_templates`): content, paper, size and background of the saved page, with new ids. *Save page as template...* writes it (validated); *New page from template...* (page menu) and the template row of the *New notebook* dialog open a gallery with thumbnails of the built-in templates (Cornell, meeting notes, to-do list; generated in code, translated labels passed in) and of the stored ones (which can be deleted from the gallery, through Obsidian's trash). Using a template always copies the page with fresh ids; a new page is one undo step.


### Procedural subject art (wave 0.12; DESIGN.md §2.3, §2.6)

`src/ui/art/` draws the line art of subject cards, notebook covers, header bands and empty states, and `src/ui/palette.ts` holds the subject palette. Neither imports `obsidian`; everything but the image cache is pure and tested in Node with a recording 2D context (`tests/generative-art.test.ts`).

- **Determinism.** `rngFor(seed)` is mulberry32 seeded by a 32-bit FNV-1a hash (with murmur3's final mix) of `style|seed` (`art/prng.ts`). The same `ArtSpec` gives the same drawing; the colour changes only the paint, never the geometry. `artStyleFor(name)` and `colorFor(name)` hash the trimmed, lower-cased name into `ART_STYLES` and `SUBJECT_COLORS`: both lists are append-only, or every user's art and colours would change.
- **Families** (`art/families.ts`): `branches` (recursive tapering vessels, mirrored or around an organ-like node), `cells` (Voronoi by half-plane clipping on a jittered grid, rounded double outlines), `waves` (Gaussian and sine ribbons over a fine grid), `contours` (marching squares on fractal value noise), `flow` (evenly spaced streamlines of a noise field), `orbits` (ellipses, bodies, a dial), `grid` (graph paper, axes, plotted curves, formula-like strokes). Lines are 0.5–1.3 px at three opacity levels, batched into a few paths per level; "glow" is one wider, faint pass in the subject colour on a few key paths (never `shadowBlur`, which is slow on Android).
- **Masks without compositing.** The card's right-to-left fade (and the empty state's radial fade) lives in the stroke paint, a gradient, so `drawArt` touches nothing else on the caller's canvas. A soft radial glow in the subject colour is filled behind the lines. Narrow cards (< 480 px) start the art further right to keep the title clear.
- **Outputs.** `drawArt` (cards, the shared contract); `artImage` (cached PNG data URL; with `intensity` ≈ 0.2 it makes the faint 1200 × 160 header bands); `coverImage` (portrait: diagonal gradient, wave bands, a blob, a faint art layer, a spine); `emptyStateImage` (about 240 × 160, centred); `folderIconSvg` (inline SVG, gradient ids scoped by colour so icons on one page never clash). `ArtSpec.surface: "light"` draws deeper lines for light themes. `intensity` and `surface` are optional additions to the contract.
- **Cache.** Images are rendered on one reused scratch canvas (released after each `toDataURL`) and kept in an LRU of 40 entries keyed by kind, style, colour, density, intensity, surface, size, device-pixel ratio (clamped to 1–3) and the switch state. Nothing is drawn per frame.
- **Switch.** `artEnabled()` is false when the system asks for reduced motion or after `setArtEnabled(false)` (for a future *Reduce motion and effects* setting): `drawArt` draws nothing, `artImage` and `emptyStateImage` return "" (callers show no image), `coverImage` keeps its gradient without line art.
- **Cost.** In the harness (headless Chrome, software raster, desktop CPU), one 600 × 200 card at dpr 2 takes 10–21 ms per family including raster; the art's own JavaScript is under 5 ms. Not measured on an Android tablet yet (testing step 429).

**Wave 0.13: a more advanced engine, same public API.** `artImage`, `drawArt`, `coverImage`, `emptyStateImage`, `folderIconSvg`, `artEnabled`, `artStyleFor` and `ArtSpec` are unchanged for callers; the additions are optional fields (`title`, `growth`, and `style: "veins"`) and extra exports.

- **Space colonization (`art/veins.ts`).** The eighth family, `veins`: attractor points are scattered in a region (a leaf outline with a midrib, a river delta rising from the bottom edge, or a radial disc); every vein node is pulled towards the mean direction of the attractors it is nearest to, grows one step, and an attractor dies when a node comes close. The result is drawn as a tree in four weights. Deterministic from the seed; capped at 2600 nodes, 170 iterations and 1150 attractors, with incremental nearest-node bookkeeping, so a card costs a few ms of JavaScript. Algorithm credits: docs/research.md.
- **OKLCH colour (`art/oklch.ts`, `art/art-colour.ts`, `palette.ts`).** sRGB to OKLab to OKLCH and back (the matrices published by Ottosson), a hue-shortest-arc mix, gamut mapping that lowers chroma but keeps lightness and hue. `lighten`, `darken`, the glow colour (brighter, a little more saturated, hue nudged; deeper on light surfaces), the cover gradient and `rampFor(base, steps)` (a perceptual dark-to-light ramp for tints) all work in OKLCH, so a tint of a blue stays blue and a shade does not go muddy.
- **Multi-pass glow (`art/painter.ts`).** A glow path is stroked up to three times: a very wide, very faint halo (the soft blur pass), a tighter glow, then the crisp fine line. Wide round-capped strokes stand in for a blur: no `shadowBlur` and no `filter`, which are slow in Android WebViews. The halo is skipped on wide surfaces (more than 300 000 CSS px², i.e. header bands) and on the faint companion layers.
- **Keyword motifs (`art/keywords.ts`).** A fixed, ordered table of topics (mathematics, biology and medicine, physics, chemistry, history and geography, computing, languages, arts, economics, law, earth sciences, philosophy), each with word stems in English and Italian and the families it evokes. The title's words are normalised (case, accents), the first word that matches a topic decides, and a hash of the title picks among the topic's families, so it is stable but varied. No match: the old hash of the name. `artStyleFor` never returns `veins` (the document format stores seven styles); `artFamilyFor` may, and is the default of an `ArtSpec` without `style`. The table is append-only like the style list: changing a rule changes the art of every notebook it matches.
- **Growth (`ArtSpec.growth`, `growthForPages`).** 0..1, quantised to quarters (the cache key and the picture only change at those steps). Each step adds up to 0.3 of density; from one half a faint companion layer of another family is drawn over the art, and from 1 a second one. `growthForPages(n)` gives 0 for none, about 0.2 at 10 pages, 0.6 at 40, 0.9 at 100. The default is 0, so existing art is unchanged.
- **Three cache levels.** (1) The in-memory LRU of 40 pictures (the synchronous API). (2) A persistent cache in IndexedDB (`art/persist.ts`, database `notebook-art-cache`, at most 160 pictures; keys carry `ART_ENGINE_VERSION`, so a changed drawing never shows an old picture): every picture is written a moment after it is made, and the first image request of a session warms the memory cache with the most recently used ones (`warmArtCache`) without evicting anything. Every IndexedDB call is in try/catch and has a timeout: without IndexedDB (private mode, a locked-down WebView) nothing changes. (3) Rendering: `artImageAsync`, `coverImageAsync`, `emptyStateImageAsync` and `prewarmArt` never block the UI. The drawing is recorded on the calling thread into a compact list of canvas operations (`art/record.ts`, a few ms), then rasterised and PNG-encoded in an inline Blob Worker on an OffscreenCanvas (`art/render-worker.ts`; no extra runtime file; `replayOps` and `workerMain` are self-contained functions whose source text goes into the worker). One picture at a time; concurrent requests for the same picture share one job. Where Worker, Blob, `URL.createObjectURL` or OffscreenCanvas are missing, or the worker errors, times out (6 s) or fails twice, the picture is drawn on the main thread in an idle moment. The worker is closed after 20 s idle. Nothing is drawn per frame.
- **Guard.** The 0.12.3 guard covers the new paths too: a failure drops the picture and is logged once, and after three failures the engine returns "" for the session. Elements are made with the global `createEl`, never on a Document.
- **Using it from a surface.** The synchronous functions are unchanged and draw a miss on the main thread (then write it to IndexedDB); a surface that wants the worker calls `prewarmArt([...])` or `artImageAsync` and assigns the result when it resolves. As of this wave the library, the subject card and the creation dialog still call the synchronous functions: they benefit from the persistent cache and the new families, not yet from the worker.
- **Cost and checks.** `tests/generative-art.test.ts` keeps the 30 ms budget for a 600 × 200 card at dpr 2. The harness page `art.html` (`?style=<family>|growth|mix`) shows every family, the growth steps and the keyword motifs and checks determinism, growth, the pipeline and timing (`HARNESS_DOC=art`). Not measured on an Android tablet yet (testing steps 560-568).

### Deleting files (Scorecard lint)

Rule: whatever is the user's (a notebook, a folder, a note, an attachment, a template, a stamp, a version-history snapshot, an item of the library trash) is removed only through `app.fileManager.trashFile`, so Obsidian's *Deleted files* setting decides what happens to it; moves into the plugin trash use `vault.rename`. Only the plugin's own bookkeeping files are deleted straight away, through `deletePluginFile` (`storage/plugin-files.ts`), which does exactly what `Vault.delete` did before; moving them to the user's trash would fill it with one file per opened notebook or restored page. No call was made more permanent than before. Every call site (`eslint-plugin-obsidianmd` reports `Vault.delete` / `Vault.trash` as `prefer-file-manager-trash-file`, and its config forbids disabling that rule):

| Call site | Target | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| `storage/presence.ts` `startup()` | this device's stale presence files in `_meta` (the app was killed while a notebook was open); other devices' files are never touched | plugin-internal: `deletePluginFile` |
| `storage/presence.ts` `remove()` | this device's presence file when the notebook closes | plugin-internal: `deletePluginFile` |
| `storage/page-trash-store.ts` `migrate()` | first-layout page-trash file, only once every entry in it reads back from its own entry file | plugin-internal (its content is kept in the entry files): `deletePluginFile` |
| `storage/page-trash-store.ts` `deleteEntries()` | page-trash entry files: after the restored page was saved in the notebook, and by the retention limits (50 pages, 30 days) | plugin-internal records of the plugin's own trash, as before: `deletePluginFile` |
| `storage/trash.ts` `trash()`, failed move (×2) | the meta file written a moment before and its item folder, only when empty; the user's item never moved | plugin-internal: `deletePluginFile` |
| `storage/trash.ts` `restore()` | the item folder once it holds only `meta.json` (the item was moved back) | plugin-internal: `deletePluginFile` |
| `storage/trash.ts` `deletePermanently()` / `empty()`, `integration/history-service.ts`, `library/library-view.ts`, `storage/stamps.ts`, `view/page-tools.ts` | user content | already `fileManager.trashFile`, unchanged |
| `storage/vault-io.ts` (journal slots, space probe) | files in the plugin's config folder, through the adapter | unchanged (not `Vault.delete`) |

## 10. PDFs, images and assets

- **Assets**: PDFs and images are copied into `<notebooks folder>/_assets/<id>.<ext>`, where `id` is the first 24 hex characters of the SHA-256 of the content (`pdf/hash.ts`). The same file imported twice is stored once. The notebook only holds `assets[id] = { id, kind, path, name, bytes }`. Saving a notebook with a long PDF writes only ink. `resolveAssetFile` finds the file by the stored path, then by the content-addressed name in the current `_assets` folder. Missing assets give a placeholder (file name) and the ink is kept; the document is not changed. The save check verifies that every page background and image element refers to an asset of the right kind, and the stored asset is checked when added.
- **PDF engine** (`pdf/pdf-store.ts`, pure with injected `loadPdfJs`, `readAsset` and `createCanvas`): pdf.js comes from Obsidian's `loadPdfJs()`, no bundled copy. At most 3 documents stay open (LRU, destroyed on eviction, never while busy); rendered bitmaps live in an LRU bounded by a memory budget (160 MB by default; the most recent one is always kept); the scale is clamped so neither side exceeds 4096 px; renders are cancellable; while zooming, the previous bitmap is scaled until the sharp one arrives (`peek`).
- **Import as notebook** (`library/import-pdf.ts`): reads the page sizes, builds one page per PDF page (page size = PDF page size, `background: { kind: "pdf", asset, page }`, no paper pattern), writes the notebook and opens it; a PDF of 1 MB or more shows a progress notice while its pages are measured (`LARGE_PDF_BYTES`). **Insert** (`InsertPages`) adds a range or the whole file after the current page; a blank page or an image page can be inserted anywhere.
- **Lecture pack (#191, #192, #193)**: `model/lecture-pack.ts` turns slide sizes into a page plan with no format change. Layout `slide` = one page per slide at the PDF size on blank paper (as *Import PDF* always did); `notes-right` / `notes-bottom` = one slide per page, the slide scaled so that its longer side is 640 pt (the handwriting scale is then the same for every course), the page grown by a right margin of 0.6 × the slide width or a bottom margin of 0.8 × the slide height (`PageMargins`, v6) and given the chosen paper, which shows in the margin because the background covers only its box. *Notes page after every slide* adds a blank page with the same size (margins included) and the chosen paper after each slide page. `library/lecture-pack.ts` builds the notebook (a header page copying the size and paper of the first slide page, or a header text box in the notes area of the first slide page, or no header; the header text is the one of *New lecture*) and runs the flow with an injected host: pick the PDF (vault or device) → dialog (course, date, header, layout) → store the PDF as a content-addressed asset → page sizes → `serializeChecked` → create in the course folder → open. *New lecture from slides…* is a command (folder: that of the notebook in view, else the notebooks folder), a library button and a folder menu entry (that folder). *Insert PDF* in the page menu asks the same layout questions after the page range, builds the pages with the same function and inserts them as one `Batch` (`AddAsset` + `InsertPages`). *Exercise page here* (`exercisePage`, page menu / toolbar … menu / command) inserts a blank page of the full size of the current page with the paper shared with *notes page after every slide* (one `AddPage`). The last layout is remembered on the device (Obsidian local storage, never in the vault). **Two or three slides per page need a format change and are not implemented**: a page has exactly one `background` (one PDF page). Drawing the extra slides as image elements would rasterise them (blurry when zoomed, no text for search, re-publishing or annotation export), so the proper fix is a page field with several backgrounds, each with its own box (a v7 candidate; the margins already provide the notes area).
- **Export** is described in §8: backgrounds and images become JPEG (PDF pages at 200 dpi). A page with margins is exported at its full size with the PDF in its box (`backgroundRect`), like the screen and the thumbnails.
- **Margins (#104)**: page menu *Page margins…* (or the command) on a page with a background asks left, right and bottom in mm (a right margin of half the PDF width is suggested), optionally for every page of the same PDF, and applies `SetPageMargins` as one undo step. Format: §2 (v6).
- **Re-published slides (#235)**: page menu *Update PDF from a new version…* (or the command). The chosen file is read in a temporary `PdfStore` (it is stored in `_assets` only when the update is applied). Each page of the new PDF and each distinct PDF page of the notebook gets a fingerprint (`pdf/republish.ts`): its pdf.js text, normalised; pages with fewer than 24 characters of text also get a 64-bit difference hash of a ~72 px rendering. Matching: (1) equal fingerprints are the same slide, *unchanged*, one to one, closest page number first, whatever the order; (2) "that PDF" is the asset of the page in view plus every asset with at least 3 unchanged pages or more than half of its slides unchanged (one shared "Thank you / Questions" slide does not pull in another lecture), and step 1 is repeated with those slides only; (3) remaining new pages are *updated* versions of remaining slides of that PDF when their words overlap enough (Dice ≥ 0.5 after removing words found on more than half of the pages, such as footers) or their image hashes differ by at most 10 bits; (4) the rest is *new*. A summary (new, updated, unchanged, not in the new version) lets the user switch each updated slide or keep the old one; each updated slide names the PDF it comes from, and only slides of the main asset (the one in view) start switched on. Applying is one `Batch`: `AddAsset`, `InsertPages` of the new pages after the last page of that PDF (and the blank note pages after it), with that page's margins, and `SetPageBackground` for every notebook page that shows an accepted updated slide (an infinite page never gets shorter: with margins the extra height goes into the bottom margin, `SetPageMargins`). Unchanged pages keep pointing at the old asset; old assets are never removed.
- **Synchronised PDF ↔ notes view (#106)**: command *Open PDF side by side* (and the same entry in the "…" page menu; *Close*, *Link this page to the slide in view*, *Add notes page for this slide* once open). Use cases: (a) a plain `.pdf` of the vault, read-only, rendered by a separate `PdfStore` (not stored in `_assets`); (b) another notebook, shown page by page with its PDF backgrounds and ink, read-only. **Split: inside the notebook view, not a second Obsidian leaf.** Obsidian on phones and tablets has no split panes in the main area (a split opens a tab), so only an internal split works on every device, and the controller can talk to both sides directly. The pane is inserted into `.notebook-body` before the page navigator and the stage; the stage keeps its own resize handling, so the notes refit as the divider moves. Layout (`pdf/sync-layout.ts`): side by side when the view is at least 700 px wide and clearly landscape (tablet held sideways, desktop), stacked otherwise (phone, tablet upright); a draggable divider keeps 20-80 %. The pane shows one reference page at a time, fitted to its width (scrolls if taller), so it does not depend on the notes view's scroll mode: the notes side follows through the view's current page whichever way it scrolls.
  - **Notes ↔ slide link, format v12 (#226)**: ink written while a PDF page is in the pane remembers that slide. The document holds one small table, `slides: [{ id, source, page, path? }]` (written after `position`, one entry per line), and each linked stroke names its entry: `"slide": "<id>"`, written between `hidden` and `points`. `source` is the PDF's content id (as in the links sidecar and the asset ids), `page` 1-based, `path` the file the pane showed (a plain PDF, or the notebook whose page has that PDF page as background) so the slide can be opened again; ids are 8 base-36 characters, unique in the document. One entry per slide shared by all its ink, so a stroke pays about 20 characters, not a PDF id and a path. **Why in the file, not a sidecar:** the link belongs to the ink (it follows copy, duplicate, cross-page moves, the eraser's pieces, restyling and sync of the notebook) and has to undo with it. The view links strokes at commit (`addStrokes` → `view/slide-ink.ts` `linkToSlide`, setting *Link notes to slides*): copies that carry the id, plus `AddSlideRef` in the same `Batch` when the table has no entry for that slide yet, so undo removes ink and entry together and redo restores both; the renderer still receives the objects it drew (the wet-ink handover is by identity; the geometry is the same), so the stroke pipeline and renderer are untouched. **Writer rules:** only entries some stroke names are written (in table order); a stroke whose `slide` names no entry (ink pasted from another notebook) is written without it (a cached frozen copy), so the strict re-check never refuses such a document; large documents check the written entries on their own (the skeleton check has no strokes). **Reader rules:** lenient read drops and counts an invalid entry, a duplicate id, a table that is not an array, and a stroke `slide` that names no entry (the ink stays). **Migration v11 → v12:** drops a `slides` key and stroke `slide` keys found in a v11 file (a v11 reader ignored unknown fields, so they were never ours); large v11 files are still staged (`StagedParse` ignores the stroke keys page by page). **Older readers:** 0.13 and earlier read up to v11, so a v12 file is a "newer version": it opens read-only with the usual message and is never overwritten (rule 4); nothing of v12 is visible to them. Copies (`model/pages.ts copyStroke`) keep the field; a conflicted-copy merge maps the copy's ids into the main table (`slideMerger`: same entry, an equal entry under another id, or a new entry, under a fresh id when the id is taken). **Repair** (`model/repair.ts`) salvages the table with the lenient reader (a damaged file also contributes its entry lines) and keeps each stroke's link when it names a salvaged entry; only a dangling link is dropped (the ink stays and counts as repaired), and `slides` is a known field. **Bounds:** `linkStrokes` leaves an over-long `path` out of a new entry and does not link at all when `source` is too long (the strict writer would otherwise fail every save); `checkContent` checks the table entries like the writer, so an edit that would add such an entry is refused at once. **Page trash:** a trash entry carries the slide entries its ink names (its one-page document has its own table); *Restore* maps them into the notebook with `slideMerger` (the same or an equal entry, or the entry added) and adds the missing ones with `AddSlideRef` in the same `Batch` as the restore, so the badges come back and undo removes both. **Dropped on purpose:** *Move to notebook…* (`library/page-move.ts`) and page templates (the template file has no table) leave the ink unlinked, because the other notebook has no such entry; the ink itself is kept. **Showing the slide again:** a small badge next to each group of linked ink (`slideInkGroups`, strokes within 48 pt of each other; DOM over the stage like the textbook references, following the viewport through the overlay listeners, groups recomputed only when a page's strokes change by identity), tapped: the pane shows that slide (opening the file from `path` when the pane is closed or shows another file; a slide that is a page of this notebook is shown there). *Show slide* (side-pane bar) prefers the slide of the selected ink, then the page link, then the slide most of the page's ink was written beside. Tests: `tests/model-v12.test.ts`.
  - **Drawings with states, format v13 (#241)**: a page may hold `states`: `{ parts: [{ id, name?, pivot: [x, y], items: [strokeId...] }], list: [{ id, name, poses: [{ part, dx?, dy?, rot?, scale?, hidden?, color? }] }], active? }`, written on **one line** of the page (after `infinite`, before `elements`) so the repair line scan finds it. A *part* groups strokes of the page (ink only: elements have no rotation in the model and stay out) and keeps a fixed `pivot` (the centre of its bounds when it was made) that rotation and scale turn around; a stroke is in at most one part. A *state* names a pose per part: shift `dx`/`dy` in pt, `rot` in degrees, `scale` (line width scales with it), `hidden`, `color`, each written only when it differs from the drawn part, so a part without a pose is where it was drawn. *As drawn* is not an entry of `list`; `active` is the state the page shows (absent: as drawn), saved so that other pages, exports and the next opening agree. **Why a page table of stroke ids, not a stroke field:** a part is a set of strokes that exist already (transforms keep stroke ids: `transformStroke` keeps the id), the poses refer to parts, and one line per page keeps the stroke lines identical to v12. **The transform is never applied to the ink:** `model/states.ts` `stateView` builds transformed copies of the member strokes (cached per stroke and pose, so the renderer's path cache keeps working) and `liveStateView` wraps a page so that everything but `strokes` is the page's own and `strokes` follow the page as it changes; the view hands `viewPages()` to the renderer (`setDocument`), the exports and the thumbnails instead of `doc.pages` (the same array when no page has states: no cost for notebooks without them), so the stroke engine and the renderer are untouched. **Everything that draws the page uses the state in view:** the renderer, thumbnails, PDF / PNG / SVG **and Markdown** exports (`renderPagesOf`), and the live pictures of excerpts of the notebook's own pages (`NotebookMedia` takes the same page view for the ink of the region; sizes, background and elements are the page's own). `liveStateView` re-checks that the page's strokes are the ones it derived from on every read (an O(n) identity comparison, far cheaper than drawing the page, kept exact because commands edit `page.strokes` in place and there is no single revision to key on) and hands back the same array while nothing changed. **Editing:** one user action is one `SetPageStates(pageId, before, after)` (undo restores the previous table and the state shown then); **the state shown is the view's own choice and is never written by looking:** tapping a chip, stepping and playing set the view's `ShownStates` entry for the page (per view, per device; a choice that names a state the page no longer has falls back to the saved one) and write nothing, so two devices that only look make no writes and no conflicted copies. The saved `active` is the state a view starts with (and what other readers use); it changes only through the explicit *Set as default state* (the options menu of the strip; also for *As drawn*), one `SetPageStates` and one undo step. *Add state* and *Duplicate state* are edits (one undo step) and show the new state in this view without changing the saved default. **Erasing:** the segment eraser and the area eraser cut a stroke into pieces with new ids; the view adds the `SetPageStates` that `states-erase.ts` `statesAfterCuts` computes from the returned `ReplaceStroke`s (old id replaced by its pieces in the part, in order, followed through repeated cuts) to the same `Batch`, so the pieces stay in their part and one gesture is one undo step; a stroke erased whole stays in its part's list (dropped at write, restored by undo). The erasers themselves (`src/ink/`) are unchanged. **Margins:** `SetPageMargins` moves the ink horizontally, and now the parts' pivots with it (`shiftPivots`), so a rotated or scaled part keeps turning about its own centre; undo puts back the same table object. The part editor selects the part's strokes *as the state shows them* (the lasso's own selection and handles) and `commitTransform` turns the dragged matrix, composed with the current pose, into the new pose (`poseFromMatrix` inverts `poseMatrix`) instead of rewriting the strokes. The ink tools (`inkInView`) skip the strokes a shown state has moved (`displacedStrokeIds`): a hit at the drawn spot would hit nothing the reader sees. **Writer rules** (`states-format.ts normalizePageStates`): items that are not strokes of the page leave their part (erased strokes are only dropped at write, so undo can bring them back), a part without a stroke goes with its poses, identity poses go, names are trimmed to 60 characters, numbers are rounded and bounded, an `active` that names no state is dropped, nothing is written when no part and no state is left; limits 64 parts, 32 states, 5000 items per part, ids at most 16 characters. `checkContent` checks the table as the writer would, and large documents check it per page on its own (the skeleton check has no strokes). **Reader rules** (`serialize.ts readPageStates`): read after the page's ink; lenient read drops and counts an item that names no stroke of the page or is already in another part, a part left without items, a pose of an unknown part or with an out-of-range number, an invalid state, a duplicate id and an `active` that names no state; the drawing is never dropped for it. **Migration v12 to v13:** drops a `states` key found in a v12 page (a v12 reader ignored unknown page fields, so it was never ours); large v12 files are still staged (`StagedParse` ignores the key page by page, and its document-level drops no longer run for a v12 file). **Older readers:** 0.14 reads up to v12, so a v13 file is a "newer version": it opens read-only with the usual message and is never overwritten (rule 4). **Copies:** `duplicatePage` (also *Move to notebook*) and a page-version restore carry the table to the new stroke ids (`remapStates`); page trash carries it in its one-page document; a conflicted-copy merge brings the table of a page only the copy has (under the new ids) and gives a page of the main notebook that has none the copy's, mapped to the strokes that are there (shifted with them when the page was re-framed); when both have a table the main notebook's stays. **Repair** keeps the table with the lenient reader against the salvaged strokes (a part keeps the strokes that survived; an invalid table is reset and counted).
