Tests: `tests/model-v13.test.ts`; harness `dev/harness/states-check.ts`.
  - **Locked pages, format v14 (#124)**: a page may hold `"locked": true`, on its own line after `infinite` and before `states` (a repair line scan finds it); it is **never written as `false`**: absent = unlocked. Design choice: a plain flag, not `{at}`, because the file has no use for the time (the format keeps no history of unlocks) and one boolean needs no merge rule beyond "locked on either side stays locked". A lock refuses every edit of the page until it is unlocked; reading, navigation (PDF and slides too), export, copy-out of a selection, search and organisation (tags, bookmarks, page order, adding pages around it) still work. **One guard, where commands run** (`model/history.ts` + `model/page-lock.ts`): `History.execute`, `undo` and `redo` take a `LockSnapshot` of the locked pages (their ink and elements by reference, their size / paper / background / margins / infinite flag, the `states` object; nothing is taken when no page is locked), run the command, and compare. A command that changed the content of a page that was locked before it ran is reverted at once, not recorded, and reported to the view (`pagesLocked(ids)`: the notice *Page locked · Unlock*). Nothing is listed per command, so a command added later is covered; a `Batch` is refused as a whole; a batch that unlocks and edits in one step is refused too (the lock state is read from before). The flag itself is not content: `SetPageLocked` (one undo step; a multi-page selection is one `Batch`) goes through, so do tags, bookmarks, moving pages and inserting pages. The view adds gates **before** a gesture starts, so nothing is drawn and then thrown away: the pen / highlighter / shape / text / space tools and a lasso drag on a locked page return without starting a contact (`drawStart`), the stroke eraser skips it (`eraseTo`); the lasso itself still selects (to copy out). Flows that act outside the history check first: moving pages to another notebook and deleting through the page trash refuse a locked page (a copy would be left behind), a version restore *over* a locked page is refused (*insert* next to it works), find and replace leaves text on locked pages as it is and says so, and `merge.ts` wraps its result in `exemptFromLocks` so the ink of a conflicted copy still reaches a locked page (nothing is lost because a page was locked on one device); a page locked in either copy is locked in the result. A duplicated page, a notebook made from a notebook or a template, and a restore under an id clash start unlocked; a page trash entry keeps the lock it had. The migration v13 → v14 only drops a stray `locked` key (a v13 reader never locked anything), so large v13 files still stage page by page; 0.15 and older read a v14 file as a newer format: read-only, never overwritten (checked against the 0.15.0 code with a real v14 file). Read leniently an invalid `locked` is dropped and counted (the page is unlocked); written strictly it must be `true`. The UI: the page sheet and the multi-selection menu (*Lock page* / *Unlock page*), the command *Lock or unlock the page in view*, a lock beside the page number, a badge over the page (a tap unlocks), a lock on the page panel thumbnail and on the notebook map card (with a lock button), and the notice's *Unlock* button. **Lock guard details (review fixes)**: the snapshot copies the stroke and element arrays (they are spliced in place; the items are immutable, so a reference per item is enough) and keeps the small shape fields as plain values; the background and margins are replaced as a whole by their commands, so they are compared by reference and only by text when the reference differs (an equal replacement is not a change). `lockedChanges` is one pass over the pages with a map lookup per page. A page that is gone counts as changed (deleting a finished page is an edit), except in **undo**: undoing a command removes the pages that command added, so undoing a page-trash restore that brought back a locked page works (back to the trash state) and is never stuck behind the lock. An exempt command keeps its content visible to the library check and the storage dialog (they walk the history's objects, not closures): `exemptFromLocks` keeps the original as the plain field `inner`, and `Batch` carries `lockExempt` itself. **Maintenance is exempt on purpose**: the storage manager's picture repoint (`planAssetRepoint`, used for merging duplicates and downscaling) swaps the file behind a picture without changing what the page shows, so it is a `Batch` with `lockExempt` and also works on a closed notebook (`repointInText` applies the commands directly). The public API refuses a locked page with the error `page-locked`: `insertImage` checks before the image file is stored, `planClosedEdit` compares the locked pages before and after any planned edit of a closed notebook (the same rule as the history), and an open notebook's refused `execute` is reported as `page-locked` (or `write-failed` when nothing is locked). `EditContext.execute` returns whether the command was recorded, so the flows that report success (moving pages out, the storage repoint, the board shot) no longer report it for a refused command; "Move pages out" leaves the copies in both notebooks and says so.
  - **Sticky notes, format v15 (#20)**: a page element `{"id","type":"sticky","x","y","width","height","color","fontSize"[,"text"][,"collapsed":true,"ink":[…]]}`, one line, in `elements` like the others (so copy, paste, layers, merge, repair, the clipboard and the history already know how to carry it). The box is 48 to 900 pt a side, `color` is a `#rrggbb` from the subject palette (the paper is a light tint of it, drawn by `ink/sticky-render.ts`), `fontSize` 5 to 72 pt, `text` at most 2000 characters (typed text; handwriting is ink). **Ink belongs to the note without a stored link**: it is the existing geometric rule of ink on objects (`ink/attach.ts`, `ownsInk`: at least 60 % of a stroke's points on the note), so ink written on a note is ordinary vector ink of the page (points in page coordinates, erased, selected, exported and undone like any other) and moves and scales with the note through the same selection transform (`transformElement` maps the frame and the text size; a note never rotates or flips, like a box, and stays within 48 to 900 pt). Notes are drawn after boxes and before equations and text boxes, always under the ink (`layers.ts` band 2.5). **Collapsed**: `collapsed: true` draws a 28 pt icon at the note's top-left corner (the frame is kept, so opening restores the note) and hides its ink. Because the empty place of a collapsed note must not swallow what is written there later, the note stores the ids of the strokes that were on it at collapse time in `ink` (written only while collapsed): the renderer, the exports and the thumbnails skip exactly those strokes (`hiddenStickyInk`), they move with the icon (`linkedInkIds` in `inkOwner` / `attachedInk`, also with the setting "Ink follows objects" off, like ink bound to a graph), the eraser and the lasso do not see them (`inkInView`) and deleting a collapsed note deletes them in the same undo step. Opening the note clears the list. Ids of strokes that are gone are dropped on read; copy and merge remap the list to the new stroke ids. Read: the frame, colour and size are required (an invalid note is dropped and counted when lenient, like other elements); text, flag and list are details dropped on their own. **Migration v14 → v15** only refuses a `sticky` element in a v14 file (no version of the plugin wrote one; `v15OnlyElement`, checked page by page so large v14 files still stage); 0.16 and older read a v15 file as a newer format: read-only, never overwritten. The history guard of locked pages covers notes with no extra code (every edit is a command). Exports: PNG, PDF (solid shapes and Helvetica text; the translucent shadow is left out) and SVG draw the note and its text, and Markdown writes the typed text as a block quote in reading order (`pageTexts`); a collapsed note exports as its icon without its ink. The view side is `view/sticky-tools.ts` (insert, edit, collapse / open as one undo step each) and `view/sticky-modal.ts` (the editor on `modal-shell`: text, the eight palette colours, text size); the registry has `insert-sticky` (quick slots, sheet, command) and, on the selection bar, `sticky-edit`, `sticky-collapse` and `sticky-expand`. Moving, resizing, copying and deleting are the ordinary selection tools.
  - **Margin comments, format v16 (#86)**: a page may hold `comments`, a flat list written **one item per line** (four tabs, like elements) in a `"comments": [ … ],` block after `states` and before `elements`. Two kinds of item share the list. A **comment** `{"id","region":{"x","y","width","height"},"text","author","created"[,"sketch":{"strokes":[[x0,y0,x1,y1,…],…]}][,"edited"][,"resolved":true]}` is anchored to a rectangle of the page (page coordinates, pt, rounded to 0.1, at least 4 pt a side, inside the page). A **reply** `{"id","parent","text","author","created"[,"edited"]}` names its comment in `parent`; the thread is flat (a reply never has replies; one made to a reply is attached to that reply's comment). `author` is the name of the device that wrote it (the setting *Device name*, else a generated one), `created` / `edited` are times in ms since 1970 (a resolve or an edit sets `edited`, at least one ms after the previous touch, so it wins even with a slow clock). `resolved` is a flag of the comment (never written as `false`). Limits (writer and readers share them, `model/types.ts`): 1000 items a page, 4000 characters of text, 60 of author, ids 40; **ink in a comment** is not page ink: `sketch` is up to 40 strokes of up to 400 points (3000 in all) as flat integer lists in the fixed frame 400 × 200 (drawn in the colour of the text, scaled to fit), kept **inside the comment** because a small sketch beside a note needs none of the page's machinery (no pressure, no erase, no selection), and a comment that owned page strokes (the sticky-note pattern) would have to follow them through every copy, merge and erase. The simpler design is also the safer one: a comment is one self-contained line. **Where the code is**: `model/comments-format.ts` holds the one reader (`readComments`: never throws, returns the valid items and a list of problems), the writer's normaliser (`normalizeComments`: the reader applied to what is in memory, so what is written is what a lenient read would keep) and the line (`commentLine`); `serialize.ts` calls them (`readPageComments`: **lenient** with `drop` set, every problem counted and the page kept; **strict** with `drop` null, the first problem fails, and `serializeChecked` / `checkContent` read each page's comments back strictly and write them again, so an edit that could not be saved is refused at once). `model/comments.ts` holds the editing operations (one `SetPageComments` command replaces the page's list; add, reply, edit, resolve, delete and the thread queries are built on it), the merge and the markers' numbering. **Repair and invalid input** (reader, `repair.ts`): an item that is not an object, has no valid id or time, a repeated id or a bad region / parent is dropped and counted; text and author are cut to their limits; a region outside the page is moved and shrunk into it; a sketch keeps its valid strokes; a reply to a reply goes to the comment of the first; **a reply whose comment is not there (an orphan) becomes a comment of its own in the top-left corner of the page, so no typed text is lost**. The repair line scan (`scanLines`) finds comment lines too (an item with a string `author` and a numeric `created`). **Migration v15 → v16** only drops a stray `comments` key of a page (a v15 reader ignored unknown page fields, so such a key was never ours), so large v15 files still stage page by page (`StagedParse` reads `comments` only from v16); 0.17 and older read a v16 file as a newer format: read-only, never overwritten (the same `future-version` path as every earlier format). **Undo and the lock**: every change is a `SetPageComments` through the history, one undo step; the lock guard (`page-lock.ts`) compares the page's `comments` list by identity (a command always replaces the list, committed lists are frozen), so **a locked page refuses adding, editing, resolving and deleting comments** like every other edit, and a change that arrives through a merge is exempt like all merged content. **Margins**: widening a page's left margin moves its comments with its ink (`SetPageMargins` shifts the regions, undo puts the very same list back); insert-space and split-page do not move comments (a comment is anchored to a place on the page, not to what is written there). **Copies and moves**: a duplicated page, pages moved or copied to another notebook (`planPageMove`, `planTransfer`, `planMerge` of two notebooks) and a page restored from the page trash keep their comments (they travel with the page; comment ids only have to be unique within a page); restoring a version over a page **joins** the comments of the page and the version by id (later edit wins), so restoring never drops a comment written since; a notebook made into a template, or from one, carries none, and neither does a page saved as a template, a page or notebook made from a template, or a practice attempt (review threads are about one page of one notebook). Restoring a version over a page with other margins moves the comments of the page in view by the difference of the left margins, like the ink it replaces. **Clipboard (decision)**: comments do **not** travel with a copied, cut, duplicated or moved selection, nor with *Move selection to page*: a comment is a review note about a place on a page, not content, and a pasted comment would carry a discussion about something that is not there; copy the page to keep its comments. **Merge of a conflicted copy** (`model/merge.ts`, `mergeComments`): items are matched by id; an item only one side has is kept (two devices that added comments or replies both keep theirs), and for an item both have the one touched last wins (`edited`, else `created`; the main notebook on a tie), so edits of one comment on two devices resolve to the later one, and a resolve counts as an edit. **When both devices edited the text (or the sketch) differently, the losing version is not dropped**: it is kept beside the winner as a comment (same region and sketch) or reply (same thread) of its own whose text starts with the language-neutral mark `⚠ `, author and time of the loser, and an id derived from the loser (`<fingerprint of the whole original id>~<touch time in base 36>`, 14 characters of fingerprint so it always fits the 40-character cap without cutting the original id, and the same on every device, so merging the same copy again adds nothing); it is counted in the merge summary and `mergeCovers` looks for it. A copy that never edited the comment (a stale copy) is no conflict: the edited version simply wins. A deliberate restore of a version does not keep such marked copies. No schema field is involved (the v16 comment and reply shapes are unchanged). The copy's regions are clamped to the size the page has after the merge's own growth. The copy's regions are moved with the content when the page had to be re-framed. A page only the copy has comes with its comments. Deletions are not inferred (a comment deleted on one device comes back from a copy that still has it, like erased ink). The merge summary counts the added and replaced items (*n comments or replies added or updated*), `mergeCovers` checks every comment of the copy is in the saved result before the copy is moved away. **UI** (`view/comments-*.ts`): *Comment* on a selection (the selection's box, padded) or *Comment on a region* (a drawn rectangle: a surface over the page takes the one drag, so the ink tools are never reached; the bar has a Cancel button, Escape cancels) opens the editor (`comments-modal.ts`, on the modal shell: text and a sketch pad); the comment appears as a numbered marker in the right margin of its page (`comments-layer.ts`, DOM over the canvas like the zettel regions; only the marker takes taps and keeps its contacts from the pen input; markers are numbered top to bottom and pushed apart; resolved ones are muted and show a check), joined by a thin line to a faint outline of the region. A tap highlights the region and opens the card (`comments-card.ts`: author, time, text, sketch, the flat thread, a reply box, Resolve / Reopen, Edit, Delete; docked at the bottom on a phone). The side panel (`comments-panel.ts`) lists every comment of the notebook with a filter (Open / Resolved / All) and jumps to the region. Everything but writing also works on a read-only notebook or a locked page. Settings: *Show comment markers* (on by default; the opened comment is always shown) and *Include comments in exports* (key `commentsInPdf`, off by default; covers the PDF and the Markdown export). **Exports**: the PDF with the setting on gives each page that has comments a strip of 190 pt on its right (`MediaBox` wider; `export-pdf.ts`): the region is outlined on the page, joined by a thin line to a numbered disc in the strip, and the strip holds the note (author and time, text up to 14 lines, the sketch, up to 5 replies); notes sit level with their region and are pushed down below the previous one, and what does not fit above the bottom edge is counted in a last line (*+n more comments*); a background-only export leaves them out, the automatic PDF copy and the API's PDF are the plain pages. The Markdown export lists a page's comments under its text (*Comments*, a numbered list in marker order with author, time, *resolved*, the text escaped like text boxes, replies as a nested list; only with *Include comments in exports*, like the PDF, because the list names the devices that wrote them). PNG, SVG and printing do not draw comments. The registry has `comment-selection` (selection bar, sheet, command), `comment-region` (sheet, command; the panel has a button too), `comments-panel` (sheet, command, checked) and `comment-next` (command).
  - **Opacity, format v24 (#358)**: one more optional number, `opacity` (0.05 to 1), on a stroke of any pen (written after the shape style, before `points`), on an `image` element (after `rotation`) and on an `area` element (after `color`). **Semantics**: it MULTIPLIES the look the item has without it, so 1 and an absent value are the look of every earlier version and the pens' own translucency stays the default: a ballpoint, a fountain pen, a brush and a shape are opaque, the highlighter is 0.35 multiplied over the page (0.45 screened at night), the marker 0.85, the tint of a fill 0.25, an area the highlighter's look. 0.5 on the highlighter is half of its 0.35, still multiplied. The plugin writes the key only below 1, so a notebook that never uses it is byte for byte what v23 wrote, and the golden render hashes (`tests/pens-golden.test.ts`) do not move. **One description of the look**: `inkAlpha(tool, opacity)` (`ink/stroke-shape.ts`) is the alpha of a committed stroke in the day look (PNG raster, thumbnails, SVG, PDF), `fillAlpha` scales the fill of a shape with it, `drawArea` and `drawElement` (a picture) multiply by `opacityOf`, and the renderer's `setInk` does the same for the static layer, the live layer and the night look. **Wet ink**: the pen's wet canvas holds opaque ink and the highlighter's and the marker's get their translucency from CSS; a stroke with an opacity overrides it with an inline `opacity` on that canvas (the product with the pen's alpha, blend mode kept: overlaps inside one stroke still never darken twice) and the canvas is hidden the moment the static layer shows the stroke, so a translucent stroke never shows twice. **PDF**: one graphics state per distinct alpha used on the page (`alphaState`, `/GO<n>`), with `/BM /Multiply` for the highlighter; the fixed states `GS1`, `GS3` and `GS4` keep serving the items without an opacity. **Making and changing it**: a slider in the pen options (`view/opacity-row.ts`; the pen's value `ToolbarState.penOpacity` also serves the pen kinds and the shapes, `highlighterOpacity` the highlighter and the area highlighter; a favourite pen and a pen set keep it as `FavouritePen.opacity`, like the width) and the same row in the selection bar, which sets the opacity of the selected ink, pictures and areas as ONE `UpdateItems` step titled "Opacity" (`planOpacity` in `ink/restyle.ts`; locked elements and elements that take none are left alone, a locked page refuses it). **Reader**: a v23 to v24 migration that is the identity plus the version (a stray `opacity` key of a v23 file, which a v23 reader ignored, is dropped), so large v23 files are staged too; leniently a number outside 0.05 to 1 is clamped and a value that is not a number is dropped, each counted; strictly the writer refuses both; repair keeps a valid one. Older readers open a v24 file read-only. Joining broken strokes and the hook clean-up treat two strokes of different opacity as different styles.
  - **Area highlighter, format v23 (#99)**: one more element type, `area`: `{"id","type":"area","x","y","width","height","color"}` (one line, then the usual `group` / `locked` / `lockAt`), an axis-aligned translucent rectangle in a highlighter colour (`#rrggbb`), each side 2 to 20000 pt, that never rotates. **Why an element and not a stroke flag** (the choice that disturbs least): the stroke pipeline (input, engine, renderer, the golden hashes of `tests/pens-golden.test.ts`) stays untouched, and nothing that reads strokes as lines of writing (ghost guide lines, scribble to erase, recognition, joining, hook clean-up, the segment eraser, the word and line finder) ever sees a rectangle by mistake. Elements already give, with no new command, selecting, moving, resizing (the selection frame's scale handle; `transformElement` maps the rectangle, each side on its own, and a turn that is not a quarter turn keeps it upright and the same size, moving only its centre, like a text box), deleting, copying, groups, locks (`locked` is element-only), the page lock, the page times (`AddElement` stamps the page) and undo (`AddElement`, `RemoveElement`, `ReplaceElement`, `UpdateItems`, all of which already implement `remap`); the layer is "under the ink" by construction, because every element is drawn before the strokes. **Look**: exactly the highlighter's (opacity `HIGHLIGHTER_ALPHA`, multiplied over the page; at night the screened night highlighter), so a notebook looks the same everywhere and the opacity is not stored (`ink/area-render.ts` is the one description: the canvas, the PNG raster, the thumbnails and the previews call `drawArea`; the SVG writes the same opacity with `mix-blend-mode:multiply`, the PDF the highlighter's graphics state `GS1`). **Order**: after the sticky notes and before the equations and the typed text (`layers.ts` band 2.7), always above the background (PDF page or scan) and below all ink. **Colour meaning**: the "filter by meaning" dimming (`Renderer.setInkDim`, now taking anything with a `color`) dims an area like highlighter ink. **Erasing**: an area is highlighter ink with no stroke to cut, so the whole-stroke and the segment eraser remove it whole as soon as the eraser touches it (`ink/area-erase.ts`; a locked one stays; the selective eraser sees it as a highlighter of its colour, so the "Pen" filter spares it and "Highlighter" erases it), and the area eraser removes the areas whose centre is inside its outline. **Making one**: the highlighter's options have *Stroke | Area* (`ToolbarState.highlighterArea`, also the action `highlighter-area`); in Area mode a drag is a rectangle, drawn from corner to corner and clamped to the page, shown live on the live layer and added as one `AddElement` on pen-up (a drag under 2 pt a side adds nothing). **Recolour**: the selection bar's highlighter colour row (`planRestyle` with the `highlighter` group). **Reader**: a v22 to v23 migration that is the identity plus the version, except that a pre-v23 file with an area is rejected like every element type before it (a v22 reader rejected unknown element types, so no version of this plugin wrote it; `v23OnlyElement` checks it for the chain and for staged loading; the file is left untouched); leniently an area with an invalid rectangle or colour is dropped and counted (like the other details), strictly the writer refuses it (`readArea` is the one reader, so the strict check after a write is the same code); repair salvages a valid area. Older readers open a v23 file read-only (they never rewrite a file of a newer version). Limits live in `model/area.ts`.
  - **Filled shapes and dashed or dotted lines, format v22 (#37)**: three more optional stroke fields, only on `shape` strokes and written after `nib`: `fill` (`#rrggbb`, the colour a CLOSED shape is filled with; an open shape that has one is simply not drawn filled), `fillTint` (`true`: a translucent tint, alpha 0.25, instead of the solid colour; needs `fill`) and `dash` (`"dashed"` or `"dotted"`; absent = solid). A dashed line is the same polyline cut into runs by arc length (a dash is three widths with a gap of three, a dot every two widths, the unit never below 1.5 pt) and each run is its own round-capped outline, so every renderer only asks `ink/shape-style.ts` for the runs; the fill is the polygon through the points, painted under the line. The v21 to v22 migration is the identity plus the version (stray `fill`, `fillTint` and `dash` keys of a v21 file are dropped); lenient reading drops and counts a bad value (the shape is drawn plain), strict writing refuses it; a piece left by cutting a shape (segment eraser) is an open line and keeps only the line style. Freehand ink never has them, and the golden render hashes are unchanged.
  - **Saved views, format v21 (#264)**: one optional notebook-level key, `views`, a list written **one view per line** (two tabs, like `slides`) between `slides` and `assets`, **only when there is at least one**. A view is `{"id","name","page","x","y","width","height","zoom"}`: `page` is a page id, `x y width height` the region of that page that was in view (page coordinates, pt, never screen coordinates, rounded to 0.1 pt) and `zoom` the scale relative to the fit-to-width scale of the page (4 digits), so a view means the same on any screen. Recall (`ink/view-region.ts`) puts the **middle of the region in the middle of the view** at that zoom: exact on the screen it was saved on, the same part of the page, centred, on another. Limits (`types.ts`): at most 100 views, ids unique (at most 64 characters), `name` 1 to 80 characters, trimmed, on one line, single spaces (`normalizeViewName`), `page` at most 200 characters, region numbers finite within +-1e6 with a positive size, zoom 0.01 to 100. **Reader**: leniently an invalid view, a repeated id or one past the limit is dropped and counted in `dropped`, a name that is merely not in its stored form is kept normalised (counted); strictly all of them fail, so a malformed list is refused by `serializeChecked` and by the history guard (`checkContent`). A view of a **page the notebook no longer has is valid and kept** (shown as *Page missing*, it can be removed, and works again when the page returns, e.g. Undo or the trash): nothing is dropped without being asked. **Migration v20 -> v21** is the identity plus the version (a stray `views` key of a v20 file is dropped, like `slides` in v11 -> v12), so large v20 files are still staged page by page; 0.22 and older open a v21 file as a newer format, read-only. **Edits** are one command, `SetViews(before, after)` (`model/views.ts`), that replaces the array (never changes it in place): save, rename, update, reorder, remove and merge are each one undo step, refused by the page-lock and guard rules like any edit; it holds no page, stroke or element, so it has nothing to `remap`. **Copies and moves**: a view names a page by id, so a duplicated page, a page copied or moved to another notebook (merge, split, extract) and a template never take views along; pages that leave a notebook leave their views behind as missing ones. **Merge of a conflicted copy** unites the views by id (the main notebook's wins on a shared id, a name already in use gets a number, only pages the result has, up to the limit; counted as `viewsAdded`, with `viewsDiffer` for a shared id with other contents and `viewsFull` for views that did not fit, which keep the copy in place). **UI**: the *Saved views* dialog (`view/saved-views-modal.ts` on the modal shell: a name field with *Save*, the list with a tap to go, a menu to rename, update to the current view, move up or down and remove) and the commands *Save current view…* and *Go to saved view…* (`view/saved-views.ts`; the sheet, the notebook menu and the palette). Tests: `tests/model-v21.test.ts`, `tests/format-schema.test.ts`; harness `dev/harness/savedviews-check.ts`.
  - **New pens, format v20 (#35)**: three more values of `Stroke.tool` (`fountain`, `brush`, `marker`) and one optional stroke field, `nib` (whole degrees 0 to 179, counter-clockwise on the page, written after `group` and before `points`, **only when set**; absent means 45; only the fountain pen and the marker have one, so it is never stored on anything else). The new pens are **always `render` 2** (wet-ink geometry): there is no perfect-freehand outline for them. **Geometry** (`ink/ink-geometry.ts`): the same disc chain as the pen, with the radius of each stored point worked out once, when the point is added (`InkBuilder.movingRadius`, kept in `InkBuilder.pr`), from its pressure and how the pen has been moving; it depends only on the points up to that one (smoothed direction, smoothed step length, arc length), so the wet ink and the committed stroke are the same chain and nothing on screen changes at pen-up. The old pens keep `inkRadius` (pressure only) and their output is frozen by `tests/pens-golden.test.ts`. **Fountain pen**: a nib is a line at the stroke's nib angle; the disc is `(width/2) * (0.14 + 0.86 * |sin(angle between the movement and the nib)|) * (0.7 + 0.6 * pressure)`: along the nib a hairline, across it the full width, crisp ends (no taper). **Marker**: `(width/2) * (0.6 + 0.4 * |sin|)`, pressure ignored; **drawn 85% opaque (`MARKER_ALPHA`), source-over, in stroke order** (decision: above the ink written before it, which shows through, so it covers a little more than the highlighter, which is 35% and multiplied and never hides ink; one stroke never darkens itself because the path is filled once; the wet canvas is a third canvas, `.is-marker`, 85% by CSS). **Brush**: `(width/2) * 1.6 * (0.08 + 0.92 * pressure^1.4) * (1 - 0.5 * smoothstep(speed))` with a start that grows from 30% over `0.8 * width + 2` pt, and the last stored point closed to 35% when the stroke is finished (only the last, not yet built segment changes). No per-frame work is added: the radius is a few multiplications per stored point into typed arrays (a test counts garbage like the pen's). **Reach**: boxes and hit tests use `inkReach(tool, width)` (the half-width as before for the pen, highlighter, shape and marker; 1.3x for the fountain pen and 1.6x for the brush, the largest disc they can have), so culling, the eraser and the lasso see the whole line. **Nib after a move**: `nibAfter(nib, matrix)` in `ink/lasso.ts` turns the nib with a rotation, mirrors it with a flip and leaves it for a move or a uniform scale. **Exports**: SVG, PDF (ExtGState `GS3`, `/ca 0.85`), PNG, thumbnails and the HTML export (which embeds the SVG) all use `inkPath(sink, points, width, tool, nib)`, so they are one construction; `inkAlpha` / `inkBlend` give the paint. **Migration v19 -> v20** refuses a v19 file with one of the new tools (no earlier version wrote one; the same rule as "shape" in v3 -> v4, also checked stroke by stroke when a large v19 file is staged page by page) and drops a stray `nib` key (a v19 reader ignored unknown stroke fields; not counted); 0.21 and older open a v20 file as a newer format, read-only. **Reader**: leniently an unknown tool name is read as a `pen` and counted in `dropped`, a new pen with `render` 1 is read with render 2 (counted), an invalid `nib` (or one on a tool without a nib) is dropped (counted); strictly all of them fail. **Repair** keeps the tool and a valid nib and forces render 2 for the new pens. **Merge**: the content key of a stroke includes `nib` (only when set, so existing keys are unchanged). **UI**: the *pen* tool has a pen kind (`penKind`, `nibAngle` in the toolbar state; `ink/pen-kinds.ts`); favourites and pen sets keep an optional `kind` and `nib` (read leniently: an invalid kind is a ballpoint pen); *Settings > Writing > Pen type / Nib angle* set what a new view starts with. A shape recognised from a stroke of a new pen becomes an ordinary `shape` (no nib). Tests: `tests/model-v20.test.ts`, `tests/pens-engine.test.ts`, `tests/pens-golden.test.ts`, `tests/pens-ui.test.ts`; harness `dev/harness/pens-check.ts`.
  - **Page start and end time, format v19 (#217)**: no new element type and no new element field; a page may carry two more optional fields, `startAt` and `endAt`, each on its own line (three tabs, `"startAt": <ms>,` then `"endAt": <ms>,`) right after `locked` and before `states`. `startAt` is when the **first ink or element was added** to the page and `endAt` when the **last one was** (ms since 1970, whole numbers from 0 to 8 640 000 000 000 000, the range of a JS `Date`, so a stored time can always be shown). **They are a pair**: both or neither, `endAt >= startAt` (`pageTimesProblem` in `model/page-times.ts`; one rule for the reader, the writer's check, the guard and the repair). They are the time base that audio recorded with a page will be laid against (M5): nothing else uses them yet except the places below. **Upkeep, not an edit**: `History` takes a clock (its last constructor argument; the view passes `Date.now`, tests a fake one) and, **after** `execute` has run a command and the page-lock and content guards have accepted it, stamps every page the command added ink (`AddStroke`) or an element (`AddElement`) to, or changed the **text** of an element of (`ReplaceElement` whose text, marker label, box title, equation source or checkbox label differs: `elementTextChanged`), looking into batches and into the lock-exempt wrapper (`collectAdds`). The stamp runs **after the step is recorded, inside a try/catch** (a throwing clock is logged and never costs the undo step). The first stamp sets both fields to the time; later ones move `endAt` forward (`stampPage`; a clock that went back never moves the end back nor the start forward). **Clock window**: a reading before 2015-01-01 or after 2100-01-01 (UTC; a device that booted without network time) is ignored, never widens the span; a wrong reading inside the window still widens it for good (nothing shrinks a span). **What counts as written (decision)**: new ink, new elements (including a paste and a copy to another page: they are new objects) and edits of an element's text stamp; a pure move or restyle of an element does not; **ink or elements moved in from another page** (`selection-move` move, `cross-page` regrouping) and **ink made from an equation** are built with `AddStroke` / `AddElement(…, stamps = false)` and do not stamp the page they land on, because they were not written then and no audio was recorded beside them; the destination keeps whatever span it had. There is **no undo step for it**: one Undo undoes the stroke and nothing else, Undo and Redo never touch the times (a page that was written on stays started after the ink is undone), a Redo does not stamp, and the document guard and the lock guard do not count them as content (a locked page keeps its times and a refused or locked command stamps nothing). Removing, replacing, moving and erasing never stamp, and neither does a page that is added or inserted with content (a duplicated, restored or imported page, or the page a *Split page* overflows onto, starts without times until something is added to it). Upkeep through `applyQuietly` is not an addition either. **Why not `applyQuietly`**: it only lets a command replace element objects (the stacks find their elements by identity), and the stamp changes the page object's fields, so it is a small dedicated path inside `execute`; the pages keep their identity and no command holds a copy of the times, so no `remap` is needed. `History` restores the page fields it snapshots exactly when it refuses an undo or a redo (`restoreShot`), so the times are part of "put back exactly". **The closed notebook**: `planClosedEdit` (the API's edits to a notebook that is not open) stamps the pages its commands added to with the time of the call, before the strict check. **The reader**: leniently a lone value, a value that is not a whole time in range, or an end before the start is a detail: **both** are dropped and counted in `dropped` (the ink stays); strictly it fails (`validate`, the checks of `serializeChecked`), and `checkContent` refuses a page whose pair is invalid, so a bad pair in memory is refused at once and can never be written. The writer writes what the page has (it never invents the other half), so the strict check is what refuses a bad pair. **Migration v18 → v19** only drops `startAt` and `endAt` from the pages of a v18 file (a v18 reader ignored unknown page fields, so such a key was never ours: like `comments` in v16 it is dropped, not interpreted; nothing else changes, so large v18 files still stage page by page, where the page reader is told to ignore the keys); 0.20 and older open a v19 file as a newer format (read-only, never overwritten, the same `future-version` path as every earlier format). **Repair** reads the pair from the page's field lines (`PAGE_FIELD_LINE`), keeps a valid pair, and resets an invalid one (counted in `fieldsReset`; the page then has none). **Copies**: a duplicated page, a page or notebook saved as a template and a notebook made from one, a page restored as a new page, a copy of pages to another notebook and a practice attempt **start without times** (they are new pages, and their ink has no time of its own); a page that is **moved** to another notebook or **restored from the page trash** is the same page and keeps its times (`duplicatePage(page, { keepTasks: true })`, the same switch as the checkbox links); restoring a version over a page keeps the earliest start and the latest end of the page in view and the version (`mergedTimes`), so restoring never shortens the span. **Merge of a conflicted copy** (`model/merge.ts`): the times of a page both copies have are not content; the result is the **earliest start and the latest end** (`mergedTimes`), written by a `SetPageTimes` command in the merge's one undo step. Because a stamp can land between that step and its undo or redo (stamps are never recorded), `SetPageTimes` undoes only while the page still has exactly the times it set (a later stamp is never lost) and a redo **widens** the page's current times instead of overwriting them, so `endAt` stays at or after the time of every ink on the page. A times-only difference is a **mergeable change** (`MergeSummary.timesUpdated`, counted when the page brings no ink; part of `mergeChangeCount`, so the dialog's summary, the *Merge* button and the recovery union agree and the widest span is kept). A merge that brings ink or elements onto a page always emits that command (even when the span does not change, even with `null`), which is also what stops `History` from stamping the moment of the merge on the page: `History` leaves alone the pages a `SetPageTimes` of the same command names. A merge that brings nothing and changes no span adds no command (it stays idempotent). A page that only the copy has comes with its times. **Where they are shown** (`headerPageTimes`, setting *Show when a page was written*, on by default, in the page header group): the header line (only when the header is on) appends the short text of the pair (`pageTimesText` in `ink/page-header.ts`, `3 Jun 14:05 – 15:20`, month names in the reader's language, local time; the end only when it is a different minute, with its date when it is another day), for pages that have times; exports use the same line; the notebook map adds it to the card's tooltip and label and as a quiet line under the thumbnail (`written` in `NotebookMapHost`); the page information always shows *Page started* and *Page last written* (or *Not recorded yet*). A page written with 0.20 or earlier has none until something is added to it. Tests: `tests/model-v19.test.ts`; harness `dev/harness/pagetime-check.ts`.
  - **Groups and locked elements, format v18 (#39)**: no new element type and no new page field. Every element (all nine types) can carry up to three more keys at the end of its line, in this order: `"group":"<id>"`, `"locked":true`, `"lockAt":<ms>`; every stroke can carry `"group":"<id>"` right after `slide` and before `points`. **A group** is a set of at least two strokes and elements of **one page** with the same `group` id on that page (the id is scoped to the page: the same id on two pages is two groups; ids are UUIDs, at most 64 characters). Nothing else is stored about a group, so it has no geometry that can go stale; it shrinks when a member is erased or moved to another page. **The writer never writes a group of fewer than two members** (`liveGroups` in `model/serialize.ts` counts strokes and elements together per page; a member of a smaller group is written without its `group`, the objects in memory are untouched), so erasing members can never make a notebook unsavable and the history guard has nothing to refuse. **The reader**: leniently a lone member is released and counted in `dropped` (a merge or a hand edit can leave one); strictly (`validate`, the checks of `serializeChecked`) it fails with "a group has fewer than two members". An invalid `group`, `locked` (anything but `true`) or `lockAt` (not a non-negative integer) is a detail: dropped and counted on its own, the item stays. **A locked element** is fixed in place: `locked: true`; `lockAt` is the time (ms since 1970) of its last lock or unlock decision (written once it was decided; unlocking removes `locked` and keeps `lockAt`, so an older copy that still has the lock cannot bring it back). Locked means: `ink/elements.ts` `elementsInLasso` and `hitElement` skip it, so the lasso, a tap, the **area eraser** (`ink/erase-area.ts`, which uses `elementsInLasso`) and the pen gestures that count what a scribble would erase never pick it; `selectItems` in the view drops a locked element whoever asks; so it cannot be moved, resized, restyled, copied away, erased or deleted until it is unlocked. Ink drawn over it is unaffected (a locked background picture can be written on and the ink can be erased). A tap that only *uses* the element (ticking a checkbox) still works; the text tool does not edit a locked text box. A locked element is unlocked from the small chip a tap with the lasso offers (*Locked · Unlock*), or from *Locked elements…* in the page menu (a list on the modal shell with *Show on the page*, *Unlock* and *Unlock all*, `view/locked-elements-modal.ts`). **Groups in the UI** (`view/group-tools.ts`, pure model in `model/groups.ts`): picking one member with the lasso, or tapping a grouped element or grouped ink, picks the whole group (`expandToGroups`: the strokes come from the ink in view, so invisible ink and the ink a collapsed note hides stay out; a locked member never comes along); move, scale, rotate, copy, cut, duplicate, delete, move-to-page and the clipboards then act on all of them. *Group* (two or more items that are not already exactly one group; one page) gives the selection a new id and *Ungroup* removes it; *Lock* locks the selected elements and drops the selection. Every one is one `UpdateItems` undo step, so the page lock refuses them like any other edit. **`UpdateItems`** (`model/groups.ts`) finds its strokes and elements **by id** when it runs (so it survives a margin change in the same merge step), redoes onto the very objects its undo put back, and implements `remap(swap)` for the elements it holds. **Migration v17 → v18** only drops `group` from strokes and `group`, `locked`, `lockAt` from elements of a v17 file (a v17 reader ignored unknown keys, so such a key was never ours: like `slide` in v12 and `comments` in v16 it is dropped, not interpreted; nothing else changes, so large v17 files still stage page by page, where the page reader is told to ignore the keys); 0.19 and older open a v18 file as a newer format (read-only, never overwritten). **Copies**: a pasted or duplicated selection, a duplicated page, a page copied or moved to another notebook (`duplicatePage`), and a notebook or page made from or saved as a template get **fresh group ids** (the same new id for the members that shared one, `groupRenamer`); **locks are kept**. `copyStroke` keeps `group` (and replaces or removes it on request). A selection saved as a stamp or shared with the other devices is written by the same writer, so a group cut in two is saved free. Exports are unaffected (the flags are not drawn or exported). **Merge of a conflicted copy** (`model/merge.ts`): the group and the lock are an element's **state, not content** (`elementContentKey` leaves them out, so an element locked on one device only is still the same element and is never added as a copy); the strokes' content key never had a group. For the same item (same id, same content) `mergedFlags` unites the groups (an item in no group in the main notebook takes the copy's group; the main notebook's own group stands when both have one; a group only grows in a merge, deletions are not inferred) and lets the later lock decision win (`lockAt`, none counts as 0: the copy's lock or unlock replaces the main notebook's when it decided later; on a tie that disagrees the lock wins, so a lock is never lost). The update is **one `UpdateItems` per page** (an id → copy's item map, so apply, undo and redo are linear in the page, whatever the number of items), computed against the object as it is when the command runs, counted as `flagsUpdated` (which `mergeChangeCount` includes, so a flags-only copy is "something to merge" and the recovery union keeps it). An item takes the copy's group only when that group still has two members in the result (`groupJoins`: members already here, items the merge adds, other joins); otherwise it stays free, so no lone group is made and the copy is not offered again on every open. `mergeCovers` also checks the lock (the result decided at least as late; a tie keeps the copy's lock). Items only the copy has come with their group and lock. **Across pages**: a cross-page drag (`planCrossPageTransform`) and *Split page* (`planPageSplit`) never carry a group id to another page: members that arrive together on a page form a new group there (a fresh id), a member that arrives alone leaves its group, and a group left with one member is released. *Split page* keeps locked elements on the page, moves the movable members of a group to the side of their common middle, and refuses (the *cannot be split* notice) when everything that would move is locked. **Editors**: a tap that would open the marker, box or equation editor of a locked element shows the *locked* notice instead (the editors hold the Delete button). **Selections made by tools** (`selectItems`: find, select similar, states, stamps) are completed to their groups and drop locked elements (`selectableItems`). **Cut ink**: the pieces of a stroke cut by the segment or the area eraser stay in its group only while the page has another member of it (`strokePieces`). Tests: `tests/groups-review-fixes.test.ts`. **Repair** reads the group apart from the one-item pages it checks each item on (a group needs the other members of its page), puts it back, counts a group left with fewer than two members as a reset field, and carries a valid lock onto a repaired element. Tests: `tests/model-v18.test.ts`; harness `dev/harness/groups-check.ts`.
  - **Handwritten checklists, format v17 (#103)**: a page element `{"id","type":"checkbox","x","y","size","color"[,"checked":true][,"at"][,"taskAt"][,"label"][,"task":{"path","block"}]}`, one line, in `elements` like the others. The square is `size` pt (8 to 96) on a side with its top-left corner at (`x`, `y`) and drawn in `color` (`#rrggbb`, the colour of the pen it was drawn with). `checked` is the tick (never written as `false`). `at` is the time of the last toggle in ms since 1970 (written once it has been toggled; unticking keeps it): it is what the merge compares. `taskAt` is the time the link was last set or removed (same unit; written once it was; the merge compares it, so an *Unlink task* is not undone by an older copy that still has the link). `label` is an optional typed name of the item (one line, at most 200 characters, normalised like marker labels: an un-normalised label is cut and counted when read leniently and refused strictly; the cut trims the end again, so a label cut right after a space is still the normalised one). `task` links the checkbox to one line of a note: `path` is the vault path of the note and `block` the block id (`nb-` and letters and digits, at most 40 characters) that the plugin wrote at the end of the line; the reader refuses any block id that does not start with `nb-`, so no other line of a note can ever be named. **The handwriting beside the box is not stored**: the checkbox "owns" the ink on its line only geometrically (`ink/checklist.ts` `checkboxLabelInk`: visible strokes that start at or beyond the box's right edge and whose centre lies in the row of the box, followed one after the other by their left edge until a gap wider than the larger of 24 pt and 1.8 box sides, stopping before the next checkbox on the same row). The label is whatever ink is there now: it is used for the crop of a task and for *Select with text*, never for moving or deleting (decision: the ink-follows-objects machinery in `ink/attach.ts` is untouched and a checkbox owns no ink in the sense of `ownsInk`, because a label found by neighbours cannot be decided stroke by stroke; a lasso over the box and its line moves both, and *Select with text* selects both). **Where the code is**: `model/checklist.ts` (pure: creation, toggling, label and link edits, clearing, the open-item lists, `blockIdFor`, `setTaskLineChecked`), `model/checklist-commands.ts` (`UpdateCheckbox`: one command that finds its target **by id** when applied, like `SetMarkerDone`, so it survives a margin change in the same step), `ink/checklist.ts` (the square detector, the label ink, `checkboxAt` for taps), `ink/checkbox-render.ts` (one description of the shapes that the canvas, the SVG and the PDF draw alike: a rounded square and, ticked, a tick that overshoots the top-right corner), `view/checklist-tools.ts` (taps, the offer chip, insert, label, send to tasks, unlink, the task-line sync) and `view/checklist-modal.ts`. **Migration v16 → v17** only refuses a checkbox in a v16 file (a v16 reader rejected unknown element types, so no version of this plugin wrote one): the check runs page by page (`v17OnlyElement`), so large v16 files still stage (`StagedParse` checks each page as it is read); 0.18 and older read a v17 file as a newer format: read-only, never overwritten (the same `future-version` path as every earlier format). **Read and write**: a checkbox is a detail like markers and notes: read leniently an invalid one is dropped and counted, and an invalid tick time, label or link is dropped on its own (the checkbox stays, unticked-as-written, unlabelled or unlinked); strictly any of them fails, and `serializeChecked` / `checkContent` read each checkbox back and write it again. **Repair** reads a damaged checkbox with the lenient reader of the format (`fixElement`). **Undo and the lock**: a tap, an insert, a label, a link and a conversion are each one history step; a locked page refuses them all with the usual notice. **Copies**: a duplicated page, a pasted or duplicated selection, a page copied to another notebook (or split into a copy) and a merge's *copy under a new id* keep the tick and its time but **never the task link** (`copiedCheckbox`): a copy that kept the link would tick the task line of the original. So does *Duplicate* in the Library (`patchNotebookId` unlinks the checkboxes of the byte copy). A page that is **moved** (pages moved to another notebook, a split, a merge that sends the source to the trash) or **restored from the page trash** is the same page: its checkboxes keep their links (`duplicatePage(page, { keepTasks: true })`). A notebook or page saved as a template, and a notebook or page made from one, have every checkbox **unticked, never toggled and unlinked** (`clearChecklist`): a template is a form to fill in again. The lasso scales a checkbox with the selection (`transformElement`: the centre follows, the side follows the scale within 8 to 96 pt, never rotated or mirrored). **Merge of a conflicted copy** (`model/merge.ts`): a checkbox's *content* is its place, size and colour; the tick, its time, the label and the link are its **state** (`mergedCheckbox`). The same checkbox on both sides is kept once; if the copy was toggled later (`at` greater; never toggled counts as 0) the copy's tick wins, so **toggled on both devices, the later toggle wins**; on a tie that disagrees the tick wins (a tick is never lost). The link follows the later decision (`taskAt`; none counts as 0): the copy's link, or its absence, replaces the main notebook's when the copy decided later, the main notebook's stands when it did, and when neither ever decided, a link only the copy has is added. A label only the copy has is added (the main notebook's own stays; a label that was removed can come back from an older copy). The update is an `UpdateCheckbox` computed against the element as it is when the command runs, counted as `checkboxesUpdated` in the merge summary; `mergeCovers` also checks that each checkbox of the copy is in the result toggled and linked or unlinked at least as late (and that a tick of the copy that tied with an untick is in the result). A checkbox only the copy has comes as it is, link included (it names the same task line). The task line itself is a note, not part of the notebook, and is never merged. **Notebook transfer** (`planTransfer`, *Move to notebook…*) goes through `duplicatePage`: a transferred page keeps its ticks, and its links only in `move` mode. **Task link** (`view/checklist-tools.ts`): *Send to tasks…* reuses the code of *Make task* (`view/selection-task.ts`, `model/selection-task.ts`, `appendToDailyNote`): the same dialog (the text is optional here: the picture is the task), the same crop (of the box and its handwritten label), the same daily-note path, and `- [ ] <typed label> ![[crop|200]] [[page link]] <due> ^nb-<id><salt>` is appended to the end of the note; the block id is derived from the element id plus a salt (the time in base 36) so sending again after an unlink never writes a second line with the same block id. The checkbox then stores `{path, block}`. **Ticks follow into the note** (`model/checklist-sync.ts`, pure bookkeeping; the view writes): after any change of the document `collect` compares each linked checkbox with the tick last written for it (taken from the document when it is opened, so opening never writes) and notes at once, as a pending entry, every one that differs; a debounced write (700 ms), the app being hidden (`flushNow`) and the view closing (`destroy`) then run the entries, which survive a reload of the document. Where the line differs the write rewrites **only the one line of that note that ends with the block id** through `vault.process` (`setTaskLineChecked`: only the status character between the brackets changes, `x` when ticking a space and a space when unticking `x` / `X`; a custom status such as `[/]` is left alone when ticking; every other byte of the note, the line ending of that line included, is untouched; a line that is not a task is never touched even when it carries the block id; frontmatter and fenced code blocks are skipped, a task in a callout or quote counts, and when two task lines carry the id nothing is changed and a notice says so). Because it compares states and not events, undo, redo, a merge and a reload keep the line right. **If the line is gone** (the note exists, its modified time is later than `taskAt`, no task line ends with the block id, no fenced code block in it is left open (`unclosedFence`: everything after it reads as code), and the same was seen a second time at least `GONE_CONFIRM_MS` (1 min) after the first), the checkbox is unlinked and a notice says so. Anything else reads as "missing" and keeps the link: an older copy of the note that has not received the line yet looks the same as a deleted line, which is why the note must have been edited after the link was made (`taskAt` is stamped on every link) and the sight is repeated. The unlink is **upkeep, not an undo step** (`History.applyQuietly`, through the host's `maintain`: applied, saved, not recorded, redo steps kept), so Undo right after the tick un-ticks it; `UpdateCheckbox.revert` finds a checkbox that was replaced since by that upkeep by id and keeps the upkeep's link unless the step was the link's own. **Upkeep and the stacks** (0.19): many steps find their elements by identity (`ReplaceElement`, `AddElement`, `RemoveElement`, `SetPageMargins`, `ReorderLayer`, the cross-page move), so `applyQuietly` only lets an upkeep command replace element objects (same pages, same strokes, an element with the same id in each changed slot; anything else is refused and taken back) and then tells every step of both stacks what replaced what (`Command.remap` with `quietSwap`): the very object replaced becomes the new one, and any other copy of that element a step holds takes the changed fields where it agreed with the replaced object (an earlier position keeps its place and gets the followed link; a copy from before the link was made stays unlinked). A step that holds element objects implements `remap` (also `Batch`, `exemptFromLocks`, `UpdateCheckbox`, `SetMarkerDone`, `SetPageContent` and the page commands, for the elements of the pages they keep); `UpdateCheckbox` and `SetMarkerDone` redo onto the object their undo put back with the very same `after`, so the steps above them still find it. **Backstop**: `History.undo` / `redo` take a shallow snapshot of the document (its fields, its pages' fields and lists; no stroke or element is copied) and run the content guard afterwards, which also refuses an id used twice (`checkContent`, as `serializeChecked`); a step the guard refuses is taken back from the snapshot, dropped from the history and reported (`history.stepRefused`), so an undo can never leave a notebook every later save would refuse. A followed link keeps its `taskAt` (plus 1 ms, so it wins a merge with a copy that still has the old path): a note edited since the link still reads as edited when the rename is seen later. A refused unlink (a locked page, a read-only notebook) tells nothing and is tried again. **If the note is not in the vault** (the notebook can sync before the note does) or cannot be written, the link stays and the entry is tried again after 4 s, 15 s, 1 min and 5 min (then given up with one notice until the checkbox is toggled again). A note that is renamed or moved, or whose folder is renamed, is followed (the vault `rename` event of an open notebook updates the stored path, also as upkeep and not an undo step; a new path longer than `CHECKBOX_PATH_MAX` is not followed: the old link stays and one notice says so). A notebook that is closed at the time keeps the old path, so the plugin records every rename of a note or folder in a small device-local list (`model/rename-map.ts`, `storage/renamed-notes.ts`, local storage key `notebook-renamed-notes`: at most 200 renames, 30 days, never synced, corrupt data reads as empty; a folder rename also explains the notes in it); it is consulted when a notebook opens (a link whose note is missing but whose renamed path exists follows it) and when a tick finds no note at the stored path, using only the renames made after the link (`taskAt`). The reverse (ticking the task in the note ticks the checkbox) is not done: the notebook is the master. **UI**: a tap with the pen, a mouse or a finger on a checkbox ticks it (pen and mouse go through the draw start like an excerpt's badge: the contact is only noted and is ink unless it lifts within 500 ms without moving 8 px, when the dot is dropped and the box is ticked; a finger that would only pan ticks it on a short unmoved tap, unless the pen is down or was used within the palm rejection time (a palm); the pen and mouse target is the box and 3 pt around it (`checkboxAtPen`: a dot or a colon beside the box is ink), the finger target is at least 28 CSS px wide; the page lock is checked first). A small square drawn with the pen is recognised with the shape recogniser of the hold-to-snap feature (`recognizeShape`: closed and rectangular, turned by at most about 11°, a box 0.75 to 1 square, 10 to 44 pt a side; handwriting is never turned into one) and, by the setting *Squares drawn as checkboxes* (key `checklistDraw`: `offer` by default, `auto`, `off`), a chip *Make checkbox* is offered below it for 9 s (it goes when the pen goes down again, the view moves or the page changes; after three offers nobody took, none is made until a checkbox is made or the notebook is opened again) or the square is turned into one after 450 ms unless ink was drawn inside it since (a tick across a square is not a checkbox; the wait is extended while the next stroke is drawn and the check is made again after it). Either way the square stroke is replaced by the checkbox in **one** undo step. Strokes of the Shape tool, highlighter strokes and invisible ink are never offered. *Checkbox* is also in the insert sheet. The selection bar has *Tick / untick*, *Select with text*, *Label…*, *Send to tasks…* and *Unlink task*. The notebook map has an **Open items** filter (a page state `checklist`; the count of unticked checkboxes is on the badge and the label of the first is the tooltip). Exports draw the box and the tick above the ink (the page image of a checklist is the checklist); the Markdown export lists a checkbox that has a typed label as a task-list item `- [ ] label`. Thumbnails draw them too. Tests: `tests/model-v17.test.ts`, `tests/checklist-review-fixes.test.ts`; harness `dev/harness/checklists-check.ts`.
  - **Slide strip (#417)**: `model/slide-strip.ts` (pure) builds, per PDF and per slide, the slide's own page (a page with that PDF page as background), the notes pages linked side by side (from the sidecar), the exercise page after it (the plain page right after the slide's page), the ink written beside it (v12) or in the margin of its page (outside the background), and the questions on it (a question marker belongs to one slide: on its page, next to its linked ink, on its exercise page, or on a notes page linked only to it). `view/slide-strip-modal.ts` shows it on the modal shell (action `slide-strip`: the side-pane header button, the actions sheet when the notebook has PDF pages or the pane is open, the palette, which also works without PDFs and shows an empty state); each chip goes to its page, the slide tile shows the slide. Nothing is stored: it is computed when opened. Tests: `tests/slide-strip.test.ts`.
  - **Links**: the notebook format has no field for them (no format change), so they live in a sidecar, `<notebooks folder>/_meta/pdf-links.json` (a vault file in a reserved folder, read and written through `app.vault` like every other file; it syncs with the notebooks): `{ format: "obsidian-notebook-pdf-links", version: 1, links: [{ notebook, page, source, pdfPage, path? }] }`, one link per line. `notebook` is the notes document id, `page` the notes page id, `source` the content id of the PDF (first 24 hex of its SHA-256, the same id a notebook uses for the asset, so renaming or moving the file does not matter and a PDF imported as a notebook asset keeps its links), `pdfPage` the 1-based page, `path` a hint for plain vault PDFs (a file replaced in place by a new version is still recognised by its path). A notes page has at most one link; several notes pages may share a PDF page. The file is written only by explicit actions (*Link*, *Unlink*, *Add notes page for this slide*), never on scroll or page turn. Each write reads the file again first and applies the operation to what is there (another device may have synced a change), serialises, re-validates and writes only if the text changed; an unreadable file or one from a newer version is never overwritten (one copy `pdf-links.unreadable-<hash>.json` is kept, links are switched off with a message). Entries a version does not understand are written back unchanged. Links to deleted pages are ignored, not removed (so undoing a page delete brings its link back). Until 0.6 previews the file lived in `<config dir>/plugins/<plugin id>/pdf-links.json`; when the vault file does not exist yet, that old file is read once through `app.vault.adapter` (the only way to reach the config folder; nothing is ever written there) and copied over after re-validation, and an unreadable old file is not used (a copy goes next to the new file).
  - **Following** (header toggle *Follow*, on by default): turning the PDF page shows the first notes page linked to it, unless the page in view already is one; scrolling the notes (after 120 ms) shows the PDF page the notes page in view is linked to, if that PDF is the one beside it. Programmatic moves are not echoed back. A PDF page without a linked notes page shows the strip *Add notes page for this slide*: it inserts a page (size and paper of the page in view) as one undo step, placed in slide order (after the last page linked to this PDF at or before that slide, otherwise before the first linked to a later slide, otherwise after the page in view), and links it. If the notes are elsewhere than the linked page, the strip offers *Show*. The reference pane is not remembered across restarts.
- **Annotation export (#155)**: page menu or command *Export PDF annotations to Markdown*. For every PDF page: each highlighter stroke (with the PDF text under it: pdf.js text runs are split into equal-width character cells, and a cell whose middle lies within half the stroke width of the stroke's centre line is selected) and each non-empty text box, top to bottom. The note (`<notebook> annotations.md` next to the notebook, ` 2`, ` 3`… if taken: an existing note is never overwritten) has a wikilink to the notebook (`fileManager.generateMarkdownLink`), one section per notebook page and one callout per item with an `obsidian://notebook?…&page=n` link (`integration/links.ts`). PDF text is escaped; text boxes are copied as written. Pages without a text layer (scans) list their highlights without text.
- **Reading tools (wave 0.6, no format change)** (`view/pdf-reading.ts`, `pdf/text-search.ts`, `pdf/text-index.ts`, `pdf/text-snap.ts`, #44, #43, #188, #199). Everything here is view state or ordinary ink; no field is added to the file.
  - *Search* (#44): command *Search PDF text* or the "more" menu. The text of a PDF page comes from `PdfStore.pageText` and is cached per (asset, PDF page) in `PageTextCache` (at most 4000 pages; a page that cannot be read is not cached). `assemblePageText` joins the runs of a page (a space where there is a gap or a new line) and remembers each run's span; matching ignores case, accents (NFKD), ligatures and the amount of white space, needs at least 2 characters and finds non-overlapping matches. `runSearch` starts at the page in view and wraps round, fetches 4 pages at a time, matches them and yields to the event loop after 8 ms of work, so the UI stays responsive on big PDFs (pdf.js already parses in its worker); a newer search cancels the older one; it stops at 500 matches (and says so); the pages of a PDF that cannot be read are skipped after the first failure. Hits are kept in notebook order and remember the notebook page id, the span in the page text and a snippet. They are marked on the pages by a canvas overlay in the stage (`notebook-reading-overlay`, `pointer-events: none`, redrawn on every view change; it exists only while there are hits to mark, so an idle notebook pays no memory for it) from the boxes `hitBoxes` computes for the page's current PDF box (`backgroundRect`, so margins are respected); nothing is written to the notebook. The character widths inside a run come from a canvas `measureText` in the run's font family (pdf.js `styles`) scaled to the run's width, so a hit inside a long run is placed by real glyph widths; without a measure (or in tests) the characters of a run count as equally wide. Next/previous wrap round and scroll the hit into view below the search bar (the results list folds away if it covers the hit at the top of the notebook).
  - *Snapping highlighter* (#43): option *Snap to text lines* in the highlighter's options popover (setting `snapHighlighter`, off by default). At pen-up a highlighter stroke on a PDF page is replaced by straight highlighter strokes, one per text line crossed (`snapHighlight`: runs whose box the stroke's centre line crosses, within 15% of the run height, otherwise the nearest line within 60%; words covered by at least 40% are included; stretches of a line more than 1.5 heights apart, such as two columns, are separate), each as high as the line (about 1.1 times the run height) with the stroke's colour and opacity (`snappedStrokes`, v24), `render` 2 and the points of a horizontal line, in the same undo step (`addStrokes`). The text is read when the pen goes down (and for the visible pages when the option or the highlighter is switched on); a stroke that ends before it is read, or that is not near any text, stays as drawn. A snapped stroke is an ordinary highlighter stroke, so the format, the eraser, export and undo are untouched.
  - *Hide annotations* (#188): command or "more" menu. `Renderer.setReading(hide, alpha)` skips the ink and elements of pages whose background is a PDF (other pages are drawn as usual); while hidden `editable()` is false, so the toolbar is disabled, nothing can be drawn or changed and a finger pans instead of drawing (the input policy is adjusted), and a banner at the bottom says so and has the button that shows everything again. The stroke being written and the text being edited are closed first. The state is per view, is never saved, and is reset when a document is loaded. Thumbnails, exports and the file are unaffected.
  - *Dim background* (#199): command or "more" menu with a strength slider (10-80%, default 45%), settings `dimBackground` and `dimStrength`. The renderer draws PDF and image backgrounds with opacity 1 minus the strength over the white page. It is a view setting: **PDF/SVG exports, thumbnails and embeds are not dimmed** (an export is meant to look like the notebook, not like one reader's screen); there is no export option for it yet.

## 11. Scanner

`scan/` turns a photo into a page background; there is no live camera inside the plugin (the photo comes from the system camera through a file input with `capture`, or from a file).

1. Load the photo, downscale to at most 2400 px on the long side.
2. **Detect** (`detect.ts`): downscale to 256 px, find the page quadrilateral (contrast, convex hull of the bright region, area between 15% and 97% of the image, fill ratio); no result means default corners are shown.
3. **Crop**: four draggable corners with a loupe (`scan-modal.ts`); corners are ordered (`orderCorners`).
4. **Warp**: a homography maps the quadrilateral to a rectangle (`geometry.ts`); the output size comes from the edge lengths.
5. **Filter** (`filters.ts`): *Original*, *Document* (pixels clearly darker than their surroundings become black, the rest white, with a short soft ramp) and *Whiteboard*; rotate; the preview uses a smaller size than the final output.
6. The result is encoded as JPEG, stored as an image asset and becomes a page with `background: { kind: "image" }` (`scan/pages.ts`, page size from the aspect ratio), inserted with `InsertPages` after the current page, or as an image element (**Insert scan as image**), or as the pages of a new notebook (library, command). Several sheets can follow one another in one session.

### Redo the exercise (#303; wave 0.9: practice)

No format change. *Redo this exercise…* (page sheet entry and command) makes a **practice copy** of a page without some of its ink; the rules are pure (`model/practice.ts`), the dialogs, notices and files are in `view/practice*.ts`.

- **What is hidden** (`HideSpec`): no criterion = every stroke; otherwise a stroke is hidden when it matches every criterion given: its colour is in `colors`, its tool in `tools` (pen, highlighter, shape), its id in `strokes` (the lasso selection, at most 4000 ids). Text boxes, images, the PDF/image background, margins and paper stay: they are the exercise. The copy is `duplicatePage` (new page, stroke and element ids) minus the hidden strokes; the original is never touched.
- **Where it goes**: right after the page (`AddPage`, one undo step, scrolled to), or the end of the *Exercises* notebook (name configurable in the dialog; both choices are remembered on the device in local storage). The notebook is created in the notebooks folder when there is none. An open Exercises notebook takes the page as one `Batch` through its edit context and is saved; a closed one is parsed, extended, re-validated (`serializeChecked`), backed up (`before-move`), written only if the file is still what was read, and read back, exactly like *Move to notebook…* (which is not reused because it also removes the pages from the source). Asset records the target lacks are added; the asset files are content-addressed and shared. The link is written only after the page is safely in the target.
- **Link** (no new fields): the copy carries the page tags `practice` and `attempt/<n>` (they travel with the page, and a copy of a copy never piles up markers), and the sidecar files `<notebooks folder>/_meta/practice-<device>.json` (reserved folder, like `pdf-links.json`; one per device, wave 0.8 review) hold one record per attempt, keyed by the practice page id: `{ practice: { doc, page, path? }, original: { doc, page, path? }, n, at, hide, seconds? }`, plus `removed: [{ page, at }]` for attempts this device removed. A device writes only its own file; reading merges every device's file and the old shared `practice.json` of earlier versions (read lazily as one more source, never written or deleted): a removed page stays removed whichever file still holds it, and the seconds of the same attempt in several files add up (a device that times an attempt it did not create writes a copy of the record with only its own seconds). `doc` is the notebook's document id, so renaming or moving a notebook keeps the link (`path` is only a hint; a search of the notebooks folder by document id is the fallback). The text is deterministic (records sorted by original, number, time, practice page; one per line) so two devices that each add an attempt do not touch the same line; every write re-reads the files, applies the operation to this device's file, re-validates and writes only if the text changed; entries a version does not understand are written back unchanged; an unreadable or empty own file, or one from a newer version, is never overwritten (one copy `practice.unreadable-<hash>.json` is kept) and *Redo this exercise* then refuses to make a copy it could not link; another file that cannot be read is skipped and reported once. Written only by *Redo this exercise* (`add`) and the timer's *Stop* (`addSeconds`, which adds to what is stored, so two devices timing the same attempt add up). A moved page (*Move to notebook…*) keeps its page id (unless the target already has it) and its tags, so its record follows it; a deleted page's record is ignored, not removed (undoing the delete brings the link back).
- **Redoing a practice page** starts from the page it came from (found by the record), so every attempt begins from the same sheet and the numbers continue (`nextAttemptNumber`: one more than the highest for that original). The selection option is only offered on an original.
- **The strip** (`practice-banner.ts`, in the view body, over the page): shown on a page with a record or the `practice` tag. *Peek*: while the button is held (pointer capture, so sliding off still ends it; Space or Enter on the keyboard) `Renderer.setPeek` draws the original's hidden strokes (`peekStrokes`) on the live layer at 85 % opacity, in the copy's page coordinates; released, on a page change or when the view closes, it is removed. It is never part of the document, the static layer, thumbnails or exports. The command *Peek at the solution* switches it on and off (a hotkey cannot be held). *Original* and *Attempts* open the page (scroll when it is in the same notebook, `openNotebookAtPage` otherwise); the attempts list shows the original and each attempt with its number, date and time spent.
- **Timer**: *Start* keeps the start time in memory (per practice page, shared by the views, so it keeps running while another page is shown); *Stop* adds the elapsed seconds to the record; closing the view stops and saves the timers of its notebook. A timer still running when the plugin unloads is lost.
- **Wiring**: `NotebookHost.practice` (the plugin's `PracticeService`) gives each view a `PracticeHooks` through `practiceHost()`; the view calls `practiceHooks.page(index)` from `updatePageIndicator` (cheap when nothing changed), `loaded()` and `destroy()`. The commands (`registerPracticeCommands`) go through `view.practiceHooks`.
- **Not done**: variants with different numbers (they need the variables of #182 and the drawings with states of #241, which do not exist yet).

## 12. Obsidian integration

`integration/` registers the parts that talk to Markdown notes and the app (called once from `onload`):
- **Embeds** (`embed.ts`, `links.ts`): the code block language `notebook`. Source forms: `path: Notebooks/Physics.notebook` with an optional `page: 3`, a bare path with `#3` / `#p3` / `#page=3`, or a wikilink `[[Physics#p3]]` (resolved relative to the note with Obsidian's link resolution, then by file name in the notebooks folder). The path may omit the extension. The block renders one page with the thumbnail code at the width of the note (device pixels capped at 2048), is static (no editing), redraws 400 ms after the notebook changes (modify, rename, delete, create), and a click opens the notebook at that page. Errors (empty block, bad page, not found, page out of range, unreadable) are shown in the block. The command **Insert notebook embed** writes the block at the cursor.
- **Protocol** (`protocol.ts`): `obsidian://notebook?path=<vault path>&page=<n>&id=<page id>` (`vault=<name>` is handled by Obsidian). It opens the notebook, reusing the open tab, and scrolls to the page (the id wins over the number).
- **Opening at a page** (`open.ts`): library search hits, embeds and links go through `openNotebookAtPage`, which opens the file and gives the view `{ page, pageId }` as state; a view that is already open is told to scroll. The view resolves a page id first (`resolvePageTarget`) and says when the page was deleted.
