- **Stable page links (wave 0.8, 388)**: every link the plugin writes uses `stablePageSubpath(page, pageId)` (`integration/page-links.ts`): `#p3&id=<page id>`; Zettel cards already did (`cardSubpath`). Users: `page-share.ts` (copy / insert link and embed), `page-link-suggest.ts`, `notebookUri(…, page, pageId)` (companion notes, PDF annotation notes), the API (`getPageLink`). Recent pages and the quick switcher (roadmap 364, 365, branch `feat/quicknav`) must use the same helper. **Update links** (`link-update.ts` pure, `update-links.ts` command and list): scans notes outside code for wikilinks, embeds and Markdown links with a page subpath, resolves the notebook with Obsidian's link resolution (bare names shared by several notebooks are ambiguous), and plans three kinds of change, each replacing one exact range of the note: *pin* (`#pN` → `#pN&id=<id of the page now at N>`), *renumber* (the id is in the notebook at another position) and *relocate* (the id is not in the linked notebook but in exactly one other). Nothing is written until the user accepts the list; each accepted change is applied only if the text is still where it was (`vault.process`). Pages moved to another notebook keep their ids (`planPageMove`), so *relocate* finds them; only a page whose id the target already has gets a new one, and that old → new pair is recorded per device in `_meta/page-moves-<device>.json` (`storage/page-moves.ts`), which *relocate* consults when the old id is nowhere. A sync conflicted copy (`storage/conflicts.ts`, same folder as the notebook it copies) holds the same ids and is never a relocation target. A Markdown link target is percent-encoded with `encodeMarkdownPath` (also `(`, `)`, `#`, `?`). The scan leaves out the frontmatter, fenced code (also inside blockquotes, callouts and lists), inline code and `%% %%` comments; the list shows the text of each link's line.

- **Version history** (§4): `history-service.ts` registers the snapshot hooks and the restore command.
- **Companion notes (O1)**: see below. Without them (the default) notebooks stay invisible to Obsidian's own search, tags, backlinks and graph.

### Companion notes (O1)

A Markdown note per notebook so that Obsidian's search, graph, tags, Dataview and Bases see notebooks. **Off by default** (setting *Companion notes*): turning it on creates files in the user's vault (one per notebook, synced to every device), which should never happen as a side effect of an update, and the notes only help people who use those Obsidian features. When on, a note is created the first time a notebook is opened or changed; *Update companion notes of all notebooks* (command, and a button in the settings) does the rest at once.

**Three layers**, so M4 can reuse the content:
- `model/companion-content.ts` (pure): `companionContent(doc, { notebookPath, pageUri })` → `{ properties, tags, text }`; `renderRegion()` wraps the text in markers; `mergeRegion()` and `mergeProperties()` apply it to a note that may have been edited. The output depends only on the document and the notebook path: no clock, time zone, UI language or vault name, so two synced devices produce byte-identical notes.
- `storage/companion-path.ts` (pure): settings (`companionNotes`, `companionLocation` = `beside` | `folder`, `companionFolder`, `companionName` with `{name}`) and `companionPath()`. In folder mode the notebook's folder relative to the notebooks folder is mirrored, so equal names in different folders do not collide.
- `integration/companion.ts`: when to write, which note is the companion, and the Vault calls.

**Content.** Plugin-owned properties, in order: `notebook` (`[[<vault path>|<name>]]`, a real link: the graph and backlinks see it), `notebook-id` (the document id: how the plugin recognises its notes), `pages`, `created`, `modified` (UTC, minute precision, `2026-10-29T14:42:00Z`: identical on every device, and an ink-only save changes the note at most once a minute), then every field of `meta` with a string, number, boolean or list value under its own name (`favorite`, `cover`, and `course`, `teacher`… when the format adds them), with `bookmarks` as page numbers. `tags` holds the notebook's tags as Obsidian tags (no `#`, spaces → `-`, invalid characters dropped, numeric-only tags left out). The body has one generated region:

```
%% notebook-companion:start (generated by Notebook: edit outside this block) {"v":1,"keys":[…],"tags":[…]} %%
### p. 1
[↗ p. 1](obsidian://notebook?path=Notebooks%2FPhysics.notebook&page=1)

<text boxes of page 1, top to bottom, then left to right>
%% notebook-companion:end %%
```

Only pages with text get a section. The heading is language-neutral (`p. N`), so `[[Physics#p. 3]]` links to that section. The page link has no `vault=` (Obsidian uses the current vault), so it does not change with the vault's name. Text-box text is kept as written (links and inline `#tags` work) except what could break the note: `%%` is escaped, and lines that would open a fence, a math block or an HTML block, or become a heading, a heading underline or a rule, get a leading backslash.

**Ownership** (the user may edit the note):
- Everything outside the region, and every property that is not plugin-owned, belongs to the user and is never changed.
- The region is replaced as a whole. With no region (new note, or the user deleted it) it is appended at the end. If the markers are missing one half, repeated or out of order, the note is not written at all and a notice says why.
- Plugin-owned properties are overwritten from the notebook. Which keys the plugin owns, and which tags it added, is stored on the start marker (`keys`, `tags`), so a key the notebook no longer has (an emptied `course`, cleared bookmarks) is removed, and nothing else. Without a readable state nothing is removed.
- `tags` is shared: notebook tags first, then the user's own; tags the plugin added before and the notebook no longer has are removed.
- The whole note (frontmatter with the merged properties, and the region) is built as text by `updateCompanionText` (pure, `model/companion-content.ts`) and written once with `vault.process` (atomic), so a sync client never sees a half-updated note. The frontmatter block is re-serialized only when a plugin-owned property changed; otherwise it stays byte for byte. If the frontmatter is not valid YAML, or the markers are broken, the note is left alone.

**Which note** (`companionClaim`). The note at the configured path (or `<name> (notebook).md` if a note of the user's already has that name: it is never taken over) when its `notebook` link points at this notebook, or its `notebook-id` is this notebook's and its link resolves to no notebook. Otherwise any note found through the metadata cache whose link points at this notebook, or that has this `notebook-id` and a link that resolves to nothing (the user moved it, the location setting changed, the notebook was renamed outside Obsidian: existing notes are not moved), but the latter only when no other notebook in the vault has the same id: a file copy of a notebook keeps the id, and must not take over the original's note (both would rewrite it back and forth). Otherwise a new note is created at the first free path.

**When.**
- Triggers are the notebook's own events: `modify` (4 s debounce only when the file holds what a view on this device just saved, `DocumentSession.isOwnWrite`; 30 s for every other change, i.e. sync, another app or library actions, also when the notebook is open here, so that the companion written by the other device usually arrives first and the identical content found here causes no write: two devices writing the same note make the sync client create conflicted copies), `file-open` (30 s as well; creates missing notes lazily and writes only if the note differs), `create` (5 s, registered after layout-ready), `rename`, `delete`.
- Never while writing: capture-phase, passive `pointerdown`/`pointerup`/`pointercancel` listeners note activity inside `.notebook-view`; a due update waits until no pointer is down there and 1.5 s have passed (a pointer seen down for 15 s without an up is ignored). The notebook's own saves are already deferred while writing (§4), so the `modify` that starts an update arrives in a pause anyway.
- **No write when nothing changed**: the new frontmatter and body are computed first and compared with the note (a timestamp read back as a Date compares equal); only the parts that differ are written.
- **No loops**: the service never listens to `.md` files (its own writes and the user's edits start nothing) and never writes the notebook, so the notebook view's external-change check is never involved.
- The document comes from the open view when there is one (no second parse), otherwise from the file; an unreadable notebook gets no update.

**Rename and delete.** When a notebook is renamed or moved and its companion is at the configured place for the old path, the companion is renamed to the place for the new path with `fileManager.renameFile` (Obsidian updates links to it); a companion the user put elsewhere stays there. Renaming a folder in `beside` mode moves the companions with it. On **delete the companion is kept**: it may contain the user's own writing, and deleting a notebook from the library only moves it to the trash (restoring it reconnects the companion by id). A notice names the kept note (one notice for a folder of notebooks). Its `notebook` link becomes unresolved.

**Library and index.** Companion notes are `.md`, which `isNotebookFile` rejects, so the library, the index, the picker and version history never treat them as notebooks. A companion folder placed inside the notebooks folder shows up in the library sidebar as a folder without notebooks.

**Shared tags (O2).** A notebook's tags live in `meta.tags`; Obsidian only indexes Markdown, so they reach the Tags pane and tag search through the companion note's `tags` property (with companion notes off there is nothing for Obsidian to index, and the tag editor says so and offers to turn them on). `model/tags.ts` (pure) is the single definition of a tag: `normalizeTag` writes tags like Obsidian (no `#`, spaces → `-`, `a/b` nested, no empty levels, not only digits; letters, digits, marks, emoji, `_`, `-`, `/` are kept), and the companion generator uses the same function, so what the tag editor stores is what the note gets. The tag editor (`integration/tags.ts`, used by the library menu and by the command *Edit tags of this notebook*) suggests tags from `metadataCache.getTags()` (not in the public typings: feature-detected, with a fallback that counts `getAllTags` over the notes) plus the library's own, ranked prefix, nested level, substring, and refreshes the companion note 2 s after saving. The library filter treats `a` as including `a/b` and counts a nested tag for its parents, like the Tags pane. Merge rule for the `tags` property (`computeSharedTags`): every notebook tag is present; a tag in the note that the plugin did not add is the person's and is never removed (even if the notebook later gets or drops a tag of the same name; their spelling is kept); only tags the plugin added earlier and the notebook no longer has are removed. "Added by the plugin" is the `tags` list on the region's start marker, which now holds only the tags that were not yet in the note when the plugin added them (before 0.6 it held all of the notebook's tags). The notebook is the source of truth for its own tags: deleting one of them from the note by hand brings it back, and a tag added only in the note is not copied into the notebook (one direction only: notebook → note).

**Public API (#168; `docs/api.md`).** `app.plugins.plugins["notebook"].api` (set by `registerIntegration`) exposes `version` (semver of the API), `createNotebook`, `addPages`, `insertImage`, `exportPdf`, `getNotebookInfo`, `openNotebook`, `listNotebooks` and `NotebookApiError`. No format change. Arguments are validated (unknown option names are refused) and every failure is a `NotebookApiError` with a code. An edit is built once as model commands (`Prepare` in `integration/api-plan.ts`) and run in one of two ways: **open notebook** → `NotebookView.editContext()` (waits up to 3 s for a loading view or a pen that is down, then `busy`), one `Batch` = one undo step, `flush()`, then the file is read back and must contain the change; **closed notebook** → `planClosedEdit`: parse, apply the commands to the parsed document, `serializeChecked` (full), verify the change in the parsed result; then backup of the previous text (`before-api`), `vault.process` only if the file still holds what was read (one retry, then `changed-elsewhere`), read back and verify (the same pattern as the closed destination of "Move to notebook…"). An unreadable file never produces text, so it is never overwritten. Calls on one notebook are serialised (`KeyedQueue`). Images are stored with `storeAsset` (content-addressed in `_assets/`) and decoded with `photoSize`; the format of the bytes is recognised from the first bytes. `exportPdf` uses the open view's in-memory document or the parsed file with a fresh `NotebookMedia`, and writes the PDF with the same writer as the UI.

**M4.** If notebooks become `.md` (option B), the notebook file itself gets `companionContent()` as its frontmatter and generated body (the region markers and merge rules stay), the `notebook` property is dropped, and the migration can fold an existing companion into the notebook: its user text and properties are already separated from the generated parts. Links like `[[Physics]]` written against a companion at the default place (`Physics.md` next to `Physics.notebook`) then point at the migrated notebook.

### Links written with the pen (O8)

**No format change.** A link is ordinary text: `[[Note]]`, `[[Note|alias]]` or `[[Note#Heading]]` in a text element. Everything below is derived from the text, so old plugin versions show the same elements as plain text.

- **Pure rules** (`ink/link-text.ts`, no 'obsidian'): parsing (`parseLinks`: one line, no embeds `![[…]]`, a note name is required), the wrapped layout of a link (`lineRuns`, `linkRects`: lines of `layoutText` are substrings of the text, found in order), hit testing (`hitLink`), the trigger and completion of a typed `[[` (`linkTrigger`, `completeLink`), note ranking (`rankNotes`: exact name, prefix, word start, substring, folder, subsequence; empty query = most recently modified), the path of a note created from an unresolved link (`newNotePath`) and the drawing of linked text (`drawLinkedLines`, called by `drawTextElement` only when the text contains `[[`, so text without links takes the old path). The brackets stay visible: the text is laid out exactly as typed, so wrapping, bounds, the editor and the companion note never disagree. `setLinkResolver` lets the view say whether a note exists (unresolved links are dimmed and dashed).
- **Ink links** are text elements whose whole text is one link (`soleLink`). `inkLinkElement` puts the label directly above the ink region (on the page's top edge when there is no room) and appends empty lines so the box reaches the bottom of the region: the box is the tap area and the existing rule *ink written on an object moves with it* (`ink/attach.ts`, setting *Ink follows objects*) keeps ink and label together. Limits: at most 120 lines; editing the label with the text tool trims the empty lines (the box then covers only the label). The companion note trims trailing empty lines, so the link is listed once.
- **Handwritten `[[`** (`ink/bracket-detect.ts`, pure): a stroke is a bracket when, simplified to its corners (Douglas-Peucker, then nearly straight corners dropped), it has exactly three segments: a near-vertical spine at least 60% of the stroke's height with a short near-horizontal hook at each end, corners of 50-130 degrees, both hooks on the same side, height 6-240 pt and width 10-70% of the height. `l`, `|`, `1`, parentheses, `S`, `Z` and wide letters fail one of these. Two "[" of similar height (ratio 0.6-1.67), level (70% vertical overlap) and a spine apart (0.2-1.6 heights) read as `[[`. `penLinkCandidate` looks at the last strokes of the page: the latest `[[` followed only by strokes on the same line to its right (the name being written). The result is only ever a suggestion chip; nothing is converted automatically.
- **View side** (`view/pen-links.ts`): resolves links with `metadataCache.getFirstLinkpathDest` (also trying the notebook extensions), opens with `workspace.openLinkText(…, "tab")` (the notebook stays open), creates missing notes with `vault.create` at `fileManager.getNewFileParent`; the note chooser (`SuggestModal`) used by *Link to a note…* and the chip; the `[[` suggester under the text editor (a custom popup: Obsidian's `AbstractInputSuggest` does not accept a textarea; pointer events on it are stopped and focus kept so choosing never closes the editor); tap-to-open (a finger tap that the input policy would treat as navigation, short and still, outside the text tool; Ctrl/Cmd+click for a mouse, handled in the capture phase so no dot is drawn). Redraws when the vault's notes change (`metadataCache "resolved"`, create/delete/rename, debounced 300 ms). Entry points: the lasso selection bar (*Link to a note…*, *Open link*), the command *Link selection to a note…*.
- **Companion notes** (§12) copy the text of text elements, so text-box links and ink links reach Obsidian's graph and backlinks unchanged (`companion-content.ts` already keeps `[[…]]` and escapes anything that could close the region).
- **Not done**: following a renamed note (Obsidian rewrites links in Markdown only), page-level links to a notebook from a handwritten link beyond what `openLinkText` already does for `Notebook#p3`, and a link on ink that is not a text element (a persisted anchor would need a format change).

### Zettelkasten (M15 Z1, Z2, Z3, Z11, Z12; wave 0.6)

Cards (Zettel) are ordinary Markdown notes that show a page, or a region of a page, **live**: the note embeds the ink, nothing is copied, so writing in the notebook updates every card. The document format is unchanged: **nothing about cards is stored in the notebook file**.

**Card note.** Created with `vault.create` in the card folder (setting, default `Zettelkasten/`), named `<id> <title>.md` (just `<id>.md` without a title; ` 2`, ` 3`… if taken). Properties, in order: `zettel-id`, `title`, `aliases` (the title), `type` (`permanent` | `literature` | `fleeting`), `source`, `date` (local `YYYY-MM-DD`), `tags`, `zettel-notebook` (`[[<vault path>|<name>]]`: the graph shows card → notebook), `zettel-notebook-id` (document id), `page-id` (the page's id, never its index), `region` (`x,y,w,h` in page units, absent for a whole-page card). Body: the live embed, then an empty `## Transcription` section (Z11; language-neutral so handwriting recognition can fill it later, M5):

```
![[Notebooks/Box.notebook#p3&id=<page id>&r=40,60,200,120]]

### Processing flow (M15 Z4; wave 0.8)

An **Inbox** view (`zettel/inbox-view.ts`, view type `notebook-inbox`, commands *Open the inbox* and *Process the next inbox item*) lists what still has to be processed and walks through it. No change to the document format; nothing is stored in notebooks.

**Items** (`zettel/inbox-items.ts`, pure). An `InboxItem` is a piece of one page: `key` (stable: `<prefix>|<notebook id>|<page id>|<stroke, text box or marker id>`), `source`, `kind`, notebook and page (by id), `region` (null: the whole page), `text`, `sig` (how much content it had), `origin` (PDF name and page, for a literature note) and the card type suggested. Sources are `InboxSource { id, collect(env) }` objects that return every item they find and write nothing:
- `loose-sheet`: the pages of the quick-notes sheet (`library/loose-sheet.ts`), split into **blocks** of writing at vertical gaps over 36 pt (about 1.3 cm; strokes and typed text alike). A block is named by its first stroke (else text box), so it keeps its name while it grows; its region is its box with a margin (the whole page when it fills about 70% of it).
- `pdf`: highlighter strokes and non-empty text boxes on PDF pages of every notebook that has some (the same material as the annotation export, #155, without writing a note): candidates for literature notes, with the PDF's name and page as source. Notebooks are filtered by a cheap test on the raw text before they are parsed. The text under a highlight is read from the PDF only when the item is shown (`textUnderStroke` on the page's text layer; nothing without a text layer).
- **Adapter for markers.** `registerInboxSource(source)` (exported from `zettel/inbox.ts`) adds a source; `markerKey()` and the `marker` kind with `markerKind` (`question`, `placeholder`, `importance`) are ready for the marker elements of format v8. The Inbox does not depend on them: without a registered source nothing changes.

**Processing** (`InboxService`, `inbox-service.ts`). Per item: **permanent card** or **literature card** (the card dialog of Z1 starts with a title taken from the first line of the text, the type, and for a PDF item the source `Book.pdf, p. 12`; creation goes through `ZettelService.createCardFor`, the same writer and queue as every other card, with the text as the first content of the *Transcription* section: a quote for PDF text, as typed otherwise; the card embeds the item's region live), **link to an existing card** (the card list; the item is marked processed and remembers the card), **discard**, **skip** (writes nothing). After an action the next open item is selected. A card on the same page that already covers the item (whole page, or at least half of its region) is offered as *Mark as processed*. **Nothing is deleted** from the notebook, the card notes are not changed by linking, and an item that is processed can be reopened.

**State** (`zettel/inbox-state.ts` pure, `zettel/inbox-store.ts` over a file adapter). Per device, in `<notebooks folder>/_meta/inbox-<device>.json` (`{ format: "obsidian-notebook-inbox", version: 1, device, entries: [{ key, state: open | discarded | card | linked, at, sig?, card?, path? }] }`, one entry per line, sorted by key). The device id is 8 hex characters kept in Obsidian's local storage (`app.saveLocalStorage`), so it never syncs. **A device only ever writes its own file**, so two synced devices never write the same file and a sync service has nothing to make conflicted copies of; reading merges all `inbox-*.json` files: per key the entry with the latest `at` wins (ties: the larger device id), which also lets any device reopen an item. Writes happen only in the explicit actions above; each reads the device's file again first, applies the operation, serialises, re-validates and writes only if the text changed. A file that cannot be parsed, is empty (it may not have finished syncing) or is from a newer version is never overwritten: `update` refuses with a notice and keeps one copy named by its content; another device's unreadable file is skipped on reading (and never touched). Looking at the Inbox writes nothing. A block's `sig` is its stroke and text count when processed: more content later makes the item open again ("new writing since processed"); PDF items are processed for good.

### Quick textbook reference (roadmap 208; wave 0.8)

A page reference typed in a text box ("p. 134", "pag. 134", "p134", "pp. 134-136", "page 7", "pagina 9") opens the course textbook at that page. No change to the document format; the text stays what it is.

- **Recognition** (`view/textbook-ref.ts`, pure): one regular expression (the word must not end a longer word, a bare "p" needs the dot or no space, 1–4 digits); `placeRefs` finds where each reference is drawn with the same `layoutText` that draws the box (the layout up to the end of the reference's word, so wrapping is identical), as a rectangle on its last line.
- **Tappable area** (`view/textbook-ref-ui.ts`): for the text boxes of the visible pages, one transparent `<a>` per reference over the typed words (CSS vars like the Z1 card regions; positions follow the viewport through `NotebookView.addOverlayListener`, a second overlay slot next to `overlayChanged`). Hidden while the text tool is active (`activeTool()`) and when no textbook applies. Tap: opens the textbook; long press / right click: a menu with every applicable textbook and both ways of opening.
- **Opening.** In the PDF pane of the notebook view (`view/pdf-sync.ts`: `referencePane(view)` → `show(file, page)`) when that pane is closed or already shows this textbook; when the pane shows other slides they are not replaced and Obsidian's own PDF viewer opens `<pdf>#page=N` in a split (`openLinkText`). A textbook that was moved is found again by its file name when it is the only PDF of that name.
- **Configuration** (`view/textbook-store.ts` over a file adapter): one file per device, `<notebooks folder>/_meta/textbooks-<device>.json` (`{ format: "obsidian-notebook-textbooks", version: 1, textbooks: [{ scope: notebook | folder | all, target, pdf, label?, offset, at }], removed?: [{ scope, target, pdf, at }] }`; wave 0.8 review), merged on read with the other devices' files and the old shared `textbooks.json` (read as the oldest source, never written or deleted): per textbook the latest setting or removal wins, textbooks keep the order they were first set in, a vault file in the reserved `_meta` folder like the PDF links, **not** the plugin's `data.json` (which is rewritten all the time by unrelated settings and is per device; a vault file reads the same on every device and is edited only on purpose). `target` is the notebook's document id, or the vault path of a folder (a course). `offset` is added to the printed page to get the PDF page. For a notebook the textbooks that apply are, most specific first: its own, the nearest folder up to the library root, everything. Written only by the commands *Set textbook for this notebook…* and *Remove a textbook…*, each reading the file again first and writing only if the text changed; an unreadable, empty or newer own file is never overwritten (one copy kept) and reading never writes. Entries a version does not understand are written back unchanged.
- **Insert textbook reference** (command): asks for the page number and adds a text box "p. N" (14 pt, link blue) at the last pen position seen over the pages (`pointermove`/`pointerdown` on the stage; the middle of the page in view when there is none or it is older than two minutes) with one `AddElement`, so one undo step; it goes through `editContext()`, so it refuses with a notice while a pen is down or the notebook is read-only.

## Transcription
```

**Card subpath** (`zettel/subpath.ts`, used by `links.ts`, `page-links.ts` and `embed.ts`). After `#`: the page part as before (`p3`), then `&`-separated parameters in any order: `id=<page id>` and `r=x,y,w,h` (one decimal). Unknown parameters are ignored, a malformed `id` or `r` is an error shown in the embed. With `id`, the embed shows that page wherever it is now (the `p3` is only a fallback for readers that do not know `id`); if no page has that id the embed says the card's page was deleted and never shows another page. With `r`, the page preview renderer (`library/thumbnail.ts`, option `region`) draws only that part, at the region's own aspect ratio, with the PDF or image background rendered at the matching resolution. Plain `#p3` links behave as before. Following a link: `pageTargetFromEphemeralState` + `resolvePageTarget` (`integration/page-links.ts`) resolve the page by id first and fall back to the number only when the link has no id; when the id names no page any more the link's page was deleted, and the view must say so (`links.pageDeleted`) instead of opening whatever page now has that number. Not wired yet: `NotebookView.setEphemeralState` still calls `pageFromEphemeralState`, which reads only the number.

**Ids and file names.** Two synced devices only know their own cards until the other's notes arrive, so an id free here may be taken there. Timestamp ids are `YYYYMMDDHHmmss-<4 random chars>` (local time; `crypto.getRandomValues`), so their file names never collide either; older 12-digit ids stay valid. Luhmann ids keep their numbering (`1`, `1a`…; two devices can still pick the same number before syncing, which the user resolves), and the file name of an untitled Luhmann card carries the random suffix (`2-k3f9.md`), so two devices never create the same file (a sync service would make conflicted copies). *Set card title* renames a note still named `<id>` or `<id>-<suffix>` to `<id> <title>`.

**Keys.** Cards use their own `zettel-notebook` / `zettel-notebook-id`, not the companion note's `notebook` / `notebook-id`: a device still on 0.5.1 with companion notes on would take a synced card for a companion note (same link and id) and write its generated region into it. Cards written with the companion keys by unreleased builds are still read, only because they also have `zettel-id`.

**Index.** `ZettelService` keeps an in-memory map of cards built from the metadata cache (`zettel-id` + `zettel-notebook-id` + `page-id`), updated on `metadataCache` `changed` and vault `delete`/`rename` of `.md` files. Those events only update the index and redraw overlays; they never write. A note counts as a card of a notebook file when its `zettel-notebook-id` is that document's id and its `zettel-notebook` link resolves to that file (or to no notebook: moved outside Obsidian), so a file copy of a notebook (same id) does not take over the original's cards. Companion notes skip any note with a `zettel-id` too (belt and braces for the cards that still have the companion keys).

**Writes** (zero data loss, no sync churn).
- A card is created only on an explicit action (*Make card from selection*, *Make card from this page*, the slip-box commands) or, in a slip-box, after this device's own save added a page.
- After creation the plugin only ever changes three things, each with one `vault.process` only when a value really changes (`editProperties`: only the lines of the changed keys are rewritten, so the user's properties, YAML comments and quoting stay as written; if the edited block would not read back as exactly the wanted properties, e.g. YAML anchors, the block is re-serialized as a whole as `processFrontMatter` does; the body is kept byte for byte, invalid YAML leaves the note alone with a notice): `orphaned` (set when the card's page is no longer in its notebook, removed when it is back, e.g. after undo), `links` (drawn links, appended, never removed) and `title`/`aliases` (*Set card title*, explicit). Earlier titles stay in `aliases`. A note still named after its id alone is renamed to `<id> <title>` through `fileManager.renameFile`, so links follow.
- **A card is never deleted** by the plugin. Orphaned cards (page deleted, or notebook not found) are listed by *Show orphaned cards*; *Show cards of this notebook* marks them too.
- Triggers are notebook `modify` events only: 4 s after this device's own save (`DocumentSession.isOwnWrite`). Card notes are written **only after this device's own saves**: any other change (sync, another app) only redraws the overlays after 30 s, because the device that made the change writes the flags itself and every device writing the same note would make conflicted copies. Due work waits until no pointer is down in a notebook view and 1.5 s have passed (`InputWatch`, shared with companion notes). Slip-box creation and arrow links run only after own saves, so two devices do not both create the same card.

**Slip-box (Z2).** A notebook is a slip-box when its document id is in the setting `zettelSlipBoxes` (plugin settings, not the notebook file: no format change). *New slip-box* creates a notebook with A6 pages (105 × 148 mm, default paper) and a card for its first page; new pages use the size of the page they follow, so they are A6 too. *Make this notebook a slip-box* creates a card for every page without a whole-page card (asking first when there is more than one). After an own save of a slip-box, every page **made on this device in this session** without a card gets one (at most 20 at a time; a `<doc id>/<page id>` set prevents duplicates while the metadata cache catches up). `LocalPages` records the pages of a notebook at the first look and the pages of every change this device did not make (read from the file when it changes), so a page that came by sync, whose card may still be on its way, never gets a second card here. Limitation: the setting is per device unless the plugin settings are synced; a device that does not know the notebook is a slip-box just does not create cards (the cards created elsewhere arrive by sync).

**Drawn links (Z3).** An arrow drawn with the shape tool (or recognised from a held stroke) is one polyline tail → tip → wing → tip → wing (`ink/shapes.ts`). `arrowEnds` takes the point farthest from the tail as the tip and checks the straight shaft and the out-back-out head; `cardAt` maps each end to the smallest region card containing it (6 pt tolerance), else to the page's whole-page card. Tail and tip on two different cards add a wikilink to the other card to each note's `links` list (`addLinks`, de-duplicated by target), which Obsidian shows in the graph and backlinks. Automatically, only arrows drawn after the notebook was opened in this session are linked (the shape strokes present when the view first had the document are remembered), so a link the user removed from a note is not put back; *Link cards joined by arrows on this page* does every arrow on the page on request. Deleting an arrow does not remove the link.

**Region overlay.** `NotebookView` exposes two small hooks for DOM overlays (`overlayChanged`, called at the end of every `setViewport`, and `overlayGeometry()` with the stage, viewport and page boxes) and `selectionRegion()` (lasso bounds). `zettel/overlay.ts` places one absolutely positioned element per visible card on the stage (`pointer-events: none`, so it never affects input): a dashed outline for region cards and a small id badge (top right; whole-page cards only get the badge). Setting *Show card regions* (on by default) and the command *Show or hide card regions*.

**Z12, the M4 container move.** Everything goes through `storage/format.ts` (`isNotebookFile`, `readNotebookText`) and the document id; cards refer to pages by id, so a migration that keeps document and page ids keeps every card. If notebooks become `.md`, the notebook's own frontmatter will hold `notebook-id` (companion content), but never `zettel-id`, so it is not taken for a card. Obsidian renders `![[X.md#…]]` itself, so the migration rewrites the embed line of card notes (`zettel-id` in frontmatter, line = `cardEmbed()`) to the `notebook` code block form with the same subpath (`path: X.md#p3&id=…&r=…`), which `parseEmbedSource` already reads; the card's properties need no change.

### Optional PDF copy (0.17, #23)

`storage/pdf-copy-logic.ts` (pure: settings, target choice, record file, fingerprint text, timers), `pdf-copy-store.ts` (the record in `<notebooks folder>/_meta/pdfcopy-<device>.json`, one file per device, lenient read, never overwritten when unreadable) and `pdf-copy-service.ts` (vault work: events, build, write). The view hands over a `PdfCopySource` (pages as an export draws them, a stamp that changes on every edit, an unsaved flag) on save, on close and when the app is hidden; the service builds the PDF with `buildPdfBytes` (`view/export.ts`) on its own `NotebookMedia`, page by page through `pagesToPdfYielding` (`ink/export-pdf.ts`, byte-identical to `pagesToPdf`), so it works after the view is gone.

Rules: a file is replaced only when path, size and SHA-256 equal a record of this notebook (`isOurs`); anything else is the user's and the copy goes to `<name> (copy N).pdf`. The PDF is checked to be complete before it is written and read back after. Settings: `pdfCopy`, `pdfCopyWhen`, `pdfCopyFolder` (tab) and `pdfCopyNotebooks` (per-notebook overrides by path, edited from the notebook menu, moved on rename). The notebook format is unchanged.

### Cloud sync (0.22, #126)

Optional, off by default, per device, no telemetry. Split in two: the engine, settings and status (`src/sync/`, this part) and the providers with their sign-in (`src/sync/providers/`, `src/sync/auth/`), which meet at `src/sync/provider-types.ts` (the `CloudProvider` contract and its errors) and `src/sync/auth-ui.ts` (`SyncAuthUI`: available, signed in, `signIn(provider, {clientId, clientSecret?})`, `signOut` → `{revoked, manageUrl}`, account and root labels, `connect` → a provider bound to the root). Until they are wired in (`CloudSyncService.setAuth`), `NO_SYNC_AUTH` makes every provider "not available" and the settings show no sign-in button.

- **Pure, Node-tested** (no `obsidian`): `rules.ts` (scope: whole vault, folders, include/exclude globs and types; always excluded: dot folders including the config dir and `.trash`, `*.tmp`, the #127 backup folder), `state.ts` (per-path remote index `r` = id, rev, size, mtime and base `b` = local mtime, size, SHA-256, remote rev; cursor; `trusted`; versioned, compact, unknown fields kept), `planner.ts` (one decision per path from local, remote and base; the mass-deletion guard; deletion policies guard / allow / restore / defer; the large-file rule; the hard size cap), `conflict-name.ts`, `engine.ts` (one run: changes since the cursor, or a full listing; plan; apply in the order conflicts, downloads, uploads, remote deletes, local deletes; up to three rounds when an upload meets a newer cloud revision; retries; state saved at most every 10 s (`SAVE_EVERY_MS`) and at the end), `settings.ts`, `network.ts`, `sync-memory.ts`.
- **Safety.** Without a trusted base nothing is deleted (first sync, damaged or foreign state, scope change: a union); a deletion needs the other side unchanged since the base and goes to a trash (Obsidian's `.trash` through `vault.trash(file, false)`, never `fileManager.trashFile`, which may delete permanently; the provider's trash); a full listing is complete by contract (the providers' index lives in memory, so the first `changes()` after a restart is one), and an empty or much smaller one trips the guard; a missing root (`RootMissingError`, a `TransientRemoteError`) is an error, never a mass deletion. Uploads carry `ifRev` (null: must not exist); a `RemoteConflictError` re-plans. Both sides changed: the cloud's version is written as `Stem (conflict YYYY-MM-DD HH.MM Device).ext` (the pattern of `storage/conflicts.ts`, so the #122 merge offer picks it up) and uploaded, then the local version is uploaded over the cloud's.
- **Obsidian wiring.** `vault-adapter.ts`: `LocalPort` over the Vault API (binary read, create/modify, trash; every write checks that the file still has the size and mtime the plan saw, else `LocalChangedError` and the path waits for the next run) and the state file `<configDir>/plugins/<id>/cloud-sync/state-<device>.json` through `vault.adapter` (unreadable: copied to `.bad.json`, then a full rescan). `sync-service.ts`: triggers (20 s after layout and never before the vault is indexed, every N minutes, when the app is hidden or quits, manual), Wi-Fi only (`navigator.connection`; unknown → runs and says so on desktop, holds automatic runs on mobile), pen idle wait, flush of the open notebooks, one run at a time, notices once per distinct problem, the confirmation dialogs, status and log (local storage, 50 entries). Downloads into an open notebook use `modifyBinary`, so the view reloads through its usual external-change path; a notebook with unsaved ink is saved first (`settle`) and never replaced while it still has some.
- **Client.** The user's own OAuth client: the client ID is a setting (the same on every device; Google's `drive.file` scope only sees files made by the same client); Google's client secret is kept in the device's local storage only.
- **Joining the engine to the providers (integration).** `sync/auth-adapter.ts` implements `SyncAuthUI` (`auth-ui.ts`) over `auth/sign-in.ts` (`openSignIn`, `disconnect`), `auth/token-store.ts` and `providers/createProvider`, with the dialog, HTTP and strings injected (tested with fakes); `auth/obsidian-auth.ts` wires the real ones and `main.ts` plugs it in with `cloudSync.setAuth`. Storage, all in `app.loadLocalStorage` (per vault and device; never the vault, never `data.json`): the sign-in blob under `notebook-sync-auth` (refresh and access tokens, cloud folder; one account per device; **never the client secret**), Google's client secret under `notebook-cloud-sync-secret-gdrive` (the one key; the settings field edits it and the adapter merges it in when a provider is built), the account label under `notebook-cloud-sync-account`. A token refresh that ends after *Disconnect* is dropped. The client ID is the only OAuth value in the synced settings. Google's `rootLabel` is shown as the folder the app created (“My Drive/Notebook sync (created by Notebook)”) and the Account row adds that every device must use the same client ID. The sync state file lives in the plugin folder under `.obsidian`, which the sync never uploads.
- **Review W22 safety rules** (sources in `docs/research.md`, *Review W22 fixes*):
  - *Ready gate (S1).* `CloudSyncService.ready()` = layout ready AND the metadata cache's first `resolved` (or `READY_FALLBACK_MS`, 30 s, after the layout). Every trigger, *Sync now* included, is refused before; the start-up run waits for it.
  - *Trigger policy (S7).* `triggerPolicy`: start-up and leaving runs use the `defer` deletion policy (deletions are counted in `SyncResult.deferredDeletes`, never run) and the automatic guard limit `MASS_DELETE_AUTO_MAX` (10); periodic runs guard with 10; manual runs with 20.
  - *Disconnect (S5).* The controller is made before any wait; *Disconnect* aborts, awaits the run (its final state save included), then signs out and removes the state file; no run starts meanwhile. Signing in to the other service runs the same disconnect first; the dialog (`CloudDisconnectModal`) can also forget Google's secret.
  - *Twins (S4).* Providers list every file of a path (`TreeIndex` keeps all ids; a deletion of a path another id still holds is dropped). `applyChanges` follows the smallest id; `resolveTwins` trashes an identical twin, else writes it as a conflicted copy (and uploads it) first.
  - *Displaced versions (S3).* `upload` returns `UploadedEntry.displaced` (Drive: revisions between the checked one and ours, from `revisions.list`); the engine keeps each as a conflicted copy. A Drive trash that raced a write is untrashed and reported as a conflict.
  - *Same content as the base (S6).* In `keepBoth`, a cloud version whose hash equals the base's is not a conflict: the local version goes up on that revision.
  - *Names (S9, S10).* `names.ts`: one rule for both sides (`segmentIssue` of `storage/sync-state.ts`); a skipped name is `skip name` and `SyncResult.unsyncable`, never a deletion (a cloud rename to such a name forgets the old path's base, so the local file goes up again). Paths are grouped by `foldKey` (NFC, lower case): a group is never written twice; a two-path group that is a case-only rename on one side (trusted state, guard or allow) is followed.
  - *Scope of copies (S8).* A conflicted copy outside the scope is only written locally (never uploaded nor recorded).
  - *Mobile (S16).* Unknown network type on mobile holds automatic runs; `hardMaxBytes` (`cloudSyncMobileMaxMb`, default 50, 5–500) puts larger files in `Plan.tooLarge` whatever was approved.

## Cloud connector: providers and sign-in (0.22, #126)

Optional, off by default, no format change. The engine and its settings section (`src/sync/`, other files) talk to a remote only through the `CloudProvider` contract in `src/sync/provider-types.ts`. This part implements it for Google Drive and OneDrive and adds the sign-in. Sources, endpoints and scope limits: `docs/research.md`, *Cloud connector*. User guide: `docs/cloud-sync.md`.

```
src/sync/auth/        (pure unless marked)
  http.ts             Http interface (request → status, headers, text, bytes) and JSON/form helpers
  http-obsidian.ts    Http on Obsidian's requestUrl (throw: false)                         [obsidian]
  retry.ts            Clock, AbortError, sendWithRetry: 429/5xx/Drive 403 rate limits/no answer → backoff or Retry-After, then TransientRemoteError
  oauth-config.ts     endpoints and scopes per provider; ProviderAuth = StoredAuth + Google's clientSecret
  device-flow.ts      RFC 8628: requestDeviceCode, pollForToken (interval, slow_down +5 s, expiry, cancel), SignInError(reason)
  tokens.ts           TokenManager (refresh 60 s before expiry, one at a time, onChange), revokeAuth
  token-store.ts      loadAuth / saveAuth / clearAuth in app local storage (key notebook-sync-auth), lenient
  connect.ts          connectAccount (device flow → sync folder → account), disconnectAccount
  sign-in.ts          signIn, openSignIn (the dialog), disconnect                           [obsidian]
  sign-in-modal.ts    SignInModal on modal-shell                                             [obsidian]
src/sync/providers/   (pure)
  index.ts            createProvider(auth, http, opts) → ConnectedProvider (CloudProvider + prepareRoot + auth)
  api.ts              ApiClient: bearer token per attempt, 401 → refresh once → AuthRequiredError; RemoteHttpError
  tree.ts             TreeIndex: id → path index, buildFull, applyDelta, RootMissingError
  gdrive.ts           GoogleDriveProvider, prepareDriveRoot
  onedrive.ts         OneDriveProvider, prepareOneDriveRoot
```

**Sign-in.** The settings section calls `openSignIn(app, { provider, clientId, clientSecret? })`. The dialog runs the device flow: it shows the user code and the verification address (Copy code, Open sign-in page, a countdown), polls every `interval` seconds (+5 s after each `slow_down`), and ends on tokens, `access_denied`/`authorization_declined`, `expired_token` (or the code's lifetime), a refused client, or Cancel (the abort signal stops the wait at once). Then it finds or creates the sync root folder (Drive: `Notebook sync` at the top of My Drive; OneDrive: the app folder, `GET /me/drive/special/approot`, `Apps/<app name>`, made by OneDrive on first use) and reads the account label. It resolves `{ auth, account }` or `null`; the caller stores `auth` with `saveAuth(app, auth)`. `disconnect(app, auth)` revokes (Google) and always clears the local state; Microsoft has no per-token revocation for public clients, so the result carries the consent page to show.

**Tokens.** Device-local only: Obsidian's local storage, per vault and per device, never in the vault or `data.json`, never logged (no request or response is ever logged; error messages carry only status and provider error codes). `TokenManager` refreshes 60 s before expiry and after a 401; `invalid_grant` (and unknown/deleted client, consent needed) → `AuthRequiredError`. Every refresh calls `onAuthChange` with the new state: the owner must store it (Microsoft rotates refresh tokens). Google's client secret is part of the stored state (`ProviderAuth.clientSecret`, an additive field next to the contract's `StoredAuth`).

**Paths from ids (tree.ts).** Neither delta feed gives paths. Each provider maps its items to `TreeItem` (id, folder, removed, name, parent, entry) and `TreeIndex` keeps folder id → (name, parent) and file id → path for the root's subtree, in memory.
- A full listing rebuilds the index (Drive: breadth-first `files.list` of the root, start token taken first; OneDrive: `delta` on the root folder without a token). Two files at one path (Drive allows it): both are returned (review W22 S4; the engine decides). A `/` inside a listed name becomes `\`, which the engine skips as an unsyncable name.
- A delta: the last occurrence of each id counts; a file whose parent chain reaches the root is an upsert (and a deletion of its old path when it moved); a deleted or trashed file, or one whose chain no longer reaches the root (moved out), is a deletion of its old path. An unknown parent is looked up once (`files.get` / `items/{id}`) and cached for the call.
- **Full listing instead of a delta** when the index cannot answer safely: the first non-empty delta after a restart (the index is not persisted, so an unknown id could be a move or a delete of a file it never saw), any change to a folder already inside the root (rename, move, delete: its descendants change path without being listed), an expired cursor (Drive 400/404/410 on `changes.list`, OneDrive `410 Gone`), or a foreign cursor. A full listing is always correct; it only costs requests.
- The sync root itself deleted or trashed → `RootMissingError` (a `TransientRemoteError`): nothing is listed and nothing is reported deleted, so the engine never deletes local files because the remote folder vanished.

**Writes.**
- Drive: `upload` creates missing folders (searched by name under their parent, created if absent; cached by path until the next full listing), looks up the file by name, checks `ifRev` (`null`: no file may exist; a string: `headRevisionId` must match), then sends multipart (≤ 4 MiB) or a resumable session (8 MiB chunks, multiples of 256 KiB, following `308` `Range`; the session address must be on `https://www.googleapis.com`, since it gets the bearer token). With two files of that name, the one whose revision is `ifRev` is written. A new file or folder gets its id from `files.generateIds` first, so a retried create answers `409` (then the file is read) instead of making a twin. **Race:** Drive v3 has no precondition header on writes, so after a replace the provider lists the file's revisions and returns those written between the checked revision and its own (`displaced`, with their bytes) for the engine to keep. `remove` reads the metadata, checks `ifRev`, then sets `trashed: true`; if the trash answer shows another revision, it untrashes and reports a conflict.
- OneDrive: folders via `items/{parent}:/{name}` (404 → `POST children` with `conflictBehavior: fail`, 409 → read again). Small files: `PUT …:/content` with `conflictBehavior=fail` (create-only) or `replace` + `If-Match: <eTag>` (the revision is the content hash, `quickXor:<hash>` or `sha1:<hash>`, review W22 S6: `ifRev` is compared with a metadata read and the eTag read with it goes in `If-Match`); above 4 MiB an upload session in 5 MiB fragments (multiples of 320 KiB) sent to the upload URL **without** the bearer token; 409/412 → `RemoteConflictError`; a session left by a failure is cancelled (`DELETE` on the upload URL). `remove` reads the item (404 → already gone), compares the revision, then `DELETE` with the eTag in `If-Match` (to the recycle bin; 412 → conflict). Error texts pass through `withoutUrls` (`auth/retry.ts`), so no address (and no token in it) reaches a notice or the log.
- Neither provider ever deletes permanently. `download` of a vanished file is `RemoteConflictError` (the engine re-lists). OneDrive downloads read `@microsoft.graph.downloadUrl` (pre-authenticated, no bearer token) instead of following the `/content` redirect.
- Cursor and link safety: OneDrive's cursor is the `deltaLink`; the provider follows a cursor or `nextLink` with the user's token only when it starts with `https://graph.microsoft.com/v1.0/`.

**Errors.** Transient (no answer, 429, 5xx except 501, Drive's 403 rate limits) are retried 4 times with exponential backoff and jitter or `Retry-After` (seconds or a date, capped at 60 s), then `TransientRemoteError` (with `retryAfterMs`). 401 → one refresh and retry → `AuthRequiredError`. Conflicts → `RemoteConflictError`. Anything a provider does not expect → `RemoteHttpError(status, code)` (a bug or an unsupported remote set-up). Abort → an `Error` named `AbortError`.

**OneDrive scope and Business.** The scope is `Files.ReadWrite.AppFolder` (review W22 S14): the root is the app folder and nothing else of the drive is reachable. Older OneDrive for Business libraries offer delta only on the drive root, which that scope cannot read: when the folder delta is refused (400/403/501) the provider walks the folder's `children` (breadth first, `$top=1000`) at every run for the rest of the session (cursor `od1:walk`, always a full listing; after a restart the delta is tried once more), never the whole drive.

## Redaction before export (0.26, #285)

The boxes are an export-time choice, not document data: no format change, nothing in the notebook, nothing synced. `model/redact.ts` holds them (page-id keyed, bounded, lenient reader); `ui/redact-store.ts` keeps them per notebook in the device's local storage. `ink/redact.ts` turns a page and its boxes into a COPY of the page in which everything under a box is gone and the boxes are black, filled shape strokes appended last. Because every writer draws the page it is given, the copy is the whole mechanism for vector output: PDF, SVG, the interactive HTML (its links come from the surviving text boxes), the page rasteriser behind PNG, print, Markdown, share and copy as image. The one thing a copy cannot carry is the pixels of a PDF page or picture behind the page, so the copy also carries its boxes in a symbol-keyed field (it survives `{ ...page }`, is never serialised) and the two code paths that load a background (`collectPageImages` for PDF/SVG/HTML, `PageImageRenderer.background` for the rasters) read it and burn the boxes into a copy of the picture before encoding it (`burnPixelRects`, `view/burn.ts`); a failure to burn drops the background. `view/export-redact.ts` (`withRedaction`) is applied by each writer on its own pages, so a path that forgets the dialog cannot export an uncovered page when the notebook has boxes switched on; applying it twice is a no-op.

Rules for whole items: an element is dropped when the area any writer may draw for it (an equation includes its number, a note its shadow) touches a box grown by 3 pt. Text is measured pessimistically (`pessimisticMeasure`, by character with `charEm`: ASCII 0.8 em, capitals and `m w @ % &` 1 em, each checked Unicode range its own upper bound (Latin and Greek 1 em, Cyrillic, Hebrew and Thai 1.25, Arabic letters, CJK, Hangul, emoji, punctuation, arrows and math 1.5, Indic scripts 2, ⸺ 2, ⸻ 3), and every character outside those ranges, U+FDFD among them, 6 em, the widest bound; the largest of that, the estimate and the wide layout, at least one line height, and as wide as its widest character), so no font can draw a line past the measured area. The page header or footer is measured the same way, not capped at its maximum width (the SVG text is not squeezed into it). A checkbox whose label a box touches keeps its box and loses the label. An excerpt is also dropped when its SOURCE region (on another page of the notebook) touches a box there (`coveredExcerpt`), even on a page with no box: its snapshot is the covered pixels. The same holds at any depth: `withExcerptChains` adds, to a symbol-keyed field of the set, the rectangle of every covered excerpt, repeating until nothing new is covered (each excerpt once, so cycles end); those rectangles decide only `coveredExcerpt`, never cut ink or draw a box, and never reach storage. `redactPages` computes the chains over the pages it is given; the view and `runExport` compute them over the whole notebook so a writer given one page knows them. A single element copied on its own (the figures' *Copy*) goes only when `elementKept` says the export of its page keeps it. Ink is cut with Liang-Barsky against each box grown by the reach of its stroke (`redactReach`: the widest the nib can draw at the stroke's highest pressure, pen 0.8 w, fountain and brush by their own pressure curves); a filled shape that a box reaches is dropped whole; ink that a collapsed note hides is never drawn by an export and is removed from the copy; ink bound to a dropped graph goes with it. The copy is made with `pageCopy` (model/states.ts), which walks the prototype chain, so a drawing-state view or a clean copy keeps the page id and the boxes still find it.

Fail closed (review fixes): `ui/redact-store.ts` keeps what this session set in memory per app (it wins over storage, so boxes drawn while the device cannot store them still apply), reads every save back, reports a save that was not stored, never forgets a notebook whose switch is on (only switched-off notebooks make room, and a full store says so), stores an unusual notebook id under a hash, and reads a damaged value as `unreadable`, never as "no boxes". A value it cannot read at all (not an object, a key or an entry of another shape such as a future version's, or missing while the sentinel key `notebook-redactions-present` says one was written) is copied once, bounded to 256,000 characters, to `notebook-redactions-unreadable`; while that copy exists every notebook without a fresh entry reads as `unreadable`, a read never writes the main value, and a save (only ever from the reader's own action in the dialogs) replaces it only after the copy is confirmed, keeping a notebook saved with no boxes as an explicit `cleared` entry. One notice per session says so (`takeRedactStoreNotice`). `resetRedactStorage` (the dialog's *Reset redaction storage*, after a confirmation) removes the three keys. The Redact dialog commits on *Done* (exactly the boxes shown) and on every change, never on Escape. The view turns `unreadable` into `ExportOptions.redactBlocked`; `withRedaction`, `planWithRedaction` and the public API refuse (`RedactionUnavailableError`, a notice, `export-failed`) and the PNG, print and Markdown writers return before drawing; the export dialog's own boxes lift the block. With boxes switched off, an export outside the dialog shows one quiet hint (`redactOff`). The digests that quote PDF text exclude every word a box touches (`textUnderBands` exclusions), and the book index, the formula sheet, the colour-meaning digest, the figures' *Copy image* and the API's `exportPdf` all read the redacted pages.

Deliberately not covered (also listed in the export dialog under the switch): the PDF annotations digest (it quotes the PDF's own text), *Extract pages* / merge / split, the automatic PDF copy, the notebook file and its backups, the companion note, Zettel inbox cards, page embeds in notes (the live page), PDF text the reader copies by hand, excerpts of OTHER notebooks (their boxes are not read), and the file title (the notebook's name).
